# Decisions log

Append-only. One entry per decision. Status: **Approved** (PM said so), **Working default** (our reading; PM has not objected; change freely), **Open** (needs the client or PM).

The original documents in `Documentation/` (Product Design Document, `sample-creation-information.md`, `CLAUDE.md`) still describe a School + College sample. Where this log disagrees with them, **this log wins** until those documents are rewritten.

---

## 2026-09-20

**D-001 Institution structure.** Approved (PM: "that is the final").
Royal Softech College has only Grade 11 and 12 (+2) and three bachelor's degrees. There is no Nursery to Grade 10 school. Grade 11 and Grade 12 are one year each. Each bachelor's degree is four years.
Supersedes: School (Nursery to 10) + College sections, and the placeholder faculty names, in the original documents.

**D-002 This is the real product, not a throwaway sample.** Approved.
"We will be building the entire website on this." Supersedes `sample-creation-information.md` section 5.2 and `CLAUDE.md` section 2 (sample mode: no backend, fake in-memory data, throwaway code, 2 to 3 day time-box).
A demo is a deployment of the real product loaded with invented data. It shows a "Sample, fake data" banner. It is never a separate code path.

**D-003 All data originates from role screens.** Approved.
"The data should be added by the Co-ordinator as per the design." Nothing is hard-coded into screens. A demo or test data set is loaded by a script that calls the same service functions the screens call, so every rule, permission and audit entry applies to it too. No direct SQL inserts.

**D-004 Co-ordinator and Accountant scope is institution-wide for now.** Approved, with a design constraint.
PM: keep it the same now, design so it can differ later.
Constraint: permissions stay expressed as role + action + scope, and a role assignment carries a scope (whole institution, or one section). Today every Co-ordinator and Accountant is assigned whole-institution scope. Splitting later is a data change, not a code change. Permission tests run under both configurations from the first day. Ranking (Top 20) and receipt numbering stay keyed per section so a later split renumbers nothing.

**D-005 Stack.** Approved (PM: "Stack is B").
Django (modular monolith, PostgreSQL, JSON API with OpenAPI schema) + Next.js (TypeScript) front end with a generated typed client. Two managed deployables behind one domain.
Supersedes `CLAUDE.md` section 4 ("no separate front-end app and no separate API layer in V1"). Everything else in `CLAUDE.md` section 4 stands: no Redis, no microservices, Postgres-backed job queue, private object storage with signed links, one deployment per school.

**D-006 Levels and terms.** Working default. Open with the client. **Superseded by D-109 (academic terms of any length).**
A programme has an ordered list of levels. +2 programmes: Grade 11, Grade 12. Bachelor's: Year 1 to Year 4. Each level is one academic year, assessed by terminals (yearly, with terminals).
Not modelled: semesters. PM said "4 year" and "1 year each"; whether any bachelor's runs semester-wise inside those four years has not been confirmed. The level and term structure is data, so semesters can be added without a rewrite.

**D-007 Brand assets.** Approved.
The client will supply logo, colours and photos. Until then: a neutral wordmark and a restrained accent. No assets are taken from the client's Facebook page without PM approval.

---

**D-008 This is a school-website product, configured for one school first.** Approved direction (PM).
PM: "a school website product which is modified for one school", like SAP: out of the box, then adjusted through defined enhancement spots. Royal Softech is customer one and the sample for other schools. A new school should take little effort.
Working design (details awaiting PM): variation is handled in order of cost. Configure, then apply a template, then theme, then a code extension at a named extension point, and only last change the core (for everyone). The core never names a school. Portal layout stays identical for every school (original docs), with slots for extra cards, tabs and menu items. Extends, does not replace, the original rule "add a setting only when a second school asks": we cut the seam now where a variation is already visible, and build a settings screen only when a second school needs it.

**D-009 Permanent second-school test.** Working default, awaiting PM.
A fictional "Sample Basic School" (Nursery to Grade 10, percentage grading, monthly fees, different theme) runs every flow in CI beside Royal Softech. This is the original documents' School structure, kept as a template and test fixture. It is also the demo to show other schools, so Royal's data is never shown to prospects.

**D-010 Tenancy and extensions.** Working default, awaiting PM.
Keep one deployment per school (original docs). Do not build multi-tenancy; revisit at about 30 schools. Extensions are written and reviewed by us only, no customer-uploaded code.

---

**D-011 Forget the sample; build the product in phases.** Approved direction (PM).
PM: the client demo is the PM's concern. The build is the reusable product, foundation first, then easy and visible modules for momentum, then difficult and paid parts last, finished as fast as is sound. The Thursday demo-link deadline is dropped. Phase order and estimates are in `build-plan.md`, proposed and awaiting approval. Where a hard piece is a dependency of an early one (permissions, audit, approvals, dates), it is built early in its smallest generic form.

---

**D-012 Build plan, installs and repo approved.** Approved (PM: "all approve").
Phase order in `build-plan.md`, installs of Python (via `uv`) and PostgreSQL 17, and a private GitHub repo. Phase 0 started 2026-09-20.

**D-013 Toolchain and API conventions.** Approved (PM: "approve", 2026-09-20).
Python 3.12 (via `uv`), Django 6.1, Django REST Framework, PostgreSQL 17, Next.js 16, strict TypeScript. Plain CSS with design tokens (no Tailwind), so colours and fonts can only come from tokens. Typed API client generated from the OpenAPI contract (`openapi-typescript`, `openapi-fetch`); CI fails if the contract or generated types are stale. Every API route ends in a slash and `APPEND_SLASH` is off, so a missing slash is a 404 and never a redirect (browsers cache redirects permanently). Requests run in a transaction (`ATOMIC_REQUESTS`); the health check opts out so it can report a database failure as 503. Every view must declare its permissions or it is denied, and a test enforces it. Demo mode is refused when `ENV=production`.

**D-014 BS dates: verify by year, not by library.** Approved (PM: "approve", 2026-09-20). Details in `spikes/bs-dates.md`.
The two candidate libraries agree on every BS year up to 2083 and disagree on every year from 2084, because no official calendar exists that far ahead. Use `nepali-datetime` inside a date module that converts only for verified BS years (currently 2000 to 2083) and refuses the rest. Add each year to the verified list when its official calendar is published and checked. Dates of birth store AD and BS as entered.

**D-015 Job queue: Procrastinate.** Working default. Details in `spikes/job-queue.md`.
Django 6.1 has no production task worker. Procrastinate 3.9 passed retry and duplicate-guard tests on PostgreSQL 17. Wrap it in `core.jobs`. Outbox events are written in the same transaction as the business write, and a job drains them.

**D-016 Login session and CSRF rules.** Working default (proven in a spike). Details in `spikes/session-csrf.md`.
Set `CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` to the web origin in every environment. Never enable `USE_X_FORWARDED_HOST`. The web client re-reads the `csrftoken` cookie on every request, because login rotates it. Server-side calls must forward the session cookie explicitly.

**D-017 PDF documents: HTML rendered by Chromium.** Working default, partly verified. Details in `spikes/pdf-devanagari.md`.
Devanagari renders correctly on screen; the PDF text layer is unreliable for copy and search, so the database is always the record. Embed a self-hosted Noto Sans Devanagari. Confirm in a real PDF viewer in Phase 6. WeasyPrint is the fallback.

**D-018 Free-first hosting; no native app for now.** Working default, awaiting PM go on the test.
PM: whole-project budget 50K a year (assumed NPR, about US$350), no spend on servers, run free as a starter, talk subscriptions later. Direction: start free. Run a one-day test of an all-Cloudflare build; if it passes, propose replacing the Django backend (this would supersede D-005's backend); if it fails, keep Django on free tiers (Cloudflare Pages and R2, Northflank Sandbox, Neon Singapore). Front end is static export either way. Nightly database copy to R2, because free tiers give little backup. Hosting cost moves into the yearly subscription later. No native Android app now (iOS is about a quarter of Nepal mobile use); the site is installable. Supabase not used. Research, sources and the test plan: `spikes/hosting-options.md`.

**D-019 Backend on Cloudflare (Workers + D1) instead of Django.** Approved (PM: "approve 1", 2026-09-20). Details in `spikes/cloudflare-test.md`. Supersedes the Django parts of D-005 and D-013, and D-015 (Procrastinate), D-016 (CSRF proxy rules) and D-017 (Chromium PDFs), which are replaced by D-021.
The one-day test passed on a free account: simultaneous approvals had one winner every time, receipt numbers stayed gapless through failures, Nepali PDFs render correctly and generate quickly, restore works with a 30-day window. Recommend replacing the Django backend with TypeScript Workers and D1, which would supersede the backend part of D-005 (the web front end stays Next.js, exported static). Retire the Django skeleton in `apps/api` when approved; the data model and permission matrix carry over unchanged. Mitigations: (1) hash-chain the ledger and audit rows and export them daily, because D1 cannot stop our own code from removing a guard; (2) scrypt N=2^15 for passwords, since PBKDF2 is capped at 100,000 rounds; (3) one database round trip per request (about 100 ms each); (4) R2 file storage needs enabling and possibly a payment method: PM decision needed.

**D-020 File storage (R2) deferred.** Approved (PM: "hold for 2 until it's needed").
R2 needs enabling in the Cloudflare dashboard and probably a payment method. PM said no cards for now. Build the storage interface; build no uploads until the PM says R2 is needed. Phase 4 (certificates, vouchers) and Phase 5 (notes, homework) depend on this, so ask before starting Phase 4.

**D-021 Architecture on Cloudflare.** Working default. Details to go in `architecture.md`.
- One Worker per school serves the static web app (Workers Static Assets) and the API under `/api/`, on one origin: no CORS, no cross-site cookies, no separate domain needed while testing.
- API in Hono with `@hono/zod-openapi`; Zod for all input and output; the OpenAPI contract is committed and the web client is generated from it. Web is Next.js static export.
- D1 with hand-written SQL migrations, **no ORM**. One database round trip per request; atomic changes in one `batch()`; ledger and audit protected by triggers and a hash chain with a daily export.
- Sessions: `__Host-` cookies, a 10-minute signed access token, a rotating refresh session in the database. Money, approval and publish actions re-check assignments inside their own batch. Non-GET requests must be same-origin.
- Passwords with scrypt (N=2^15). Jobs on Cloudflare Queues and Cron Triggers. PDFs on Browser Rendering, generated in batches.
- Tests with Vitest on the Workers runtime, plus property-based tests for ledger invariants.

**D-022 BS date library and the disputed stretch.** Working default. Details in `spikes/bs-dates-js.md`.
`@inicrea/bikram-sambat-core`, pinned to exactly 0.1.3, wrapped by `core/dates`. Chosen because it matched all 30,681 reference days; seven more popular npm libraries carry a different calendar for BS 2062 Baisakh and Jestha, so popularity was not used. Golden test guards against upgrades. BS 2062 Baisakh 31 to Jestha 31 is flagged as disputed (two of three public calendars agree with us). Risk: the package is a month old with one maintainer, so the wrapper keeps it replaceable.

**D-023 Base schema and keyed audit chain.** Working default. Migration `apps/api/migrations/0001_foundation.sql`.
Tables are plural (`users`, `role_assignments`, `audit_events`). The database refuses impossible role and scope pairs (an Admin limited to a section, a Student with institution scope), duplicate assignments, and case-different duplicate emails. The audit log is an **HMAC chain** keyed by the `AUDIT_HMAC_KEY` Worker secret, which is never stored in the database, so someone with database access alone cannot rewrite history and still verify. Triggers block edits and deletes and refuse an entry that does not link to the current head; a losing writer's whole batch, business change included, rolls back and retries. Detects an edited entry, a middle insert or delete, and a tampered head. **Known limit:** deleting the newest entries together with the head is invisible inside the database and is caught only by the daily export of `auditChainSummary` (to build in Phase 10). Tested on the simulated database and on a real, temporary D1 database.

**D-024 Sign-in and sessions.** Working default. Amends D-021 (access token 10 to 30 minutes). Migration `0002_sessions.sql`.
- Passwords: scrypt (N=2^15) with `@noble/hashes`, NFKC-normalised, stored as `scrypt$N$r$p$salt$hash`; a hash with weaker settings is upgraded at the next sign-in; stored cost parameters are capped so a tampered row cannot burn CPU. Policy: 10 to 128 characters (counted as characters, so Nepali passphrases are not penalised), not a common password, not containing the email name. **OPEN:** a fuller breached-password check.
- Access token 30 minutes, because every renewal is a D1 write (free plan: 100,000 a day). Roles are in the token; money, approval and publish actions re-check assignments in their own batch.
- Refresh token: random, stored only as a SHA-256 hash, rotated on every use with a conditional update (two simultaneous refreshes: one wins). Reuse of the previous token within 20 seconds is treated as two tabs racing (retry); later, as theft, and the session is revoked. Sessions end after 30 days, or 7 days unused. A deactivated user's session ends at the next refresh.
- Lockout: 5 failures per email and 30 per address in 15 minutes. Unknown emails are locked identically, and wrong-password and unknown-email answers are identical, so nothing reveals which emails exist. Throttled attempts write nothing.
- Sign-in attempts are stored in an append-only `sign_in_events` table (the Admin Sign-ins view, later). `POST /api/auth/sign-in`, `/refresh` and `/sign-out` are public; `GET /api/auth/me` is "any signed-in user". Both lists are reviewed allowlists.
- **Known limit, tested and documented:** after sign-out the access cookie works until it expires (at most 30 minutes).

**D-025 Permission layer.** Working default. Code in `apps/api/src/core/permissions`.
The matrix lives in code (61 actions, 8 groups, from the reviewed `permission-matrix.md` draft), and the document's tables are generated from it, with a CI check so they cannot drift. A route declares an action; no sign-in is 401, no granting role is 403, and the handler never runs. The handler gets a **grant** (institution, sections, own, assigned, class, limits, read-only) and builds its queries from it. Section-scoped Co-ordinators and Accountants narrow to their section by data, with no code change (D-004). Unknown actions, unknown roles and roles not listed are denied. Combined roles union, widest wins. Tested three ways: one generated test per row, hand-written independent rules for the sensitive actions (these caught corrupted cells that generated tests could not), and every role against every action over HTTP. Mutation-tested (9 breakages caught). **Still to do:** money, approval and publish actions re-check assignments inside their own batch (with each module in Phases 3, 6 and 7), and data-level tests per module.

**D-026 School configuration, themes and packs.** Working default. Migration `0003_configuration.sql`, code in `apps/api/src/core/{config,theme}` and `modules/config`, packs in `packs/<school>/pack.json`.
- A **pack** is one school as data: name, sections, optional-module switches, renamed words, theme. `parsePack` checks it and lists every problem at once. Unknown fields, unknown modules, unknown terms, and a theme that fails the readability check are refused.
- Ten modules are **mandatory** (accounts, admissions, academics, approvals, audit, content, fees, files, notifications, results) because they carry rules that must always hold; a pack cannot switch them off. Five are optional (attendance, teacher attendance, homework, notes, top 20) and default on. Renaming is limited to a catalogue of known words (roles and a few terms); nothing else can be renamed.
- Applying a pack **only adds and updates, never deletes**, and is safe to repeat (tested: the second and third run change nothing). A section removed from a pack stays in the database. It runs as one batch. `applyPack` is used by tests; `npm run provision -- --pack <dir> --local|--remote [--config …]` renders the same operations to SQL (values escaped, line breaks and NUL refused) and runs migrations and then the SQL through wrangler. Provisioning is not a user action and is **not in the audit chain**; the pack in git is its record. Provisioning outside the audit log is deliberate and stated here so nobody is surprised.
- Configuration is **public read**: `GET /api/config/public` (name, sections, modules, words, theme; no secrets; cached 60 s; 503 until a pack is applied), one database round trip. Changing the theme is `PUT /api/config/theme`, action `branding.manage` (Super Admin only), written with an audit entry that names the actor, in one batch with the change. The old theme is kept; the database allows only one active theme.
- **Theme readability is enforced on the server**, not only in a screen: WCAG 2.2 contrast, 4.5 for text pairs, 3 for the primary colour against the page, checked for light and dark. An unreadable theme is refused with 422 and the list of failing pairs, and nothing is saved. Fonts come from a fixed list (system, Inter, Noto Sans); no URLs.
- `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs` runs in CI: core never imports modules; a module never reaches into another module's internals; no source imports `packs/`; no source file names a specific school. It already caught a school name in the password denylist, now removed.

**D-027 Access is checked before the request body.** Working default. `defineRoute` runs the permission check as route middleware, ahead of validation. Found on staging: an anonymous call with a bad body got 400 (and so learned what the route expects) instead of 401. Now anonymous gets 401 and a role without the action gets 403 whatever they send. Covered by a test that fails without the fix.

**D-028 Design system and app shell.** Working default. Code in `apps/web/src/{ui,shell,theme,config,session,i18n}`.
- **Tokens.** Colours, font and corner radii come only from the school's theme, written into one `<style id="school-theme">` by `theme/css.ts`, which accepts nothing but hex colours, a font from the fixed list and small whole-number radii (so a theme can never inject CSS). Spacing, type scale, elevation and motion are the same for every school (`tokens.css`, no colours in it; shadows are mixed from theme colours). Input borders use the muted text colour, because the server's contrast rules guarantee it is readable while the decorative border colour is not.
- **No flash.** The built-in neutral theme is in every page at build time. A returning visitor gets the school's cached theme applied by a tiny inline script before first paint (Next's documented pattern); the very first visit shows the neutral theme for a moment. **OPEN:** to remove that first-visit flash, the Worker could inject the theme into the HTML as it serves it.
- **Configuration at run time.** `ConfigProvider` reads `/api/config/public` (with a localStorage copy for instant paint and offline use). The page title is the school's name, rendered by React as the only `<title>` (Next's metadata title is left out, because Next rewrites it on every client-side navigation).
- **Shell.** One portal layout for every school (header, menu, page; menu is a scrolling row on a phone and a column from 56 rem). The menu is a registry (`shell/nav.ts`) filtered by role and module switch, which is where modules add their entries later (D-008 slot). Real permissions stay on the API (D-025). Roles show the school's own word (`term`), and Super Admin shows as "Support".
- **Session in the browser.** `createRefreshingFetch`: on 401 it renews the session once and repeats the request; any number of simultaneous failures share one refresh (a refresh token works once); `refresh_in_progress` is retried once; sign-in, refresh and sign-out are never refreshed; going offline is not "signed out". Signed-out visitors and expired sessions on the portal are sent to `/sign-in`.
- **Rules checked by tests (fail the build).** No hex, colour function or colour name outside the theme files; no `font-family` outside the theme; transitions and keyframes only on transform and opacity; reduced motion respected; no sentence or label written in a component (all words come from `i18n/messages.ts`; a Nepali catalog is added later with the same keys). The component gallery `/design` is exempt from the words rule and is public (it holds no data).
- **Tested.** Rendered markup for both packs: identical structure, different words; landmarks, skip link, labels wired to fields, errors announced. 58 web tests, 17 deliberate breakages caught. Checked in a real browser (phone and 1280 px, dark mode, wrong and right password, sign-out, live swap from the Royal Softech pack to the sample school), and the deployed staging pages.
- **Not done, on purpose.** Self-hosted font files (Inter, Noto Sans, Noto Sans Devanagari): downloads need PM approval, so the theme names the font and the stack falls back to the device's fonts, which include Devanagari. Nepali toggle, dark/light switch (follows the device), sign-in Enter-key check by a trusted key event (the browser tool cannot send one; the form is a standard `<form>` with a submit button).
- **Known cost.** Every page load by a visitor who is not signed in makes two quiet 401 calls (`/api/auth/me`, then `refresh`), which show as red lines in the browser console. Fine at this scale (free plan: 100,000 requests a day). **OPEN:** avoid it with a non-secret "signed-in" marker cookie.

**D-029 Apple-level design standard.** PM instruction, 2026-09-20. The product is to be designed at Apple level, using the `apple-design` skill (github.com/dickwu/apple-design-skill, reviewed before install, installed per developer and git-ignored because it reproduces Apple's guideline text) through the whole build. Rules are in `CLAUDE.md` section 7 "Design quality". It is a working standard, not a licence to copy Apple: principles are translated for a responsive website, and a school's brand, the readability tests, the security rules and the phased plan take precedence. Consequence for the plan: every phase that ships screens ends with a design review, and the current placeholder look (D-028) is a first draft that the skill will be used to raise, starting with the public website in Phase 2 (a review of the existing sign-in, dashboard and gallery can be done at any time).

**D-030 Apple-level design review 1 (sign-in, portal shell, gallery) and its changes.** 2026-09-20, using the `apple-design` skill (D-029). Reviewed as a responsive website, so the principles and foundations applied, not Apple's platform conventions. Rating before: **Needs work** (one High accessibility gap, several High and Medium findings). Pages used: `accessibility.md`, `buttons.md`, `text-fields.md`, `entering-data.md`, `feedback.md`, `loading.md`, `motion.md`, `branding.md`, `color.md`, `typography.md`, `layout.md`, `dark-mode.md`, `writing.md`, `managing-accounts.md`, `tab-bars.md`, `sidebars.md`, `onboarding.md`.
- **Contrast (High, amends D-026).** The server only required 3:1 for the brand colour on the page, but links and quiet buttons are text (`accessibility.md › Vision`: text up to 17 pt needs 4.5:1). Now 4.5:1 for brand colour on page and on cards. Both packs and the built-in theme already passed (Royal 5.68 and 6.56, Sample 4.88, default 4.75), so no colour changed. The test was written first and failed.
- **One prominent button per view (High, `buttons.md › Style`).** Home and sign-in each showed two solid "Sign in" buttons. The header's Sign in is now quiet, and absent on the sign-in page.
- **Menu (High, `tab-bars.md`, `layout.md › Tablet`).** A lone "Dashboard" chip is not navigation, so a menu of one entry is not shown. From two entries: a bottom tab bar on phones (thumb reach, current tab marked by weight, colour and a top bar), a sidebar from 56 rem. `OPEN:` a "More" tab beyond 5 entries (`tab-bars.md › Avoid overflow tabs`); a test fails first. `OPEN:` tab icons, which need an icon-set decision.
- **Colour meaning (High, `color.md › Best practices`).** Labels were filled with the brand colour, the same as buttons. There is now no brand-coloured badge, and a test keeps labels off the brand colour.
- **Large text and narrow phones (High, `accessibility.md › Vision`, `typography.md › Supporting Dynamic Type`).** At 200% text on a 320 px phone the page grew wider than the screen, button labels wrapped, and a heading split mid-word. Now one column exactly as wide as the screen, buttons on one line, and page and card padding capped by screen width. Checked in a browser at 320 px and 200%.
- **Sign-in flow (Medium, `text-fields.md`, `entering-data.md`, `writing.md`, `managing-accounts.md`).** Focus returns to the password field after a wrong attempt; the message says what to do ("Check them and try again"); a Show/Hide toggle (44 px, reports its state) because passphrases are long and typed blind. **Judgment, not in the guidelines:** the toggle. One heading "Sign in to {school}" (it read badly split in two), and a line saying where the login comes from.
- **Session ended (Medium, `feedback.md › Best practices`).** A person whose session the server ended is told so on the sign-in page; a first-time visitor is not. The rule is a tested function.
- **Loading (Medium, `loading.md › Best practices`).** A placeholder shaped like the page replaces a lone spinner, and the brand shows a placeholder, so nothing jumps.
- **Targets and states (Medium, `buttons.md › Best practices`).** The brand link had a target under 44 px. Menu entries have hover and press states.
- **Copy and restraint (Low, `writing.md`, `branding.md`).** Possessives dropped ("Roles", not "Your roles"), scope wording in sentence case, no footer repeating the school's name under a header that already carries it, no card border (the surface and soft shadow separate it, as in Apple's grouped backgrounds), no unused messages (a test checks).
- **What already worked and was kept.** Semantic tokens with dark and light from the device and no app toggle (`dark-mode.md`); errors inline, not in an alert (`feedback.md`); in-button progress with a changed label (`buttons.md › Mobile`); a press state on every button; motion only on transform and opacity with reduced motion honoured (`motion.md`); skip link and focus ring; plain-language errors that do not blame.
- **Craft.** The look is a quiet utility, right for a school platform: identity comes from each school's theme, so there is no signature element yet. The Phase 2 public website is where a point of view is designed. **Limits:** contrast was computed from values; the review used screenshots, markup and the running site, not a screen reader or a real phone.
- **Tests.** 76 web tests (up from 58) and 448 API tests. 13 deliberate breakages of the new behaviour were caught.

**D-031 GEO skill suite for the public website.** PM instruction, 2026-09-21. The GEO-SEO skills (github.com/zubair-trabzada/geo-seo-claude, code reviewed at commit 4d3da26 before install) are installed per developer in `~/.claude/` and are not committed. They optimise a public website for AI search (ChatGPT, Claude, Perplexity, Gemini, Google AI Overviews) on top of normal SEO. The PM wants all of them available across projects, using what fits each one.
- **Scope here.** The public website only (Phase 2 onward): structured data, technical checks, crawler access, `llms.txt`, content quality and citability. Never the API or the portals, which are behind sign-in. Run the matching skill when a public-site slice touches that area, and report the findings in the slice's final message, as with the design review (D-029).
- **Skills that apply:** `geo`, `geo-audit`, `geo-schema`, `geo-technical`, `geo-crawlers`, `geo-llmstxt`, `geo-content`, `geo-citability`, `geo-platform-optimizer`, `geo-brand-mentions`. **Installed but not used here:** `geo-prospect`, `geo-proposal`, `geo-report`, `geo-report-pdf`, `geo-compare`. They serve an agency selling audits to clients.
- **`geo-update` is manual.** It pulls and installs upstream code, which would undo local path fixes and bring in unreviewed changes. It is set to `disable-model-invocation`, so it runs only when the PM asks for it, and the diff is read first.
- **What still wins.** The skills advise, they do not decide. A school's content and brand, the no-hardcoded-words rule, the security and permission rules, and the phased plan take precedence. Findings that would add a feature not in the documents go in the final message as suggestions (`CLAUDE.md` section 3). A page a skill recommends publishing, such as `llms.txt`, is a Phase 2 item to propose, not to add unasked.
- **Not done.** No audit has been run yet: there is no public site to audit until Phase 2.

**D-032 `ui-ux-pro-max` is the main UI skill; `apple-design` is the reviewer.** PM instruction, 2026-09-21. Amends D-029, which named `apple-design` as the skill to design and review with. The quality bar (Apple level) does not change. What changes is the split of work:
- **Design** each screen and component with `ui-ux-pro-max` (github.com/nextlevelbuilder/ui-ux-pro-max-skill v2.13.0, code reviewed before install, installed per developer in `~/.claude/skills/`). It is an offline search over bundled data: styles, palettes, fonts, 119 UX rules, 22 stack guides. The runtime scripts make no network calls.
- **Review** each UI slice with `apple-design` before it is called done, exactly as in D-029 and D-030 (pages read before citing, review in the final message).
- **It advises, it does not decide.** Its palettes, styles and fonts become pack theme tokens (D-008, D-026), never pasted values. Suggested fonts are self-hosted. It must not add libraries (Tailwind, shadcn/ui) without a reason and PM approval. `--persist` is not used without PM approval, because it writes a `design-system/` folder into the repo. Everything that won over `apple-design` still wins over it: the school's brand from its pack, the contrast and no-hardcoded-colours, fonts and words tests, the security and permission rules, the phased plan.
- **Why the guard on palettes.** A trial search for "school college education portal" suggested a playful Claymorphism style in indigo and orange, which is the opposite of the calm direction of D-029. So its output is filtered through the quality bar, not applied as returned.
- **Companion skills installed but not used for the app:** `design`, `brand`, `design-system`, `banner-design`, `slides`, `ui-styling`. Use one only when the PM asks (for example public-site assets in Phase 2). Never send school data, names or logos to an external image service (`design` logo, icon and CIP generation call Gemini, Atlas Cloud or MuAPI with API keys, which are not set), and never generate a school's identity with AI.
- **Not done.** No screen has been designed with it yet, so its effect on the sign-in and shell design is untested.

**D-033 `code-review-graph` for exploring and reviewing code, in every project.** PM instruction, 2026-09-21. A local knowledge graph of a repo (functions, calls, imports, tests), from the open-source `code-review-graph` v2.3.9. The PM supplied a "staging" copy that could not be checked against the upstream release, so its code was reviewed before install: it only runs `git` (and `svn`, `ps`, `tail` in a few places), has no `exec`, `eval` or telemetry, and makes no network calls in normal use. It is installed per developer, not committed: the command in `~/.local/bin`, seven skills in `~/.claude/skills/`, a user-scope MCP server, and two global hooks in `~/.claude/settings.json`. It is developer tooling, not a project dependency, so the dependency rule in `CLAUDE.md` section 3 is not engaged.
- **Use.** In a git repo, before scanning files to explore code, debug, review changes or plan a refactor, use the graph first (skills `explore-codebase`, `debug-issue`, `review-delta`, `review-pr`, `review-changes`, `refactor-safely`). The graph narrows scope; the source and its tests are still read before anything is changed, and Grep and Read remain the fallback.
- **Hooks.** After an Edit or Write the graph is refreshed, and at session start its status is shown, but only in a repo that already has a graph. The stock hook was changed for this: `update` on a repo with no graph does a full build, so it would have created a `.code-review-graph/` folder in every git repo edited. The guard asks the tool itself (`code-review-graph status` exits with an error and writes nothing when there is no graph), so a graph stored outside the repo is found too; a first version checked for a file inside the repo and would have skipped it without saying so. Bash calls are not hooked.
- **This repo's graph is stored outside OneDrive.** The graph is a SQLite file that changes on every edit, and a sync client can conflict with a live database, so it must not sit in the repo folder. It was built on 2026-09-21 with `code-review-graph build --repo <repo> --data-dir C:\Users\kumar\.code-review-graph\data\school-website` (146 files, 1,202 nodes, 12,257 edges, 5.6 s). The tool remembers the location per repo in `~/.code-review-graph/registry.json`, so nothing is written into the repo and `git status` stays clean. `CRG_DATA_DIR` is not used: it is one setting for every repo, so all repos would share a database. The graph is disposable: if it is lost, rebuild it the same way. Any other repo under OneDrive gets the same treatment.
- **Never** run `code-review-graph install` in a repo: it writes `.mcp.json`, skills and hooks, adds a git pre-commit hook and edits `CLAUDE.md` and `.gitignore`. Preview a rename before `apply_refactor_tool` writes it.
- **Not done.** The graph tools, hooks and global instruction load at the start of a session, so the first session that uses them is the next one. Its effect on tokens and review quality is untested here.

**D-034 Notifications and jobs.** Working default. Migration `0004_notifications.sql`; code in `apps/api/src/core/{jobs,notifications,email,crypto-box.ts,background.ts}`.
- **Outbox and runner.** A change that needs a notification writes an outbox row in the same batch (`enqueue`), so the message exists if and only if the change does. `drainOutbox` claims an event with a conditional update (a two-minute lease), so two runners never send one message; a failure is retried after 1, 5, 30, 120 and 720 minutes; after 6 attempts the event is given up on (`dead_at`) and its payload wiped. A unique `dedupe_key` means the same event can never be queued twice, and is also the provider's idempotency key. Handlers must be idempotent, because a crashed runner's event is picked up again.
- **No Cloudflare Queues yet (amends the jobs line in `CLAUDE.md` section 4).** A request delivers what it queued straight away (`waitUntil`), and a cron sweep every 5 minutes (`scheduled` in `src/index.ts`) retries whatever failed. One less account resource, still free. Add Queues only if volume needs it.
- **Secrets in the queue are sealed.** `crypto-box` (AES-256-GCM through Web Crypto, key from the `DATA_KEY` Worker secret) encrypts a payload before it is stored; the purpose is bound in, so a sealed value cannot be moved elsewhere and opened. A copy of the database (or its daily export) cannot be used to take over an account.
- **Email is an adapter (D-008 extension point).** Only the `dev` adapter exists: it writes to `dev_mailbox` so development and staging can read what would have been sent. It is refused in production (`validateEnvironment`), like demo mode. A real provider is a Phase 9 adapter. Links in emails use the per-deployment `SITE_ORIGIN`, never the request's Host header, so a forged Host cannot poison a reset link.
- **Tested.** 13 deliberate breakages caught (no lease, no backoff, never gives up, payload kept, stored unsealed, condition ignored, purpose not bound, dev adapter in production, and more). `OPEN:` move the email wording into the message catalog when Nepali is added; rotate `DATA_KEY` (sealed values become unreadable, so it needs a re-seal procedure); clean out old dev mailbox rows.

**D-035 Password reset.** Working default. Migration `0005_password_reset.sql`; `modules/auth/password-reset.ts`; web `/reset-password`.
- **Request** (`POST /api/auth/password-reset/request`, public): always 202, whether or not the address has an account. It is one batch of conditional statements, so the work is the same either way. Limited to 3 an hour per account and 10 an hour per address, so the form cannot flood an inbox. A newer link replaces any older one. **OPEN:** the timing is designed to match but has not been measured.
- **Token:** 256 random bits; only its SHA-256 hash is stored; valid for 1 hour; works once. The link carries it after the `#` (`/reset-password#token=...`), which a browser never sends to a server, so it cannot reach a request log or Referer. The page removes it from the address bar once used.
- **Confirm** (`POST .../confirm`): the password is judged first (so a weak choice leaves the link usable), then the link is claimed with a conditional update (two people using it at once: one wins), then the password is set, every session of the account is ended, other open links are closed, and an audit entry names the person. If the server stops between claiming and setting, the person just asks again: the safe way to fail. Expired, used, unknown and deactivated all answer the same 400.
- **Password rules** now also refuse the school's own name (`schoolNameWords`, from configuration, so no school is named in code; generic words like "college" are ignored) and ignore punctuation inside it, so "r.o.y.a.l" is caught. The same rule applies in `createUser`.
- **Tested:** 26 tests; 12 breakages, 11 caught at first and the survivor (a redundant expiry check) fixed by a new test. Checked live in a browser on localhost and on staging.

**D-036 Two-step sign-in with an authenticator app.** Working default. Migration `0006_two_factor.sql`; `modules/auth/two-factor.ts`, `core/two-factor/`; web `src/two-factor/`.
- **Who:** the Super Admin must (`core/two-factor/policy.ts`, per CLAUDE.md section 7); the Admin too, from D-038. Anyone who has turned it on is asked for the code. Other staff get a code by SMS or email in Phase 9, when those channels exist. ~~**OPEN (PM):** should Admin be required too?~~ Answered yes, D-038.
- **Flow.** After a right password, sign-in returns a *challenge* (signed with the session secret, 5 minutes, not a session) instead of cookies: `required` (enter the code) or `setup` (set the app up first). `POST /api/auth/2fa/verify` exchanges it for a session; `/setup` returns a new secret and an `otpauth://` address; `/enable` confirms the first code, turns it on, signs in and returns ten recovery codes, shown once. A session is never issued before the second step is done.
- **Codes:** RFC 6238 through the `otpauth` library (SHA-1, 6 digits, 30 s, one step either side), checked against the RFC's own test vectors. A code works once: the newest accepted time step is claimed with a conditional update (a replay, or two simultaneous submissions, cannot both win). Wrong codes count toward the same lockout as wrong passwords (5 an email in 15 minutes) and a throttled attempt writes nothing.
- **Secret:** stored sealed, bound to the person (D-034), so a copy of the table cannot make codes and a sealed secret copied to another row will not open. Setup never overwrites a confirmed secret.
- **Recovery:** ten single-use codes stored as hashes; using one is audited. **Reset for a lost phone:** `POST /api/users/{id}/two-factor/reset` (action `accounts.reset_2fa`, Super Admin only) removes it, ends the person's sessions and audits who did it. Nobody resets their own, since a stolen session could otherwise switch the protection off. **OPEN:** with only one Super Admin who loses both phone and codes, nobody can reset it; recovery is an operator procedure (Phase 10). **OPEN:** regenerate recovery codes; a QR code for setup (the key is shown to type or copy, and an "open in authenticator app" link works on a phone).
- **Screens (Apple review, D-029):** one prominent button per step; the code field offers the number keypad and one-time-code autofill (`entering-data.md`, `text-fields.md`); a wrong code keeps the cursor in the field with a message that says what to do (`writing.md › error messages`); the method is named "authenticator app" (`managing-accounts.md`); recovery codes need an explicit "I have saved these codes" before entering (the session is held back until then); "Copied" is announced to screen readers (`feedback.md`). **Judgment:** the setup key in groups of four. **Limits:** not reviewed on a real phone or with a screen reader; `ui-ux-pro-max` (D-032) was not used for these screens because it was not loaded in the session that built them.
- **Tested:** 53 API tests and 15 web tests. 19 breakages: 15 caught first time, 2 redundant-layer survivors (the read-side replay check and the conditional-update guard each have a second layer that still holds in sequential tests), 2 re-run and caught.

**D-037 Phase 1 exit check, and operator tooling.** 2026-09-21.
- **Automated:** `test/foundation-exit.test.ts` runs the whole foundation for BOTH packs (Royal Softech and the sample school) over real HTTP and the real schema: the pack starts the school; all six roles sign in (the Super Admin through setup, then the code); a session says exactly what was granted; every non-Super-Admin is refused a theme change and a two-step reset; a section-scoped Accountant reaches only their section; the theme swaps live and an unreadable one is refused; a password reset works and the email names the right school; the audit log is one unbroken chain afterwards.
- **On staging (`school-platform-staging`):** migrations 0004 to 0006 applied, secrets rotated (`DATA_KEY` added; the audit log was empty, so rotating its key broke nothing), cron sweep configured, one user per role created with the operator tool, all six signed in and were refused or allowed correctly, and the Super Admin set up an authenticator app in a browser, signed out, was stopped at the code, was refused a wrong code and let in by the right one. A password reset ran end to end: the email was delivered by the deployed Worker, a weak password naming the school was refused, the link worked once, the old password stopped working.
- **Operator tooling (this is how the first Super Admin is made on any deployment):** `npm run dev:user -- --remote --config wrangler.local.jsonc --email ... --role super_admin` with `USER_PASSWORD` and the deployment's `AUDIT_HMAC_KEY` in the environment (never on the command line). It runs the same `createUser` service through a small D1-over-wrangler shim (`scripts/wrangler-d1.ts`, with `renderNumbered` for values), so the same checks and the same audit chain apply. Secrets for staging are kept outside the repo.
- **Known limits, stated:** the 30-minute access cookie is stateless, so after sign-out, a reset or a two-step reset it can work until it expires (why money, approval and publish actions re-check the person themselves, D-024); the cron sweep's reliability on the free plan has not been observed over days; the dev mailbox is a staging convenience.
- **Result:** 590 API tests and 108 web tests, CI green.

**D-038 Phase 2 start: PM answers.** PM instruction, 2026-09-21.
- **Phase 2 (public website and content) is approved.**
- **Brand assets:** none received. Use the neutral wordmark and restrained accent from D-007. The real logo and colours arrive later as a pack theme change and must pass the contrast check (D-026).
- **Fonts:** approved to download and self-host Inter, Noto Sans and Noto Sans Devanagari, subset to keep to the page-weight budget. Fonts live in the repo and are served by the Worker, never from an external font host.
- **Photos and R2:** the public site is text and design only. R2 stays held (D-020); no uploads until the PM turns it on.
- **Content:** the programme and stream list stays a placeholder marked `OPEN:` (unconfirmed, `docs/client-profile.md`).
- **Admin two-step sign-in is required.** Closes the open question in D-036: the Admin role must, like the Super Admin, set up an authenticator app before getting a session. Not built yet. It must land before any real Admin account is created and before Admin content publishing goes to a real school. It is built first, as its own small slice (slice 0), before the content work.
- **Slice 0 built (2026-09-21).** One line of policy: `core/two-factor/policy.ts` now requires the second step for `super_admin` and `admin`; the flow, screens, recovery codes and reset are the ones from D-036 and needed no change (the web screens are not role-specific). A person with several roles is held to it if any one of them is Admin or Super Admin. Only the Super Admin can reset an Admin's two-step sign-in (`accounts.reset_2fa`), so an Admin who loses the phone and the recovery codes asks the Super Admin. **Existing sessions:** someone given the Admin role while already signed in is asked at their next sign-in, not mid-session (the same as any role change, D-021). **Tested:** 593 API tests (3 new in `two-factor.test.ts`, `foundation-exit.test.ts` now runs the Admin through setup and the code); the breakage `some` to `every` was caught by the new two-role test, and removing `admin` from the set was caught by the new Admin tests before the change was made. **Not done on staging (see below):** the `admin@school.example` test user has no authenticator app yet, so it will be asked to set one up at its next sign-in there (and the staging deploy of this change is not made yet).
- **Phase 2 slices confirmed by the PM:** 0 Admin two-step sign-in; 1 content model and cached public read API; 2 Admin editing screens; 3 public pages (Home, Programmes, Admissions, Scholarships, Facilities, Contact), with the fonts self-hosted here; 4 Notice, Holiday, Routine, Vacancy and Post with expiry; 5 design and GEO pass, page-weight budgets, staging exit check. Each slice is reported when done.
- **Left open, not needed for Phase 2:** a QR code on two-step setup, and whether applying a pack removes settings it no longer lists (D-026 open item).

**D-039 Website content model and public read API (Phase 2, slice 1).** Working default, 2026-09-21. Migration `0007_content.sql`; code in `apps/api/src/modules/content/` (`schema`, `queries`, `service`, `routes`); one small addition to `core/audit`.
- **Rules and where they are written:** five content types with a title, text, optional image or file, publish date and optional hide-after date; expired items hide automatically; Admin's own edits go live directly; a vacancy shows an email or phone, with no application system (`docs/source/sample-creation-information.md` §6.7); `ContentItem` in `docs/data-model.md`; actions `site.view`, `content.draft`, `content.publish` in the matrix.
- **PM answers (2026-09-21):** live content reaches the public pages by **fetch after page load** from a cached public API (the fixed pages stay static and crawlable); the Admin edits **only the five content types**, and the fixed pages' text comes from the school's pack. **Known limit of that choice:** a crawler that does not run JavaScript does not see the five types, and a post has no page of its own. The API returns everything a page needs, so a Worker-rendered notice board and post pages can be added later without changing it. **Correction, 2026-09-21:** the statement that the fixed pages stay "static and crawlable" was too strong. With one build for every school (rule 2) their words also arrive at runtime, so they are empty to crawlers that do not run JavaScript unless the Worker fills the pages in. That decision is now open (first item under "Open items carried forward").
- **Model.** `content_items`: kind (`notice`, `holiday`, `routine`, `vacancy`, `post`), title (1 to 200), body (1 to 10,000, plain text; a blank line is a new paragraph; markup is shown as typed, never run), contact (vacancy only, and required for a vacancy), urgent flag, status (`draft`, `waiting`, `live`), publish day, optional hide-after day, who made and published it and when. The database enforces the vacancy, contact and date rules itself. Dates are **AD calendar days by Nepal's clock**, so a day changes at Nepal midnight (UTC+5:45). An item shows from its publish day to the **end** of its hide-after day. **"Expired" is not stored**: it is derived, so nothing depends on a job having run. Nothing is deleted: taking an item down returns it to a draft.
- **Left out on purpose:** image and file columns (R2 is held, D-020; a later migration adds them); a holiday's own date (the documents give a holiday only the publish and hide-after days; suggested for the PM below); the timetable link for routines (a routine is text in Phase 2, `OPEN:` until the timetable exists); the Admin notification for an Urgent draft and the `waiting` flow (Phase 3 approvals).
- **Writes (`service.ts`).** Create, edit, publish, take down. Admin and Super Admin only (Co-ordinator drafts arrive in Phase 3). **The person is re-checked inside each write, from the database** (active user, active Admin or Super Admin assignment), not from the sign-in token, so a person switched off a moment ago is refused (CLAUDE.md sections 4 and 7). Publishing is conditional (`WHERE status IN ('draft','waiting')`), so two Admins publishing at once give exactly one winner and one audit entry; the other is told "already live". Editing merges what was sent with what is there and checks the whole again; the kind and the status can never be changed by an edit.
- **Audit addition (touches the audit log, so stated plainly):** `recordAudit` takes an option `onlyIfLastChanged`. The entry is written only if the last business statement in the batch changed a row (SQLite `changes()`), and the result says `applied`. Without it, a lost race or a refused re-check would still leave an entry claiming the change happened. The default behaviour is unchanged for every existing caller. The chain, its triggers and its verification are untouched, and a skipped entry leaves the chain unbroken (tested).
- **Public read API.** `GET /api/site/content` (action `site.view`, open to anyone), optional `?kind=`. Live items in date, urgent first, then newest, at most 200, **one database round trip**. Carries only public fields (id, kind, title, body, contact, urgent, publishedOn, hideAfter); nothing about who wrote it. The same answer for every visitor, signed in or not.
- **Cache: a deviation from the brief, stated.** The brief says publishing purges a CDN cache so a notice is live within seconds (a working assumption). This answer is cached for **30 seconds, then reusable while it refreshes for 30 more** (`Cache-Control: public, max-age=30, stale-while-revalidate=30`), so a change shows within about a minute. There is no instant purge: the free `workers.dev` address has no shared cache to purge, and purging across Cloudflare's network needs a custom domain and an API token. **OPEN:** purge on publish once a school has its own domain (Phase 10 at the latest).
- **Tested (44 new tests):** create, edit, publish and take-down; every non-Admin refused; a deactivated Admin, an Admin whose assignment was switched off, and one switched off **between the check and the write** all refused with no entry left; two Admins publishing at once; validation edges; the visibility window on both edge days; Nepal midnight; order; kind filter; exact public fields; one round trip; anonymous, signed-in and forged-cookie callers get the same answer; the audit chain still verifies. **12 breakages, all caught** (window edges, the status filter, the order, the kind filter, the cache time, the publish condition, the person re-check in publish, create and edit, the audit option and the audit condition itself).
- **Suggestions for the PM (not built):** an optional event date on a holiday, so the site can show "Dashain: 3 to 12 Kartik" and sort by it; a "pinned" flag beyond Urgent.

**D-040 Admin content routes (Phase 2, slice 2, API half).** Working default, 2026-09-21. Same module as D-039 (`modules/content/`). The Admin screens that use these routes are not built yet.
- **Routes,** all `content.publish` (Admin and Super Admin) and all `Cache-Control: no-store` on the list: `GET /api/content` (every item in every state, most recently touched first, at most 200, filters `kind` and `state`), `POST /api/content` (a draft, 201), `PATCH /api/content/{id}` (send only what changes), `POST /api/content/{id}/publish`, `POST /api/content/{id}/unpublish`. A permission is checked before the body is read (D-027), so a signed-out caller with a garbage body gets 401, not 400.
- **Why `content.publish` for all five:** in Phase 2 only the Admin writes, and the service refuses everyone else anyway. When Co-ordinator drafts arrive (Phase 3), create and edit move to `content.draft`. The matrix and its generated document are unchanged.
- **Answers:** 400 for a body that is malformed or carries a field that may not be there (the kind or status on an edit, or any unknown field), 401 signed out, 403 for a role that may not, and also for a valid token of someone switched off since signing in (the write re-checks, D-039), 404 no such item, 409 `already_live` or `not_live` when two people act at once or repeat a click, 422 with a message when an edit would break a rule as a whole (for example, removing a vacancy's contact), 201 with the new id. A repeated click never does anything twice.
- **`state` for the screen:** `draft`, `waiting`, `scheduled` (live, not yet its day), `showing`, `expired` (live, past its hide-after day). Worked out on the server, in the database query and in code, so the screen never compares days. Both agree on the edge days (tested).
- **Tested (28 new tests, 665 API tests in all):** every role on every route; signed out and forged sign-ins; a cross-site write; a person switched off since signing in; bad bodies; the optional parts; empty and refused edits; publish, repeat and race (one 200, one 409); take down; the list's states, filters and order; the exact fields returned. **10 more breakages: 8 caught first time, 2 survivors:** a test of the newest-first order could not tell the wrong order from the right one (the edited item was also the oldest), so it now edits the older and then the newer item; and a branch in the create route that reports a rule failure is unreachable (the request schema refuses first), so it stays as a defensive line.
- **Still to do in this slice:** the Admin screens (list, edit form, publish and take down), with Bikram Sambat date entry through the date module, words in the message catalog, designed with `ui-ux-pro-max` and reviewed with `apple-design`.

**D-041 Admin screens for website content, and date conversion for screens (Phase 2, slice 2, screens half).** Working default, 2026-09-21. Web code in `apps/web/src/content/`, pages `/portal/content` and `/portal/content/edit`; API additions in `modules/content/` and a new `modules/dates/`. **Not yet reviewed against `apple-design` and not yet looked at by a person in a browser (see "Still to do").**
- **Bikram Sambat in and out (CLAUDE.md section 6, D-014).** Only the date module converts, and the web app never does. New public routes `GET /api/dates/to-ad?bs=YYYY-MM-DD` and `GET /api/dates/to-bs?ad=YYYY-MM-DD` (pure functions, cacheable for a day, listed in `PUBLIC_ROUTES`) answer with the converted day and a `confidence` (`disputed` in the BS 2062 stretch), or 422 `invalid_date` / `unverified_year` for a day that does not exist or lies outside BS 2000 to 2083. Content responses now carry both forms (`publishedOnBs`, `hideAfterBs`; `publishOnBs`, `hideAfterBs`, and `todayBs` on the Admin list), and null for a day outside the verified years, so a far-future date cannot break a list. These routes are also what admissions will use for dates of birth (Phase 4).
- **A light list, a full item.** The Admin list no longer carries the text or contact (200 long notices could be megabytes on a phone); `GET /api/content/{id}` returns one full item, and `GET /api/content?limit=` lets a new form ask for just today's date. Both are `content.publish`.
- **The screens.** A "Website" menu entry for the Admin and Super Admin (current on its own pages). The list: filters by type and status, a state badge in words (Draft, Scheduled, On the website, Ended), the Nepali start and end days in words ("5 Ashwin 2083"), Edit, and Publish or Take down with an inline "are you sure" (no modal), a message after every action, and skeletons while loading. The form: type (fixed after saving), title, text (plain; a blank line is a new paragraph; shown as typed, never run), contact (vacancy only), urgent, "show from" and "hide after" as Nepali dates typed year first, and a live preview drawn as the public page will draw it. Saving a draft, or changing a live item (which goes public within about a minute, D-039). One prominent button per view: "New item" on the list, "Save draft" or "Save changes" on the form. Problems show against their own fields, the cursor moves to the first one, and each day is checked with the server before anything is saved.
- **Where the rules live.** The form's checks mirror the server's and sit in `content/model.ts` (pure, tested without a browser); every server call and its answer-to-result mapping sit in `content/client.ts` (also pure). Words are all in the message catalog, including the twelve month names and each kind and state, through typed tables so the "no dead words" guard still sees them. New controls (`TextArea`, `Select`, `Checkbox`) follow the existing field styling and the touch-target, contrast and motion guards.
- **Small changes elsewhere.** The session now exposes its API client (`useSession().api`) so screens use the one that renews the session; it is created once in state rather than read from a ref during render (the lint rule). The page address is read with `useSyncExternalStore` (`content/address.ts`), the pattern the reset page uses, because a static export cannot know the query string when it is built. `createApiClient` takes a `baseUrl` for tests.
- **Tested.** API: 683 tests in all (18 new for the routes, the BS fields, the detail route and `limit`); web: 173 tests (65 new: the form rules, every server answer mapped, the screens' markup, the new controls, the menu). Breakages against the date routes and BS fields: all 7 caught.
- **Limits, stated.** The Admin list shows the 200 most recently touched items; older ones are reached by filtering. A disputed BS day (BS 2062 Baisakh 31 to Jestha 31) is converted but the screen does not yet ask for confirmation. There is no image or file field (R2 is held). Two Admins editing one item at once: the last save wins.
- **Still to do in this slice:** (1) a person signs in and uses the screens in a browser (the agent cannot enter passwords, so this needs the PM); ~~(2) the `apple-design` review~~ done, see D-042; (3) a check at 320 px wide with text at 200%; (4) deploy to staging (blocked for the agent, PM to run or allow).

**D-042 Apple-level design review 2 (website content screens).** 2026-09-21, using `apple-design` (D-029) for the review and `ui-ux-pro-max` (D-032) for the design searches. Reviewed as a responsive website, so the principles and foundations applied, not Apple's platform conventions. **Thesis:** get a notice from the Admin's head onto the public site, safely and with no surprises. **Rating before: Needs work** (two High findings). **After the fixes below: Good.**
- **Pages read, and used** (opened in full unless marked): `accessibility.md`, `entering-data.md`, `text-fields.md`, `feedback.md`, `loading.md`, `writing.md`, `buttons.md` (to Role), `alerts.md` (to Buttons), `lists-and-tables.md` (to Style), `toggles.md` (Best practices), `pickers.md` (Best practices), `layout.md` (to Adaptability); `color.md` and `typography.md` only by their guideline sentences. **`ui-ux-pro-max` searches** (no `--persist`): form validation and inline errors, confirmation before publishing, date entry, empty and loading states.
- **Fixed, High.** (1) *Keyboard focus was lost* when Publish or Take down turned into its confirmation, because the pressed button was replaced (`accessibility.md › Speech`: "Let people use the keyboard alone to navigate and interact"). Now focus moves to the confirming button, the question is tied to it (`aria-describedby`), and focus returns to the pressed button after Cancel or after the answer. (2) *Dates had to be typed year first with hyphens*, which a phone's number keypad cannot do, and any slip was reported only on saving (`entering-data.md › Best practices`: "When possible, offer choices instead of requiring text entry"; `pickers.md › Best practices`). Now each Nepali day is a day box, a month chosen by name and a year box (`content/BsDateField.tsx`), in the order a Nepali day is read, with the number keypad for day and year; a missing piece reads "Choose the day, month and year." The value the rest of the form sees is unchanged ("YYYY-MM-DD").
- **Fixed, Medium and Low.** The confirmation buttons said "Yes, publish" and "Yes, take down" (`alerts.md › Buttons`: prefer verbs that name the result, avoid "Yes" and "No"); they now say **Publish** and **Take down**, and the question names the item ("Put “Fee notice” on the website?"), matching `writing.md › Be action oriented`. "We could not find that item" became "That item could not be found" (`writing.md › Use possessive pronouns sparingly`: "Avoid using *we* altogether"), and "your" was dropped from two lines. A test now fails if any content message speaks as "we", "our" or "us", and checks that an action keeps its name from the button to its message (Publish, Published.; Take down, Taken down.; Save draft, Saved as a draft.).
- **Left as it is, with reasons.**
  - *Asking before publish and take down* (`alerts.md › Best practices`: "Avoid displaying alerts for common, undoable actions"). That guidance is about modal alerts, and ours is inline. **Judgment:** publishing reaches the public and can be cached for about a minute (D-039), so it is not fully undoable; take down is reversible, so its confirmation could go and be replaced by an "Undo" after the fact. **PM to decide.**
  - *A new item needs two steps to go live* (save the draft, then Publish from the list). The documents say the Admin's own edits go live directly, and `design-principles.md` (Agency) argues for a shorter path. A secondary "Save and publish" on the form would do it, but it is two server calls that can half-succeed, so it is a suggestion, not built. **PM to decide.**
  - *Validation happens on save and when the form is sent* (`text-fields.md › Best practices`: "Validate fields when it makes sense"). The date boxes remove the most error-prone entry; a title or text left empty is reported on save with the cursor moved to it. Low.
  - *Changing a filter replaces the list with placeholders* rather than keeping the old list until the new one arrives (`loading.md › Best practices`: "Show something as soon as possible"). Low.
  - *An empty list has no button of its own*, only the "New item" button above it (`writing.md › Provide clear next steps on any blank screens`). Kept so there is one prominent button per view (D-030). Low.
- **What works, to keep.** One prominent button per view, everything else secondary or quiet (`buttons.md › Style`: "Keep the number of prominent buttons to one or two per view"). State is always in words, never colour alone (`accessibility.md › Vision`: "Convey information with more than color alone"), and the brand colour never marks a label (`color.md`: "Avoid using the same color to mean different things"). Labels above every field, none as placeholders, errors next to their field with the cursor moved there, and one alert-role message for the whole form. Controls are 44 px with 12 px between them (`accessibility.md › Mobility`). Skeletons while loading and a message after every action, which stays until the person does something else (no timers, `accessibility.md › Cognitive`). A live preview so nothing surprises the Admin (`feedback.md › Best practices`). No new animation, so reduced motion needs nothing.
- **Craft note.** A deliberately quiet utility: system-style controls, the school's own theme, no signature element, which is right for an admin tool. Its one distinctive part is the preview, which is the page the public will see.
- **Limits, stated.** Keyboard focus movement and the date boxes were checked by markup tests and by reading, **not in a browser** (the agent cannot sign in, and a focus effect cannot be tested without one); 320 px wide and 200% text were reviewed in the stylesheet only (the boxes wrap by `auto-fit`, gutters are capped by screen width, long titles break); contrast comes from the theme, which the server already checks, and was not recomputed here; not tried with a screen reader.
- **Tests:** 188 web tests (23 more than D-041). Breakages: 7 of 8 caught; the survivor is the focus effect, which only a browser can exercise.

**D-043 Self-hosted fonts (Phase 2).** Done 2026-09-21 under the PM's approval in D-038. The theme already named Inter, Noto Sans and Noto Sans Devanagari (`src/theme/css.ts`, `core/theme/tokens.ts`) but no font files shipped, so every device fell back to its own font.
- **What.** Three variable fonts, each one subset: Inter (Latin, 48 KB), Noto Sans (Latin, 36 KB) and Noto Sans Devanagari (Devanagari, 121 KB), in `apps/web/public/fonts/`. They came from the npm registry's `@fontsource-variable` packages, version 5.3.0, copied once (they are not a dependency, so nothing to update or audit). Files carry their version in the name. All three are under the SIL Open Font License 1.1; each licence sits beside its file (`OFL-*.txt`), as the licence requires.
- **How.** `apps/web/src/app/fonts.css` is the only place a font file is named (`@font-face`, `font-display: swap`, weights 100 to 900). The browser fetches a file only when text in its range appears: verified in a browser, the sign-in page loaded Inter only (48,256 bytes) and the Devanagari file (121 KB) was fetched only when Nepali text was put on the page. A school's theme picks `system`, `inter` or `noto-sans`; `system` costs nothing. `public/_headers` caches `/fonts/*` for a year (`immutable`), which is safe because names change with the version.
- **Guards.** The "no font-family written out" rule now exempts that one file, and new tests keep the exemption narrow: the file holds nothing but `@font-face` rules, each one swaps, names only `/fonts/` files on our own origin (no outside host), every named file exists with its licences beside it, and the three fonts the themes can choose are the three declared.
- **Not done.** Latin Extended subsets (accented letters outside Latin-1; not needed for English or romanised Nepali), italics, and preloading Inter for the first paint (the page shows in the device font for an instant, then switches). Page weight counts: Inter adds 48 KB to a page for a school that chooses it; the budget check is in slice 5.
- **Tested.** 191 web tests (3 new for fonts), lint and typecheck clean, the static export contains the fonts and `_headers`, and the browser check above.

**D-044 Public notice board (Phase 2, slice 4, public half).** 2026-09-21. Web: `content/NoticeBoard.tsx` (loader and `NoticeList`), `content/PublicEntry.tsx`, page `/notices`; a "Notices and updates" secondary button on the home page. It shows what `GET /api/site/content` returns (D-039): live items from their start day to the end of their last day, urgent first. No sign-in.
- **What it does.** One `article` per item: kind, an Urgent word, the title (a heading), the text as typed (paragraphs at blank lines; markup shown as text, never run), a vacancy's contact, and "Posted 5 Ashwin 2083" (with ", until ..." when it has a last day). Filter buttons (All, and only the kinds that have something) appear only when there is more than one kind; they are real `<button aria-pressed>` and wrap onto more lines instead of clipping (`ui-ux-pro-max`: interactive chips need a native role, a pressed state and visible focus; they must wrap). A polite status says how many are shown. Skeletons while loading, a retry on failure, a plain empty message.
- **Contacts.** A vacancy's contact becomes a link only if it is an email address (`mailto:`) or a phone number (`tel:`), built from characters that were checked (`contactHref`); anything else, including anything that looks like a script, is shown as plain text. It is a full-size touch target (found at 20 px in the first browser check and fixed).
- **One component for the page and its preview.** The Admin's preview draws the same `PublicEntry`, so the preview is the page.
- **Verified in a browser** on the local database with sample content (new script `npm run dev:content -- --email <an Admin>`, local only, through the same service functions as the Admin screens, clearly labelled sample): 7 of 9 published items show (the ended one and the not-yet-started one are hidden; a draft never shows); urgent first; the BS days are right; only the email and phone are links; markup and a script tag in a title or text appear as text (0 script and 0 image elements); pressing "Vacancies" shows 3 and marks only that button pressed, and "All" restores 7; at 320 px wide there is no sideways scroll and nothing runs off the screen, also with text at 200%; every button and link is at least 44 px tall. **Not verified:** how it looks (a screenshot could not be taken while the pane was hidden; layout was checked by measuring the page, not by eye), and it has not been read with a screen reader.
- **`apple-design` (short review).** Pages used: `buttons.md › Style` (one prominent action; filters are secondary), `accessibility.md › Vision, Mobility` (state is a word and a shape, never colour alone; 44 px targets), `feedback.md` and `loading.md` (placeholders while loading, an in-place message on failure), `writing.md` (plain, verb-led, no "we" or "our"; a test checks it), `lists-and-tables.md › Content` (short rows; long text stays in the article). Rating **Good**. Left: no keyboard test in a real browser; urgent items rely on the word "Urgent" and a heavy edge, which was a judgment (the search for an urgent-banner pattern returned no match).
- **GEO skills: not run.** This page is filled in by the browser after it opens, so a crawler that does not run JavaScript sees only its heading. Auditing it now would only report that. It waits on the decision under "Open items carried forward" (how public pages reach crawlers).
- **Tested.** 209 web tests (18 new: the contact link rules including hostile input, the public fetch, the board's markup, filters and pressed state, the notices page, the home link, the touch-target guards). Mutation checks were not run on this part.

**D-045 A session hint, so signed-out visitors make no sign-in checks.** 2026-09-21, done on the PM's "do as you recommend" (the item first under "Open items carried forward" in the previous version of this log).
- **Problem.** The session provider asked `/api/auth/me` on every page load and, on a 401, tried `/api/auth/refresh`, so a signed-out visitor cost two requests that could only fail, plus red 401 lines in the console. They are Worker requests, and the free plan allows 100,000 a day.
- **Change.** The server sets one more cookie with each session it issues: `__Host-signed-in=1` (Secure, Path=/, SameSite=Lax, 30 days, **not** HttpOnly, no Domain). It says only "there may be a session". It is set wherever a session is issued (sign-in, second step, recovery, renewal) and cleared wherever one ends (sign-out, a renewal that fails), because both go through `setSessionCookies` and `clearSessionCookies`. The page (`session/hint.ts`, `settleWithoutHint`) makes the two checks only when the hint is present; without it the visitor is signed out at once. The real cookies stay HttpOnly.
- **Safety, tested.** The server never reads the hint: a hint alone opens no door (`/api/auth/me` and `/api/content` still say 401), a hint added to a valid session changes nothing, and a valid session without the hint still works. A forged or stale hint costs one check, which is refused and makes the server clear it. It is not set for a wrong password or while a second step is still owed.
- **One-time effect.** Anyone already signed in when this is deployed has no hint, so appears signed out and signs in once more (sessions last 30 days). Staging has only test users, and nothing is live yet.
- **Verified in a browser.** A signed-out visitor to `/notices` made two API calls (`/api/config/public`, `/api/site/content`) and no sign-in checks; `/portal/content` still sent them to `/sign-in` with one API call. **Not verified in a browser:** the signed-in path (the agent cannot enter a password), which the tests cover on the API side and by `settleWithoutHint` on the page side.
- **Tested.** API: 8 new tests in `session-hint.test.ts` and one changed in `auth.test.ts` (sign-out now clears three cookies); web: 7 new. 7 breakages, all caught (hint not HttpOnly, its lifetime, its set and its clear, the exact cookie match, and both ways the page could wrongly settle).

**D-046 The Worker fills in the public pages for crawlers (option A).** 2026-09-21, on the PM's "do as you recommend". Amends nothing in rule 2: one build still serves every school.
- **How.** For a short, fixed list of public page addresses (`FILLED_PAGES`: `/` and `/notices` so far, more as slice 3 adds pages) the Worker answers the request itself. It fetches the Next-built static page from `ASSETS`, reads the school's configuration (and, for `/notices`, what is live today) from D1, and with `HTMLRewriter` puts into the HTML: a `<title>`, a description, a canonical address, structured data (JSON-LD: an `EducationalOrganization`, and an `ItemList` on the notice board), and a **visible block of plain semantic HTML** (`<div id="server-copy">`: headings, text, lists) at the start of `<body>`. A one-line inline script right after it removes the block before the app starts, so people with JavaScript see exactly the app, with no flash and nothing for the app to disagree with. A crawler that does not run JavaScript, and a visitor whose browser has it switched off, get the real words. This is the same content, not different content for crawlers.
- **Why not `<noscript>`.** Common text extractors, Mozilla's Readability among them, discard `<noscript>` on purpose, so the content would still be invisible to many crawlers.
- **Safety.** Everything that comes from the database (school name, section names, titles, text, contacts) is escaped before it is written into HTML; the structured data has `<`, `>`, `&` and the two line-separator characters escaped so a title cannot close its `<script>`. Tested with hostile titles.
- **Cost and limits.** A filled page is one Worker request and about two D1 reads (the free plan allows 100,000 Worker requests a day; static files still cost nothing). No shared cache is used (the free `workers.dev` address has none), so each view reads the database; the browser may reuse a page for a minute. The server copy uses a few English words of its own (`modules/site/strings.ts`), a web test checks they match the app's catalog. Nepali comes with the toggle. An address must be in **two** places: `FILLED_PAGES` and `run_worker_first` in `wrangler.jsonc` (and each school's local copy); a check in `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs` fails CI if the two lists differ.
- **Also fills the gap noted in D-039:** the five content types are now in the HTML of `/notices`.
- **Built.** `modules/site` (`pages.ts` the page builders and the list, `render.ts` the fill-in, `html.ts` the escaping, `strings.ts` the shared words), the Worker entry (`src/index.ts`) that answers a GET or HEAD for a listed page itself, `ASSETS` on the bindings, `run_worker_first` in `wrangler.jsonc` and in the git-ignored `wrangler.local.jsonc`, a `content/index.ts` so `site` reads live content through the module's front door, and rule 5 in `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`.
- **Found by the browser check, and fixed.** A browser that already held an older copy asked "has this changed?" with that copy's validators; the static files answered "no" (304, empty), and the Worker passed the empty answer through, so a returning visitor never got the filled page. The Worker now strips those headers (`If-None-Match`, `If-Modified-Since`, `If-Match`, `If-Unmodified-Since`, `If-Range`, `Range`) before asking for the file; a test that sends them would have caught it. Also: the app's layout added a generic description beside the real one, and the app overwrote the tab title with the school's name. The layout's description is gone, and a page can now state its own title (`usePageTitle`, `config/page-title.ts`), so the live title matches the server's ("Notices and updates | Example College") and returns to the school's name when the visitor moves to another page.
- **Verified in a browser** (local database, sample content): the raw HTML of `/notices` (what a crawler that does not run JavaScript receives) has exactly one title, one description, one canonical link, two structured-data blocks and about 1.4 KB of readable text with every live notice; the home page likewise. In a normal browser the copy is gone, there is one heading, one description, the canonical and structured data are still in the page after the app starts, no hydration or console errors, and the title follows the page through a soft navigation. **Not verified:** a run on the real Cloudflare edge (staging deploy is pending), and the page seen by a real crawler or the Google and Bing tools.
- **Tested.** API: 27 tests in `site-render.test.ts` (fields, canonical, replacing what the static page already says, header handling, the 304 case, HEAD, hostile titles and text in the block, the title and the structured data, another school, round trips) and 6 in `worker-entry.test.ts`; web: 4 for word parity with the catalog and 3 for the page title; 17 breakages, 16 caught and one an equivalent change (the database already allows a contact only on a vacancy). CI check: the boundary script fails if `FILLED_PAGES` and `run_worker_first` differ (shown by removing one).
