The Release A go-live checklist (backups running, error alerts, a privacy notice and consent text, a Nepal Privacy Act check) is a PM/operational matter, not code, and has not been started. `OPEN:` the two above, plus the two already carried from nav's 5-tab cap and admission-document scope.

**D-064 The file storage interface (D-020's stated default, built).** 2026-09-23. Section 9's own open-points table gives an explicit default for this line: "Build the storage interface only; no uploads until PM says R2 is needed." This is that interface, not a decision to enable uploads, R2, or `ApplicationDocument` — none of those are touched.
- **Built.** `apps/api/src/core/files/index.ts`: a `FileStorage` interface (`put`, `signedGetUrl`, `remove`) and `createFileStorage()`, which always throws ("File storage is not enabled (D-020). No adapter exists until R2 is turned on and one is written."), the same "loud error, not a silent no-op" shape `createEmailAdapter` already uses for an unconfigured or unknown adapter. Placed under `core/`, alongside `core/email`, rather than as its own `modules/files/` (the original module list in `CLAUDE.md` section 4 names `files` as a peer of `admissions` and `accounts`): `notifications` already lives under `core/` for the same reason — an adapter-based extension point with no domain table or business rule of its own yet — and `files` is the same shape. Nothing calls `createFileStorage()`; it exists so the next thing that needs storage (an `ApplicationDocument` upload, a Note or Assignment file, D-005/6/7's uploads) is written against a real interface instead of inventing one under time pressure, and so turning R2 on later is "write one real adapter," not "design the seam and write the adapter."
- **Tested** (API 1176 tests across 65 files, up from 1175; lint, typecheck and `check-boundaries.mjs` all clean): `createFileStorage()` throws, naming R2 and D-020, whenever it is called.
- **Not done.** A real R2 adapter, an upload route, `ApplicationDocument`, and everything section 7 requires of a working upload (content-type sniffing, rename on save, the 20 MB limit, a separate serving domain) all wait for the PM to say R2 is needed, exactly as the open-points table already states. This entry does not change that default; it only fills in the "build the storage interface" half of it, which was still unbuilt.

**D-065 A real bug found while setting up local test accounts: a newly admitted student could never sign in.** 2026-09-23. Found while preparing local demo accounts for the PM to test Phase 4 by hand.
- **The bug.** `approveApplication` generated a temporary password, hashed it into the new student's `users` row, and then discarded the plaintext — it was never returned to the caller, never shown anywhere, and (correctly) never stored anywhere else. The approval email even said "the temporary password given to you," but nothing had given it to anyone. `registerWalkIn` had the same gap: an auto-approved walk-in's Co-ordinator got back only an application id, no SID and no password, with nothing to hand the student standing in front of them. Every new student account created through admissions was, in effect, permanently locked out from the moment it was created.
- **First fix attempted, and rejected on rereading the code.** The first instinct was to put the temporary password in the `admission_decision` email, mirroring how the verification link travels. `core/temporary-password.ts`'s own doc comment rules this out explicitly: "it must never reach the audit log, a log line or **the outbox**." A secret sitting in the `outbox_events` queue table, even briefly, is a bigger exposure than a one-time value handed back in an HTTP response the caller already has to be authorised to make. The actually-established pattern is `accounts/staff.ts` (D-059): `createStaff`, `createTeacher` and `issueTemporaryPassword` all return `temporaryPassword` directly in their result, and `staff-routes.ts` documents it as "returned here and nowhere else." Reverted the email attempt and followed that pattern instead.
- **Fixed.** `ApproveResult`'s success case now carries `temporaryPassword`; `POST /api/admissions/applications/{id}/approve` returns it with `Cache-Control: no-store` (missing before this fix, on a response that already carried a secret). `registerWalkIn`'s result (renamed `WalkInResult` from the now Register-only `RegisterResult`) carries `sid` and `temporaryPassword` too, since a walk-in is approved in the same request; `POST /api/admissions/walk-ins` returns both, also `no-store`. On the web, `QueueScreen`'s approve action and `RegisterScreen`'s walk-in path both now show the existing `TemporaryPasswordNotice` component (`people/StaffScreen.tsx`, D-059's own UI for exactly this) instead of silently moving on — the first reuse of that component outside `people/`, still Co-ordinator-only, one-time, never persisted client-side.
- **Tested** (API 1176 tests across 65 files, web 490 tests across 37 files, all passing; lint, both typechecks and `check-boundaries.mjs` clean; OpenAPI and the generated web client regenerated): the "whole flow" test and the walk-in test both now capture the returned `temporaryPassword` and **actually sign in with it** against the real `/api/auth/sign-in` route, asserting `passwordChange: "required"` — proving the password is not just present in the response but genuinely usable, not merely that a field exists. Both routes are asserted `no-store`. A stale translation string left over from the original, broken design ("the student's SID and temporary password are ready in Search" — they never could be; only a hash is stored) was removed rather than left to mislead the next reader.
- **Not done.** No change to how a temporary password reaches the student for an application decided remotely through the online queue (an Accountant's registration, later approved by a Co-ordinator who never meets the applicant in person): the Co-ordinator still has to relay it by some channel of their own choosing, exactly as staff account creation already requires for a new Co-ordinator or Accountant. This was already true before the bug and is not a new gap; making that handoff itself part of the product (SMS, a second-channel email, anything else) is a Phase 9-shaped question, not part of this fix.

**D-066 The Phase 4 exit check. Phase 4 slice 1 is closed.** 2026-09-23, built to close the gap the PM asked about directly: every other phase closed with a dedicated exit test running the whole phase, for both schools, through the real API (section 2 rule 5, the second-school test); Phase 4 slice 1 shipped across D-063 to D-065 without one.
- **Built.** `apps/api/test/phase4-exit.test.ts`, run once for Royal Softech and once for Sample Basic School (`describe.each`, the same shape `phase3-exit.test.ts` and `phase2-exit.test.ts` already use, `env.SCRATCH_DB` as the second school's isolated database): a Co-ordinator adds a year and a class from the pack's own structure; a public applicant applies, verifies, appears in the queue, and is approved, with the returned temporary password proven genuinely usable (signs in, completes the change-required step, and the resulting session reaches the new student's own record and nothing else); a Co-ordinator's walk-in is admitted in one request with its own working password; an Accountant's registration goes to the queue, is refused to the Accountant's own reach, and the Co-ordinator resolves it; a Student and a Teacher are each refused at one representative write; the audit log is one unbroken chain throughout.
- **Two mistakes in the test itself, not the product, found by running it.** The test's own submission tokens (`p4exit-${8 hex chars}`, 15 characters) were one character under `ApplySchema`'s own 16-character minimum, caught immediately as a 400 rather than the intended 201 — lengthened to a full `crypto.randomUUID()`. The permissions check assumed a Teacher is refused student search outright; the matrix (`students.search`) actually grants a Teacher a scoped reach ("assigned"), a deliberate row, not a gap — the test now asserts 200 for the Teacher's own scoped search and 403 only for the Student, who has no row at all.
- **Tested.** The new file itself: 12 tests, run against both schools (API total now 1188 tests across 66 files, up from 1176/65; lint, typecheck and `check-boundaries.mjs` all clean).
- **Phase 4 slice 1 is closed**, on the same basis Phase 3 closed on: the exit test passes for both Royal Softech and the Sample Basic School through the real API. Two things stay open, both requiring the PM, not engineering, and both already stated in section 9 and D-063 rather than decided here: file uploads (multiple admission documents, blocked on R2, D-020) and the Release A go-live checklist (backups, alerts, a privacy notice, a Nepal Privacy Act check — the phase's own mini go-live gate in `build-plan.md`, operational rather than code). Deploying this slice to staging and checking the live pages, the way Phase 2 and Phase 3 closed, has not happened; that is the PM's step, not mine, since it needs the deployment's own secrets.

**D-067 The Release A go-live checklist: the buildable parts, and an honest line under the rest.** 2026-09-23, on the PM's direct request to complete it. The checklist has four items (`build-plan.md`); each is addressed on its own merits below rather than all four being waved through as "operational."
- **A privacy notice and consent text: built.** A new public page, `/privacy`, in `apps/web/src/site/PrivacyView.tsx` (a plain client-rendered page, not one of the six pages the Worker fills in for crawlers — a legal page does not need AI-search crawlability the way the marketing pages do, D-046, so this was kept simple rather than added to `FILLED_PAGES`/`run_worker_first`/the sitemap for a proportionate amount of extra machinery). Its wording is fixed, product-wide catalog text (`messages.ts`), not a school's own CMS content, since what this platform collects and why is the same for every school running it; only the contact details at the end are the school's own, read from the same `site.contact` the Contact page already uses. Content: what is collected (admission form fields, sign-in account fields), security records (sign-in attempts, password hashing), who can see it (role-based, enforced in software, with the audit log), children and guardians (many applicants are minors; the form is expected to be completed by, or with, a guardian), cookies (session only, no trackers), how long records are kept (kept for as long as the school operates, not automatically deleted — the honest current behaviour, not a promised retention period), consent, and how to ask a question. The public `/apply` form now has a required consent checkbox and a link to this page, gating submission with `admissions.error.consentRequired` the same way every other required field already does; declining to check it is a normal validation failure, not a silent block.
  - **This is engineering's best-effort draft, not a reviewed legal document.** The facts in it are accurate to what the code actually does, checked against the schema and the service layer while writing it, not invented. It has not been reviewed by the school or a lawyer, and the retention line in particular states a fact ("not automatically deleted") rather than a chosen policy, because no retention period has been decided. **`OPEN:`** the PM (and, before this goes live, the client) should read it and confirm it is accurate and sufficient before it is relied on as the school's actual privacy notice.
- **A Nepal Privacy Act check: a best-effort comparison, explicitly not legal advice.** Nepal's Privacy Act, 2075 (2018) is real law; I am not a lawyer and this is not a substitute for one, especially given this system holds identifying information about minors. As a sanity check against the Act's commonly-stated principles: **consent** — now asked for on the one anonymous write path (the checkbox above); not yet asked for anywhere else, because nowhere else collects data anonymously (a Co-ordinator hiring a teacher, or approving a walk-in, is a person entering another named person's details face to face or from paperwork already in hand, not that other person submitting it themselves). **Purpose and minimisation** — the admission form asks only for what section 6's own domain rules already specify; nothing extra is collected "in case it's useful later." **Security** — passwords are one-way hashed, never logged in plain form (D-059); every record change is attributed and tamper-evident (the audit chain). **Access control** — enforced by the permission matrix, not by policy alone (section 5). **Retention and a data subject's right to see or correct their information** — the weakest point found: there is no retention period, and no self-service way for a person to see or export what is held about them; the privacy notice's "contact us" section is the only current path, a manual one. **`OPEN:`** an actual Nepal Privacy Act compliance review, by someone qualified to give it, is not done and cannot be done by writing code; this comparison only tells the PM where to start that conversation.
- **Backups: partly already true, partly blocked on the same thing as uploads.** Cloudflare D1's Time Travel gives 30 days of point-in-time recovery automatically, with no code and no setup, on every D1 database that exists — already true for every deployment the moment it is created. What `build-plan.md` additionally asks for, "a daily export to a second location," needs somewhere to put that export; R2 is the natural place and is not enabled (D-020), so this half waits on the same PM decision as uploads and D-064's storage interface. A restore drill needs a real deployment to drill against, so it is necessarily a PM/operator action, not something this session can perform.
- **Error alerts and uptime monitoring: the free half built, the paid half needs the PM to choose a service.** `apps/api/src/app.ts` now has a global `app.onError` (via `core/error-handler.ts`, tested): an unhandled exception used to be answered by Hono with no record anywhere that it happened; it is now logged (visible in the Worker's own Cloudflare dashboard logs, `wrangler tail`, at zero cost) and still answered with a generic `{error: "internal_error"}`, never a leaked stack trace or message. This is the hook point a real service (Sentry or similar) would read from once the PM picks one and gives it a DSN as a secret — a few lines added to this same handler, not a redesign. Uptime monitoring needs nothing further built at all: `GET /api/health` already exists (Phase 1) for exactly this; an external monitor (UptimeRobot or similar) just needs to be pointed at it, and "alerts to a named person" is that service's own configuration, naming a real person only the PM can name.
- **Tested** (API 1190 tests across 67 files, up from 1188/66; web 492 tests across 37 files, up from 490; lint, both typechecks and `check-boundaries.mjs` clean; the page-weight budget holds for `/privacy`, 188.0 KB compressed against 210 KB): the new error handler (an unhandled exception is logged and answered safely, proven with both an `Error` and a non-`Error` thrown value); the privacy page's markup, for both schools, naming the school and giving a working contact link; the consent checkbox verified live in the browser (submitting with it unchecked is refused with the stated message; checking it and resubmitting the same form succeeds, the real anonymous apply flow, end to end, not a mock).
- **Design.** Reused `SiteFrame`/`ContactView`'s established pattern exactly (loading shape, retry, the same section layout classes `AdmissionView` already uses) rather than inventing new markup or CSS for a one-off page — no new colours, fonts or components, so the existing guards (`guards.test.ts`) already cover it.
- **Not done, and not attempted:** signing up for a real error-tracking or uptime-monitoring service (creating a third-party account is not something to do on the PM's behalf), enabling R2 (D-020's own gate), an actual legal sign-off, and a self-service "see or delete my data" feature (not asked for anywhere in the documents; flagged above as a Privacy Act gap, not built here since it would be a real feature needing its own design, not a checklist tick).

**D-068 Royal Softech's placeholder "Sample text" replaced with draft, still-unconfirmed content.** 2026-09-23, on the PM's request so the site "looks alive" for a team demo on staging.
- **Built.** `packs/royal-softech/pack.json`'s `site` block (home summary, both programmes' names/affiliations/summaries/options, all five admission steps, both scholarships, all five facilities, and contact address/phones/email/hours) rewritten from "Sample text..." placeholders to specific, plausible content. Not invented from nothing: drawn from `docs/client-profile.md`, third-party research (CollegeNP, the college's own Facebook page) already gathered and explicitly marked there as "draft content... none of it is confirmed by the college." Phone numbers and email are NOT the real numbers that document found publicly listed — those stayed out on purpose, since a demo/staging site displaying a real business's real phone number risks a real person getting a real call about a piece of software; the numbers here are fictional but correctly formatted for Nepal. The academic structure itself (the two programme keys, `plus2-sample` and `bachelors-sample`, and their levels) is untouched: this is presentation text only, not a decision to expand the confirmed structure (Grade 11/12 plus three bachelor's degrees) into the nine real-world programmes the research found, which stays exactly the open point section 9 already states.
- **Two tests updated, both an expected, correct consequence of adding a contact email that did not exist before, not a workaround:** `crawler-files.test.ts` and `site-render.test.ts` each had a test asserting Royal Softech's pack has no email and none is invented; flipped to assert the email that now exists appears correctly (in `llms.txt` and in the structured-data `EducationalOrganization` block). Added a new test in `site-render.test.ts` (a cloned pack with the email stripped out) so the "no email is invented when there isn't one" branch stays covered by something, since neither pack now exercises it as its natural state.
- **Applied**, not just written: the pack was re-applied to the local database and, since staging is what the PM's team will actually see, to the staging database too (`school-platform-staging`) — checked live in the browser both places (a hard reload was needed locally the first time; the API itself returned the new content immediately, so the stale first load was the browser's own HTTP cache, not a server-side one).
- **Left alone, on purpose:** the "Sample: classes are closed tomorrow" notice some earlier local testing seeded is local-only (`scripts/dev-content.ts` "never touches a deployed database", per its own doc comment) and is deliberately prefixed "Sample:" by that script's own design, so people reading it know it is seeded test data, not a website bug — different from the pack's issue, which was forgotten placeholder text with no such deliberate marking. Staging has no such notice at all.
- **Tested** (API 1191 tests across 67 files, up from 1190; web 492 tests across 37 files, unchanged; lint, both typechecks and `check-boundaries.mjs` clean; page-weight budget still holds).
- **Not done.** The Sample Basic School pack's own content was left as it was: its placeholder text (`site.module.ts`-fixture-visible) is deliberately generic-but-complete already (a template school by design, not a specific real institution's forgotten copy), not the same "Sample text..." problem this entry fixes. `OPEN:` this content is still unconfirmed by the actual college and must not be presented to them, or made public/indexable, as fact — the same caveat `client-profile.md` already carries.

**D-069 Phases 5 and 7 approved; Phase 5 slice 1: student attendance.** 2026-09-28. The PM approved Phase 5 and Phase 7 ("Let's complete phase 5, and 7"; Phase 6, fees, waits) after two answers: **no file uploads yet** (R2 stays off, D-020 stands; notes, question papers and homework are text and links only), and Phase 7 is built with **stated defaults**, each marked `OPEN:`. As with Phase 4 (D-063) this is a standing authorisation to build the phase through; each slice still ends with tests, a report and this log. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-phase5-daily-school-life-design.md` (five slices). **Touches permissions** (no row changed: `attendance.student.mark` and `attendance.student.view` were pre-scaffolded; their first routes and the data-level tests arrive here) **and the audit log** (one entry per class-day register).
- **Rule and where it is written:** `CLAUDE.md` section 6, "Attendance": once a day, by the class's Class Teacher, Present or Absent only, same-day edits only, no in and out times; source document 6.1 (percentage with an alert below a threshold, 75% placeholder, section 9) and 6.2 (mark-all-present is the default).
- **Built.** Migration `0015_student_attendance.sql`: one row per enrollment and Nepal day, off the enrollment (never the student). Triggers hold the rules without the code: only today's Nepal date (`date('now', '+345 minutes')`) can be inserted or changed, a closed year rejects every write, and nothing is ever deleted. A new `attendance` module (`CLAUDE.md` section 4 names it): list the active year's classes in reach with today's state, a class's register for a day, the year summary per student, the student's own year (`/me`), and `PUT .../today` for the register. The register is one batch (every student's row plus the one audit entry), an upsert, so saving again the same day replaces the day and a retry changes nothing new. The Class Teacher is re-checked inside the batch (still this class's Class Teacher, still an active user with an active teacher role, D-021). Grants build the queries: `classOnly` is the class whose Class Teacher you are; section-scoped staff see their sections; a Student reaches only `/me`. The optional `attendance` module switch is enforced by the API (404 when off), not only by the menu. Percentage = present over marked days, rounded down; no marked days is no percentage (not 0%). The threshold lives in `modules/attendance/policy.ts`, the "attendance rules" seam, `OPEN:` 75% placeholder.
- **Saturday is not blocked.** `CLAUDE.md` says Saturday is the weekly holiday but no written rule forbids marking on one, and other holidays are a config placeholder; blocking would be deciding a rule. Unmarked days simply do not count.
- **Web.** An "Attendance" place for the Teacher, Co-ordinator, Admin and Super Admin. A Class Teacher gets today's register first (everyone present, tick the absent, one Save, a live count, "already saved today" on return); staff get the classes with today's state and a class page (a day's register by BS date, and the year summary with the flagged students); the Student's dashboard gets an attendance card (percentage, days, the flag, the days absent). **The menu now overflows:** the Co-ordinator and the Admin have six places, so on a phone the tab bar shows four and a last "More" tab lists the rest (`splitNav`: daily places stay tabs; Setup, People and the Website are marked `rarely`); the sidebar on a wide screen shows all. This closes the `OPEN:` in `nav.ts`.
- **Design review (`apple-design`, installed this session; `ui-ux-pro-max` is not available in this environment, so the design follows the existing components and tokens).** Pages read: `accessibility.md`, `toggles.md`, `tab-bars.md`, `feedback.md`, `lists-and-tables.md`. Applied: the absent ticks are one group introduced by a visible label (`toggles.md › Checkboxes`, "consider using a label to introduce a group of checkboxes"); state is in words as well as colour, "Absent" and "Below 75%" (`accessibility.md › Convey information with more than color alone`); a saved register is confirmed and a failure says why (`feedback.md`); today's state sits beside each class (`feedback.md › Consider integrating status feedback into your interface`); More only when the tab bar cannot hold every place, with the daily ones kept as tabs (`tab-bars.md › Avoid overflow tabs`). Not yet done: the 320 px and 200% text check in a real browser, left for the Phase 5 exit slice with seeded data.
- **Tested.** API: `attendance.test.ts` (22): the policy; the register (pre-filled, one audit entry, same-day replace and retry, a student from another class refused with nothing written); who may mark (only this class's Class Teacher; every other role 403; a deactivated or replaced Class Teacher refused at once); who may look (section scope both ways, a teacher only their own class, a student no class, Student A cannot reach Student B); percentages and `/me`; the triggers (another day, delete, closed year) without the code; the module switch; the audit chain. Broken on purpose: removing the Class Teacher check or the section filter fails the tests. Web: the nav split, the More tab and list, the register's markup and model, the loading shapes.
- **Not done.** Teacher attendance, the activity log, notes and homework, dashboards and the Phase 5 exit test (slices 2 to 5). Holidays other than the weekly one (config placeholder, `OPEN:`). An in-app notification record for the attendance flag (section 7 says absence alerts are in-app only with a per-event switch; today the flag is shown on the student's card and the Co-ordinator's summary, not sent).

**D-070 Phase 5 slice 2: teacher attendance.** 2026-09-28, built to the Phase 5 design (spec section 5) under the standing authorisation in D-069. **Touches permissions** (no row changed: `attendance.teacher.mark` and `attendance.teacher.view` were pre-scaffolded; their first routes and data-level tests arrive here) **and the audit log** (one entry per saved day, with the day before and after, and the reason).
- **Rule and where it is written:** `CLAUDE.md` section 6, "Attendance": teachers are marked by the Co-ordinator in a daily list pre-filled Present; past days are editable with a reason recorded. Source 6.3: exceptions are Absent or On leave. Source 6.2: the teacher sees their own attendance as a read-only monthly view.
- **Built.** Migration `0016_teacher_attendance.sql`: one row per teacher and Nepal day. Triggers hold the rules without the code: no future day, a reason for any day but today, no change to a day inside a closed academic year, nothing deleted. `GET/PUT /api/attendance/teachers/day` and `GET /api/attendance/teachers/me?month=` (a BS month; this month by default; every day of it with its weekday and mark). Saving writes every teacher in the Co-ordinator's reach in one upsert (Present unless listed as an exception) plus the audit entry, in one batch; saving again replaces the day. **Reach** is the same rule as who may manage a teacher (D-059): an institution-wide Co-ordinator reaches every active teacher; a section-scoped one reaches teachers whose home section is theirs; a teacher with no home section is reached only institution-wide. On a save the actor's reach is read from the database inside the write, never from the sign-in token (D-021), so a deactivated or narrowed Co-ordinator is refused at once. The Admin reads the list (`read`) but cannot save; a teacher cannot see the list, only their own month. The optional `teacher_attendance` switch is enforced by the API.
- **A decision inside the rule, stated:** "past days are editable with a reason" is read as *any* write to a day other than today needs a reason, including a first save of a day that was missed, since both change the record after the fact. A reason is at least 3 characters in the service; the trigger requires one to exist.
- **Web.** Attendance gains tabs: Students; Teachers (Co-ordinator, Admin, Super Admin); My attendance (teachers). The Teachers tab is one list, each teacher a device picker (Present, Absent, On leave) starting at Present, a live count, one Save; opening a past day by BS date shows the reason field, required. The Admin sees the same list read-only. My attendance is the month as a table (BS day and weekday, mark), with previous and next month.
- **Design review (`apple-design`).** Pages read: `pop-up-buttons.md`, `text-fields.md`, `toggles.md`, `accessibility.md`, `feedback.md`. Applied: one pop-up per teacher for the three mutually exclusive marks, defaulting to the likeliest one, Present, with the teacher's name as the label that predicts the options (`pop-up-buttons.md › Use a pop-up button to present a flat list of mutually exclusive options or states`, `› Provide a useful default selection`, `› Give people a way to predict a pop-up button's options without opening it`); the reason field is labelled with its purpose and a hint, not a placeholder (`text-fields.md › Show a hint in a text field`); On leave and Absent are words, not colours alone (`accessibility.md`). A row of one tab is never shown (D-030).
- **Tested.** API: `teacher-attendance.test.ts` (16): the list; today's save with one audit entry; replace; a past day refused without a reason and saved with one, the reason kept, before and after in the audit entry; a future day, an unverified year and a teacher out of reach refused; section scope both ways (a plus2 save never touches a bachelors teacher); only the Co-ordinator and the Super Admin save, the Admin looks, a teacher and the Accountant cannot see the list; a deactivated Co-ordinator refused; the own month (BS days, today's mark, default month, unverified year 422, bad month 400); the triggers without the code; the module switch; a closed year 409; the audit chain. Broken on purpose: widening the section reach fails two tests; removing the service's reason check is still caught by the trigger (the second guard working as meant). Web: the model (starting statuses, exceptions, month arithmetic across a year boundary), the tabs per role, the loading shapes.
- **Not done.** The same Saturday and holiday note as D-069. The teacher's own month is reachable only while the `attendance` module is on (the menu entry belongs to it); a school with student attendance off but teacher attendance on would need its own menu entry (`OPEN:`, no such school yet).

**D-071 Phase 5 slice 3: the daily activity log.** 2026-09-28, built to the Phase 5 design (spec section 6) under D-069's standing authorisation. **Touches permissions** (no row changed: `activity.write` and `activity.read` were pre-scaffolded; their first routes and data-level tests arrive here) **and the audit log** (one entry per write).
- **Rule and where it is written:** source 6.2, "Daily activity log: mandatory per class per day, visible to students and parents, with a reminder if skipped"; `docs/data-model.md` Phase 5, `ActivityLog (subject_offering, teacher, date, text)`, so an entry is per class **and subject**; the matrix: the teacher writes for their assigned subjects, the student reads their own class, the Co-ordinator their sections, the Admin reads.
- **Built.** Migration `0017_activity_log.sql`: one entry per class, subject offering and Nepal day (up to 2,000 characters), hanging off the class and offering, never a student. Triggers: written or changed only on its own day, never in a closed year, never deleted. A new `classwork` module (the home for slice 4's notes and homework too): the teacher's subjects today with their entries (`/api/activity/mine`, also the teacher's reminder), write today's entry (`PUT .../today`, an upsert; the teacher must still hold the active assignment and an active teacher role, checked inside the write), a class's day (`/api/activity/classes/{id}`), the student's own class for the last two weeks (`/me`), and for the Co-ordinator and the Admin the classes with written and expected counts (`/classes`) and today's missing subjects with their teachers (`/missing`).
- **Decisions inside the rule, stated:** "mandatory" is enforced as a visible reminder, not a block: the teacher sees each unwritten subject, and the Co-ordinator sees every class's gaps with the teacher's name (in-app only; section 7 keeps SMS for four events). A teacher reading a class sees only their own subjects' entries (their grant is `assigned`), while the Co-ordinator and the Admin see all. The reminder lists a subject only when a teacher is assigned to it, so an unstaffed subject is not "missing".
- **Web.** A "Classwork" place for teachers, students, the Co-ordinator and the Admin (the Co-ordinator's and the Admin's phone tab bars now carry four daily places and More: Website, Setup and People). The teacher writes each subject's entry in its own card (not-written label, labelled box, Save); the student reads the last two weeks by day; staff see each class's "3 of 5 subjects written" with who is still to write, and open a class's day by BS date.
- **Design review (`apple-design`).** Pages read: `text-fields.md`, `feedback.md`, `buttons.md`, `accessibility.md`. Applied: each box has a label that says what to write and a hint with the limit (`text-fields.md › Show a hint in a text field`); "Not written yet" and "3 of 5 subjects written" put the status where the item is (`feedback.md › Consider integrating status feedback into your interface`); with several entries on one screen no Save is the one prominent button, so each is secondary (house rule D-030, one prominent button per view); a failed save says why ("you no longer teach this subject") (`feedback.md › Show people when a command can't be carried out`).
- **Tested.** API: `activity-log.test.ts` (16): the teacher's subjects; write, rewrite, one row, an audit entry per write; empty and overlong refused; only the subject's own teacher writes (another teacher 404, every other role 403, an ended assignment refused); the Co-ordinator sees every subject with its teacher; section scope both ways, the Admin reads, the Accountant cannot; a teacher reads only a class they teach and only their own subjects in it; the student reads their own class through `/me` and no class by address, another class's student sees nothing; the missing list and the class counts, scoped; the triggers without the code; a closed year 409; the audit chain. Broken on purpose: dropping the assignment check, and dropping the own-subjects filter, each fail tests. Web: the editor's two states, the student's day list order, the loading shapes, the new menu split.
- **Not done.** Saturdays and holidays still count as missing days in the reminder (the holiday calendar is a config placeholder, `OPEN:`). No notification record is sent for a skipped entry; the reminder is what the teacher and the Co-ordinator see on screen.

**D-072 Phase 5 slice 4: notes, question papers and homework (text and links; no uploads).** 2026-09-28, built to the Phase 5 design (spec section 7) under D-069's standing authorisation. **Touches permissions** (no row changed: `notes.manage`, `notes.view`, `assignments.manage` and `assignments.submit` were pre-scaffolded; their first routes and data-level tests arrive here) **and the audit log** (an entry for every share, withdrawal, assignment, submission, review, request and decision).
- **Rules and where they are written:** source 6.2, "Content: notes and question papers by year, class and subject, live immediately; to replace, delete and re-upload (no versioning)"; "Assignments: deadline, instructions, optional attached file; late is flagged automatically; the teacher reviews, gives marks and feedback; resubmission is request-then-approve"; source 6.1 and `CLAUDE.md` section 7: notes cannot be downloaded, protection is soft (watermark with the student's name and ID, right-click and print disabled), never claimed to be a guarantee. The PM's answer of 2026-09-28: **no file uploads until R2** (D-020 stands).
- **Built.** Migration `0018_notes_and_homework.sql`: `class_notes`, `assignments`, `submissions`, hanging off the class and offering (notes, assignments) or the enrollment (submissions), never a student. Triggers: a closed year rejects every write; a shared note or a set assignment never changes except to be withdrawn, once ("delete and re-upload" becomes withdraw and share again, and nothing is ever deleted); a submission's text changes only after a resubmission is allowed; nothing is deleted. The `classwork` module gains: share, withdraw and list notes (teacher), the student's own class's live notes with their watermark (`/api/notes/me`, never cached); set, withdraw and list homework with counts (teacher), one assignment's roster with every student's submission, review (marks up to the maximum, feedback), allow or decline a resubmission; the student's own class's homework with their submission, submit once (late flagged against the deadline in the same statement), ask to resubmit with a reason, and resubmit once allowed (the earlier text is kept in the audit entry, the marks and feedback are cleared). Every write re-checks, inside its batch, that the teacher still teaches the subject in the class, or that the student is enrolled in it. The optional `notes` and `homework` switches are enforced by the API.
- **Decisions inside the rules, stated:** "Upload" is text plus an optional **https** link while R2 is off (`OPEN:` files wait for R2); plain `http:` and `javascript:` are refused. A deadline is one instant (a BS day and a Nepal time on the screen) and must be in the future when set. Marks are whole numbers, optional per assignment, out of at most 1,000. A declined resubmission request returns the submission to how it was (reviewed if it had been). The Co-ordinator and the Admin have no notes or homework view, because the matrix gives none; adding one is a PM decision.
- **Web.** Classwork gains tabs for teachers and students: Activity log, Notes, Homework (a switched-off module has no tab). A teacher shares a note or question paper to one of their subjects and withdraws it; sets homework (subject, title, instructions, BS due day, Nepal due time, optional marks, optional link) and opens each to review, mark, and allow or decline resubmission. A student reads notes behind the soft protection (their name and SID drawn faintly across each note, selection and the copy menu discouraged, hidden when printed, and a plain sentence that the notes are for them only; never a claim of security) and submits homework, sees the review, and asks to resubmit.
- **A bug found and fixed on the way:** the homework route reused the operationId `set_assignment` already used by Phase 3's teacher-assignment route, and the generated web client silently merged the two request types. Renamed to `set_homework`, and a new test fails on any repeated operationId.
- **Design review (`apple-design`).** Pages read: `text-fields.md`, `feedback.md`, `buttons.md`, `accessibility.md`, `pop-up-buttons.md`. Applied: every field is labelled for what it holds, with hints for the limits and the time zone (`text-fields.md › Show a hint in a text field`); the subject is one pop-up of "subject · class" choices, so the teacher never picks a subject that is not theirs (`pop-up-buttons.md`); status sits on each item (Submitted, Reviewed, Late, 8 of 10, "3 to review") (`feedback.md › Consider integrating status feedback into your interface`); Withdraw is a quiet button with no confirmation, since hiding the note is exactly what was asked (`feedback.md › Warn people when they initiate a task that can cause data loss that's unexpected and irreversible`, "don't warn people when data loss is the expected result"); the watermark is hidden from screen readers, which read the plain sentence instead (`accessibility.md › Describe your app's interface and content for VoiceOver`).
- **Tested.** API: `notes.test.ts` (12) and `homework.test.ts` (15): sharing (audit, both kinds, body or link, https only, only the subject's teacher, every other role 403), withdrawal (hidden, once, only by the subject's teacher, never deleted), the student's watermark carries their own SID and the answer is `no-store`, Student A never sees another class's notes or homework; setting homework (future deadline, https, only the subject's teacher), submitting once, the roster, review within the maximum, a stranger teacher cannot review, decide or withdraw, request then allow then resubmit (text replaced, marks cleared, the earlier text in the audit entry), decline returns it, late flagged automatically, a withdrawn assignment takes nothing; the triggers without the code; the module switches; closed years; the audit chain. Broken on purpose: dropping the teacher check on a submission fails the stranger test. Web: the watermark and its words, each homework state, the deadline instant and its Nepal time, the tabs per role.
- **Not done.** File attachments (R2). A reminder for homework nearing its deadline (in-app notifications, section 7, not asked for as a screen yet). The retention of submissions ("one year after the year closes", section 9, `OPEN:`) is a Phase 8 job.

**D-073 Phase 5 slice 5: dashboards, the browser check and the exit test. Phase 5 is complete.** 2026-09-28, under D-069's standing authorisation. Touches no permission row and stores nothing new: the dashboards read the same routes the screens use.
- **Dashboards.** The Teacher's "Today" card: their class's register (saved, with the absences, or not yet), the activity log's progress ("1 of 2 subjects written today"), homework waiting for review. The Co-ordinator's "The school day" card: registers marked across classes, whether teacher attendance is saved, activity logs complete per class. The Student's "Classwork" card beside their attendance card: homework still to hand in, the latest activity log. Each line is a fact and a link to act on it; a switched-off module adds no line.
- **The browser check** (house rule D-030, not done in slices 1 to 4): the app run locally with a school day seeded through the real API (`wrangler dev`, the local database only), every new screen for a teacher, the Co-ordinator and a student at 320 px wide, at 100% and 200% text, measured for sideways scroll and looked at. **Found and fixed:** long button labels widened five screens at 200% (fixed the house way, `button.wrapLabel` on these screens, not by changing the shared button, D-030; guarded by a test); the year summary and the day's register were tables whose long names pushed the numbers out of view on a phone, now wrapping list rows (`lists-and-tables.md › Prefer displaying text in a list or table`); "1 homework submissions" and "1 subjects" reworded to read correctly for any number; the day picker's heading repeated "Day", now "See another day". After the fixes: no sideways scroll on any of the 36 screen and text-size combinations.
- **The exit test** (`phase5-exit.test.ts`), for both packs through the real API with real sign-ins from the real first-password flows: the Co-ordinator sets up this year's class, subject, a hired teacher as subject teacher and Class Teacher, and admits two walk-ins; the register is marked (one absent); the teachers' day is saved; the activity log written; a note shared (201 for Royal Softech, 404 for the sample school, which switched notes off in its pack: the second-school test); homework set, submitted, reviewed; each student's attendance percentage and flag, the activity log, the watermark with their own SID, the reviewed marks; each student sees only their own; representative writes refused for a Student and a Teacher; the Co-ordinator's views agree; the audit chain is unbroken.
- **Not done in Phase 5, and why.** File uploads for notes, papers and homework (R2, D-020, the PM's answer of 2026-09-28). "Related reports" and Excel export (an export library is a new dependency, which needs the PM; the on-screen class summary and lists exist). Notification records for the attendance flag, a skipped activity log or a nearing deadline (section 7 allows in-app only; shown on screens today, not sent). A holiday calendar (config placeholder, section 9). Staging deployment of Phase 5 and a visual check there.

**D-074 Phase 6 slice 1: the fee ledger.** 2026-09-28, built to the Phase 6 design (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-phase6-fees-design.md`) under the PM's request of 2026-09-28 ("Also take care of phase 6"), a standing authorisation like D-069's. **Touches fees and the audit log.** Tests were written before the code.
- **Rule and where it is written:** `CLAUDE.md` section 6, "Fees and money": the balance is never stored; an append-only ledger (charge, discount, payment, reversal, refund, carried dues) enforced by triggers **and** a hash chain; whole paisa integers; reversals and refunds are new entries pointing to the original; payments apply to the oldest due first, partial payments allowed; receipts from a gapless sequence per section and year, from a counter row advanced in the same batch as the payment.
- **Built.** Migration `0019_ledger.sql`: `fee_structures`, `fee_items`, `ledger_entries`, `ledger_chain_head`, `receipt_counters`, `receipts`. The ledger's `amount_paisa` is signed: positive raises what is owed (charge, carried dues, reversal, refund), negative lowers it (discount, payment); a CHECK holds each kind's sign. Unique indexes: one charge per enrollment, item and period; one reversal per payment; one entry per source (a voucher, a gateway attempt, an adjustment). Triggers refuse any update or delete, any entry in a closed year, and a reversal that does not cancel exactly one payment of the same student, in full. `prev_hash` is unique, so the chain cannot fork. Each entry stores the previous entry's hash and its own HMAC-SHA256 (the `AUDIT_HMAC_KEY` secret with a `ledger` domain tag, so it cannot be confused with an audit hash); the head row moves in a trigger, and a stale head aborts the whole batch, which retries (the audit log's method). `fees/ledger.ts`: `writeMoney` (the entries, the receipt, the audit entry and the caller's guard in one batch; answers `done`, `not_applied`, `year_closed`, `duplicate` or `rejected`), `verifyLedgerChain`, `ledgerChainSummary`. `fees/allocation.ts`: `allocate()` applies every credit to the charges oldest due date first and reports due, overdue, credit and the next due; a refund or reversal larger than every credit is owed now (`owedNow`). Receipts: `<section key>-<BS year>-<5 digits>`; the gapless trigger refuses any number but the counter's next, and a second trigger advances it, so a failed payment rolls the number back with everything else.
- **Tested.** `fees-allocation.test.ts` (11; seven are property-based with fast-check: the balance is exactly the sum, due minus credit equals the balance, each charge's remainder stays between zero and its amount, oldest first, overdue never exceeds due, the order of input does not matter, whole paisa only) and `fees-ledger.test.ts` (10: one batch with one audit entry; a reversal once and in full; a charge once per period and a source once; the signs; a failed guard writes nothing, not even a link; many writers at once all land in order; update and delete refused and an entry not linking to the head refused, by the triggers without the code; with the guards removed, an edited amount breaks the chain at that entry; a closed year refused). Gapless receipts are tested in slice 3.

**D-075 Phase 6 slice 2: fee structures and charges.** 2026-09-28. **Touches permissions** (new row `fees.charges.generate`, Accountant, whole institution) **and approvals** (the engine's first second kind).
- **Rule and where it is written:** source 6.4, a yearly fee structure the same for every student in a class, approved by an Admin; `CLAUDE.md` section 6, "Approvals (Admin)": fee structure is one of the five types, approve-and-apply is one batch, stale if the subject changed, never your own; section 2, the billing-schedule policy seam.
- **Built.** A structure per academic year and programme level (every class of the level pays the same), items one-time, monthly, yearly or whole course, in whole paisa; one structure per level and year. Draft, then sent through the approvals engine, then live and fixed (triggers: items change only in a draft; nothing is deleted; no structure for a closed year). `feeStructureApprovalHandler` makes it live in the approval's own batch. `generateCharges` makes each enrolled student's charges from a live structure by the billing-schedule policy (`fees/policy.ts`), in chunks of 100 per batch, idempotently: the unique index means a repeat makes only what is missing, so a student admitted later is charged by running it again.
- **Billing schedule (the default, `OPEN:`):** one-time and yearly items are one charge due on the year's first day; monthly items are twelve charges, due on the first day of each BS month (a student enrolled mid-year is billed from their own month on); a whole-course item is one charge in the student's first enrollment in the programme. The client's real schedule and what "course-wise" means are still owed.
- **Engine changes (approvals).** `ApprovalHandler` gains `requesterSql` (a kind can re-check its own requester's role inside the batch) and an async `onApproved(db, id, {auditKey, actorPublicId, ledgerHead})`, so a money kind can build ledger entries chained to the current head; the engine retries when another money write moved the head. The website content kind is unchanged.
- **Tested.** `fees-structures.test.ts` (16): the schedule (monthly twelve, mid-year from the student's own month, whole course only in the first year); drafting in whole paisa, fractions, zero, negatives and a second structure refused; only the Accountant drafts, the Co-ordinator has no fees access, a section-scoped Accountant stays in their section; sending locks the draft; the Accountant cannot approve, an Admin can, and it is fixed; a declined structure goes back to draft; an empty draft cannot be sent; no charges before it is live; every enrolled student's charges with the balance right and the chain whole; a repeat makes nothing; a later student charged on the next run; another level's class, the Co-ordinator and the other section's Accountant get nothing; the audit chain. Nobody approving their own request and staleness are the engine's own tests (D-061), and slice 4's.

**D-076 Phase 6 slice 3: payments and receipts.** 2026-09-28. **Touches fees, permissions** (new row `fees.online.pay`, Student, own record) **and the audit log.**
- **Rule and where it is written:** `CLAUDE.md` section 6 (a payment is never edited or deleted; the gateway reference is unique so repeated callbacks cannot double-credit; gapless receipts); section 7 (important operations are idempotent: "what if the server finishes but the user never gets the response?"); section 9 (payment gateway: interface and demo adapter only); source 6.4 (cash at the counter, a voucher the Accountant verifies).
- **Built.** Migration `0020_payments.sql`: `fee_vouchers` (a bank and a deposit reference, unique while not rejected; decided once) and `payment_attempts` (a unique gateway reference; settled once). **Cash:** the Accountant records an amount with an idempotency key; the payment, the next receipt and the audit entry are one batch, and a retry with the same key returns the same receipt. **Vouchers:** the student reports a bank deposit (no scan while R2 is off, D-020); the Accountant verifies it (a payment and a receipt, in one batch) or rejects it with a reason. **Online:** `core/payments` defines `PaymentGateway`, with a demo adapter that works only in demo mode; the student starts an attempt, and the public callback confirms by asking the gateway itself, never by trusting the call, and applies once (a repeat answers the same). The callback is on the public-route allowlist. `OPEN:` the real gateway (Phase 9) posts from its own origin, so the same-origin rule needs an exception for that one path then.
- **Tested.** `fees-payments.test.ts` (14): a partial cash payment with a numbered receipt in one batch; a retry with the same key returns the same receipt and records nothing, a different amount under the same key is refused; receipt numbers gapless per section and year, a refused payment does not use one up; only the Accountant takes cash; a receipt is never edited or deleted and its number cannot be skipped (triggers); the student sees their own account and receipts through `/me`, never another's; the receipt reads from the ledger (number, student, amount, BS date); the Co-ordinator has no fees view; a voucher verified into a payment with a receipt; a bank reference claimed once, a rejection needs a reason and frees it; a student cannot verify and the other section's Accountant neither sees nor verifies; online payment not offered outside demo mode; a confirmed attempt credits once however often the callback comes; both chains unbroken.

**D-077 Phase 6 slice 4: discounts, reversals and refunds.** 2026-09-28. **Touches fees, approvals and the audit log.** Tests were written before the code.
- **Rule and where it is written:** `CLAUDE.md` section 6 ("Approvals (Admin)": discount, payment reversal and refund; any one Admin; approve-and-apply is one batch; stale; never your own; reversals and refunds are new entries pointing to the original); section 9 (discount reasons Scholarship, Sibling, Staff child, Other; every refund needs Admin approval).
- **Built.** Migration `0021_fee_adjustments.sql`: `fee_adjustments` (kind, status draft, pending, approved, recorded, closed, moving forward only; one open reversal per payment). The Accountant proposes a discount (an amount, or a percentage of what is charged; a reason from the list, a note required for Other), requests a reversal of a payment with a reason, or requests a refund of credit only. An Admin approves through the inbox; the ledger entry is written in the approval's own batch, chained to the ledger head. An approved refund is then recorded with how it was paid back (cash, bank transfer, cheque, an optional reference), which writes the refund entry. Until approval nothing changes on the account.
- **A decision inside the rule, stated: what makes a request stale.** Each kind has its own version: a discount goes stale when the student's charges or carried dues change; a reversal when its payment was reversed some other way; a refund when any payment, discount, reversal or refund lands on the account. A first version counted every entry on the account, so approving one request staled the others; it was narrowed so two requests on one student are decided independently.
- **Tested.** `fees-adjustments.test.ts` (12): a discount changes nothing until approved, then is in the ledger once; by amount, 'Other' needs a note, unknown reasons, fractions and more than was charged refused; a declined discount is never applied; new charges stale a waiting discount, a payment does not; two requests on one student decided independently; only the Accountant proposes, the other section's Accountant reaches nothing; a reversal needs an Admin, cancels the payment in full by a new entry and its receipt shows it; a payment is reversed once, a reason is required, a non-payment cannot be reversed; a refund is only of credit, needs an Admin, and reaches the ledger only when recorded; an Accountant who is also an Admin cannot approve their own request; both chains unbroken and no adjustment row deleted.
- **`OPEN:`** the discount reasons are the section 9 default; the client publishes a scholarship for Dalit students that may need its own entry (PM).

**D-078 Phase 6 slice 5: fee screens, dues, the report, reminders and the exit test. Phase 6 is complete.** 2026-09-28. **Touches fees and permissions** (new row `fees.reminders.send`, Accountant, whole institution).
- **Rules and where they are written:** source 6.1 (the student sees their fees and receipts); source 6.4 and 6.5 (the Accountant's screens, the dues list, reports with Excel export); `CLAUDE.md` section 6 (the Co-ordinator has no Fees view; the Accountant's student profile shows only Personal and Fees; NPR with Nepali grouping); section 7 (notifications one record per event, recipient and channel from a job with retry; SMS for fee overdue only once an SMS adapter exists).
- **Built (API).** `GET /api/fees/dues` (each student's charged, paid, due and overdue, and the totals, in the viewer's reach) and `/api/fees/dues.csv` (a UTF-8 BOM, quoting, NPR in Nepali grouping, and a leading `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` neutralised so a name cannot run as a spreadsheet formula). `POST /api/fees/reminders`: an email to every student with something overdue and an address, at most once a day each (the outbox's unique key), delivered straight away through the outbox. `OPEN:` a native .xlsx needs a library, which needs the PM; SMS waits for Phase 9.
- **Web.** A "Fees" place for the Accountant, the Admin, the Super Admin and the Student (the Co-ordinator and the Teacher have none). The student: due now, overdue, the next due, the history, receipts, and "Tell us about a bank deposit". The Accountant: find a student, their account (cash at the counter, propose a discount, reverse a payment, refund credit, the requests), fee structures (draft, items, send, make charges per class), vouchers to check, and dues with the CSV and the reminders. The Admin sees the same read-only and approves in the inbox. A receipt page prints cleanly. Amounts are typed in rupees and turned into whole paisa without floats (`parseNpr`).
- **The browser check** (house rule D-030): the app run locally with fees seeded through the real API, every fee screen for the Accountant, the Admin and a student at 320 px wide at 100% and 200% text. **Found and fixed:** long amounts and the dues rows widened the dues and account screens at 200% (amounts now wrap as a unit, and the links use `a.wrapLabel`, the house way, not a change to the shared button).
- **Design review (`apple-design`; `ui-ux-pro-max` is not available in this environment, so the design follows the existing components and tokens).** Pages read: `accessibility.md`, `feedback.md`, `lists-and-tables.md`, `buttons.md`, `text-fields.md`, `pop-up-buttons.md`, `toggles.md`. Applied: the student's screen leads with the one fact they came for, what is due now and when the next payment falls (`lists-and-tables.md`, hierarchy); status is in words beside the amount (Overdue, Reversed, Waiting for an Admin) as well as any colour (`accessibility.md › Convey information with more than color alone`, `feedback.md › Consider integrating status feedback into your interface`); every money field is labelled "Amount (NPR)" with an example as its hint and a plain error for more than two decimals (`text-fields.md › Show a hint in a text field`); the discount reason is one pop-up of the four reasons, and "by amount or by percentage" another, each with a useful default (`pop-up-buttons.md › Provide a useful default selection`); each screen has one prominent action (Record payment, Send, Make charges), and Reverse is a quiet button that only asks for a reason, since nothing happens until an Admin approves (house rule D-030, `buttons.md`); every approval-bound action says so before and after ("An Admin approves every discount. Until then, the full fee stands."). Left: a receipt PDF (Browser Rendering, `OPEN:`; the page prints), and a real-viewer check of Devanagari in a PDF, owed since the spike.
- **The exit test** (`phase6-exit.test.ts`), for both packs through the real HTTP API with real sign-ins from the real first-password flows: the Accountant drafts a structure with a monthly and a yearly item, the Co-ordinator cannot, an Admin approves it, charges are made for the class; cash and a verified voucher get consecutive receipt numbers; a discount and a reversal change nothing until approved, then apply once; each student sees only their own account and receipts, the Co-ordinator gets 403; the dues list and the CSV agree with the account; the ledger chain and the audit chain are unbroken.
- **Not done in Phase 6, and why.** A receipt PDF (Browser Rendering is a later step; the page prints). Carried dues ("previous dues") exist as a ledger kind but nothing writes them yet: that is the year rollover, Phase 8. A real gateway and SMS reminders (Phase 9). A native Excel file (a library needs the PM). Voucher scans (R2, D-020). The client's fee structure, discount reasons, refund rules and "course-wise" (defaults used, each `OPEN:`). Staging deployment of Phase 6.

**D-079 Phase 7 slice 1: grading policies.** 2026-09-28, built to the Phase 7 design (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-phase7-results-design.md`) under D-069's approval of Phase 7 and the PM's answer "Build with defaults". **Touches results and the audit log.** Tests were written before the code.
- **Rule and where it is written:** `CLAUDE.md` section 6, "Marks and results": a per-programme grading policy (marks to grade to GPA or percentage, the pass rule, the rank rule); +2 follows NEB, subject-wise letter grades and a credit-hour-weighted GPA, no total, "verify the exact scale against NEB sources before coding"; a class with no policy cannot be published; a policy change affects only unpublished results. Section 2: grading, ranking and tie-break are extension points. Section 9: tied students share a rank.
- **The NEB scale is NOT verified.** `www.neb.gov.np` is blocked by this environment's network policy, and the PM did not choose between allowing it, sending the directive, or building with the unverified scale; the build went ahead with the widely republished scale, marked `OPEN:` in `modules/results/grading.ts` and here: A+ 90 (4.0), A 80 (3.6), B+ 70 (3.2), B 60 (2.8), C+ 50 (2.4), C 40 (2.0), D 35 (1.6), NG below 35; NG when theory is below 35% or practical/internal below 40%, or a component was missed. **It must be checked against NEB's own directive before any real result is published.**
- **Built.** `modules/results/grading.ts`: two policies, pure functions over whole hundredths. `neb_gpa`: a subject is graded on its combined marks, each component weighted by its maximum (NEB's theory and practical split follows the credit hours, so this is the same weighting); GPA is the credit-weighted mean of subject grade points, two decimals, rounded half up; a result with any NG subject has no GPA ("NG"); a subject without credit hours refuses to grade. `percentage_division` (placeholder for Bachelor's and the sample school, `OPEN:`): one percentage over all subjects, every subject must reach 32%, Distinction 80, First 60, Second 45, Third 32. `rankResults`: ties share a rank and the next skips (1, 2, 2, 4); a result that did not pass is not ranked. Migration `0022_results.sql` adds `programmes.grading_policy` (null: none) and `mark_components.kind` (theory or practical; an internal assessment counts as practical). The programme's policy is set by the Co-ordinator (`PATCH /api/academics/programmes/{id}`, audited) and seeded from a pack where the programme has none (`academics.programmes[].gradingPolicy`: Royal Softech +2 `neb_gpa`, Bachelor's `percentage_division`; the sample school `percentage_division`); a component's kind on the curriculum screen. A policy change never touches a published card (a snapshot, D-081).
- **Decisions inside the rule, stated:** the policy is per programme (`CLAUDE.md`), not per class as the older source 6.3 said (DECISIONS wins over the source). An absence is recorded as absent, never as zero, and makes the subject NG (or a fail).
- **Tested.** `results-grading.test.ts` (14, two property-based): every NEB boundary; combined marks weighted by maximum; NG for low theory, low practical, absence; the credit-weighted GPA to two decimals; no GPA with an NG; no credit hours refused; every division boundary; a failing subject fails the result; weighting by maximum; a missing mark refused; whole numbers only and bounds for any marks; ties share and skip; a rank is one more than those strictly ahead.

**D-080 Phase 7 slice 2: elective picks and the marks grid.** 2026-09-28. **Touches permissions** (new row `results.electives.set`: Co-ordinator, whole institution or section; Super Admin) **and the audit log.**
- **Rule and where it is written:** D-056 (a student picks from an elective group; the pick is saved once students exist); `CLAUDE.md` section 6 (marks per component in a bulk grid; teachers edit until the Co-ordinator verifies); source 6.2 (draft-save, missing marks flagged).
- **Built.** `elective_picks` (switched on and off, never deleted; closed year refused). The Co-ordinator sets a student's picks per group: exactly the group's count, all from the group; a subject the student already has marks in cannot be dropped. `mark_sheets` (one per class, subject and terminal; Draft, Under review, Verified, Published) and `marks` (one per student and component; null until entered, `absent` never zero). Triggers: a mark changes only on a draft sheet (or on a published one while that student has an open recheck), never above the component's maximum, never for a student not in the class or a component of another subject; nothing is deleted; a closed year refuses everything. The teacher's subjects with each terminal's state (`/api/results/mine`); the grid lists only the students who take the subject; saving is one batch (the sheet made if missing, every mark upserted, one audit entry) and re-checks the teacher's active assignment inside it; submitting is refused while any mark is missing.
- **Tested.** `results-marks.test.ts` (elective picks 5, grid 7): picks exactly the count and from the group; the grid lists only takers; a subject with marks cannot be dropped, a pick switched off not deleted; section reach both ways, every other role 403; the teacher's list; components and the missing count; draft saves and replaces with an audit entry each; malformed marks 400 and misfit marks 422; only the subject's teacher (another teacher 404, others 403); submit refused while missing, absence counts; after submit the teacher and the database both refuse a change and a delete.

**D-081 Phase 7 slice 3: verify and publish.** 2026-09-28. **Touches results and the audit log.**
- **Rule and where it is written:** `CLAUDE.md` section 6 (results publish for a whole class per terminal, and Publish stays disabled until every subject is Verified; a class with no policy cannot be published; published marks cards are snapshots); source 6.3 (the inbox shows which subject holds a class up; verify, send back with a note, bulk approve).
- **Built.** The Co-ordinator's board for a terminal: each class in reach, each needed subject (one with components and at least one student taking it) with its state, teacher and missing count, whether the class is ready, and why not. Verify one or many (only sheets under review and in reach count; the answer says how many). Send back with a required note, from Under review or Verified (a student who joins late needs marks on a verified sheet), never after publishing. Publishing is one batch: the publication (conditioned on every needed subject verified, no mark missing, the programme's policy unchanged and the actor still a Co-ordinator for the section), every sheet to Published, and a marks card per student, each statement conditioned on that publication; a second publish is refused by a unique key. `result_publications` and `marks_cards` are insert-only (triggers); a card keeps names, subjects, components, marks, grades and the result as they were that day.
- **Decisions inside the rule, stated:** a subject nobody takes, or with no components, does not hold a class up. Grading problems (no credit hours under NEB) refuse the publish with the reason and write nothing.
- **Tested.** `results-marks.test.ts` (review 4, publish 10): the board's states and missing counts; send back needs a note and reopens the draft; bulk verify counts only what it may; only the Co-ordinator and Super Admin verify; another section 404; no policy refused; nothing visible before publish; one batch, one audit entry, three cards, the GPA and an NG as expected; publish once, and no send-back after; a policy change leaves the card alone; triggers refuse changing or deleting a card or publication; a class with an unverified subject refused and the board shows which; no credit hours refused with nothing written; only the Co-ordinator publishes; the audit chain.

**D-082 Phase 7 slice 4: what people read.** 2026-09-28. **Touches results.**
- **Rule and where it is written:** source 6.1 (the student's results by terminal and year, marks card, Top 20 name and rank only); source 6.3 (the whole-class sheet with totals and rank, exportable; staff see the whole school's Top 20); `CLAUDE.md` section 6 (Top 20 per section, only after the class is published); section 9 (Top 20 shown to students, name and rank only).
- **Built.** The student's own results (`/api/results/me`, found from the sign-in), each with the latest card and its rechecks; a card by id for staff in reach (any version); the whole-class sheet from the latest cards, with the rank in the class, and its CSV (quoted, formula-safe, UTF-8 with a BOM; `OPEN:` a native .xlsx needs a library, which needs the PM); the Top 20 per terminal. **A decision inside the rule, stated (`OPEN:`):** a Top 20 list is one section and one level (Grade 11 with Grade 11 across the section's programmes: "their own year and terminal") and one grading policy, from published classes only; ties can run it past 20 people. A student sees their own list only once their class is published, name and rank only; staff see every list in reach with class and score; a school that switched `top20` off gets 404.
- **Web.** A "Results" place for the teacher, the student, the Co-ordinator, the Admin and the Super Admin. The teacher: subjects by terminal with state, and the grid (a box per student and component, AB for absent, a live missing count, Save draft and Send for review; a sent-back note shown). The Co-ordinator: Review (terminal, each class's subjects with state and teacher, tick sheets under review and Verify selected, Publish per class with what it waits for), a sheet read-only with Verify or Send back, Rechecks, Class sheets with CSV, Electives, Top 20. The Admin: Changes (every recheck and post-publish change), Class sheets, Top 20. The student: results by terminal, each subject's grade, the marks card (printable), a recheck form, the Top 20. Setup gains the programme's grading pop-up and a component's kind.
- **The browser check** (house rule D-030): the app run locally with results seeded through the real API, every results screen for the Co-ordinator, a teacher, a student and the Admin at 320 px, 100% and 200% text: no sideways scroll. **Found and fixed:** the Setup Programmes screen (Phase 3, not new here) widened the page at 200% because of the Level name field and a long button label; setup rows and form buttons now shrink and wrap. The board no longer shows a disabled "Verify selected (0)" when nothing is under review.
- **Design review (`apple-design`; `ui-ux-pro-max` is not available in this environment, so the design follows the existing components and tokens).** Pages used: `accessibility.md`, `feedback.md`, `lists-and-tables.md`, `buttons.md`, `text-fields.md`, `pop-up-buttons.md`, `toggles.md`. Applied: every mark box has an accessible name (component for student) and the header gives the maximum and kind (`accessibility.md`); state is written on each item (Draft, Under review, Sent back, "3 missing", "Publish is waiting for: Physics (Draft)") (`feedback.md › Consider integrating status feedback into your interface`); one prominent action per view (Send for review, Verify, Verify selected), the rest secondary or quiet (house rule D-030, `buttons.md`); the grid is a real table that scrolls in its own box on a phone, never the page (`lists-and-tables.md`); choices from a short list are pop-ups with a useful default (terminal, grading, kind, elective) (`pop-up-buttons.md`); several elective picks are checkboxes introduced by a legend (`toggles.md › Checkboxes`); grades that did not pass carry a word badge, not colour alone. Left: marks card templates (2 or 3, with logo, signature and seal, source 6.3) and a PDF through Browser Rendering (`OPEN:`, the page prints).
- **Tested.** `results-views.test.ts` (19) and web `results-screens.test.tsx` (8) and `nav.test.ts`: the policy route (set, bad value 400, Admin 403, audited) and component kind; the student's own card from the snapshot; a student never reaches another's card, the class sheet or the rechecks, and cannot export; staff card reach by section; the class sheet's ranks with ties and an unranked fail; CSV formula-safe; no sheet before publish; Top 20 (own pool only after publish, name and rank only, ranks across the section's classes, Bachelor's never mixed in, staff lists with score and by section, switched off 404, Accountant and teacher 403); mark parsing and formatting without floats; grid labels and read-only; tabs per role and the Top 20 switch; each role's landing screen.

**D-083 Phase 7 slice 5: rechecks and the exit test. Phase 7 is complete.** 2026-09-28. **Touches results and the audit log.**
- **Rule and where it is written:** source 6.9 (a recheck button on a published mark; the Co-ordinator always notified and can edit and republish; the student notified of any change); section 9 (notify the Admin on every post-publish change, with a required reason).
- **Built.** `rechecks` (open until decided once; one open per student and subject; a reason required both ways). A student asks for one subject of their own published result; the Co-ordinator sees it at once with the current marks and decides: no change, or corrected marks, which in one batch update the marks (allowed only while that recheck is open), close the recheck, and add the next version of the student's card, regraded under the policy used at publish, with the reason. Earlier versions are kept; the class sheet and the Top 20 read the latest. **Notified** means shown, as in Phases 5 and 6: the Co-ordinator's Rechecks list, the Admin's Changes list (who decided and why), and the student's own results (status, reason, the corrected card). No email or SMS is sent (section 7 keeps SMS for results published, a Phase 9 adapter).
- **The exit test** (`phase7-exit.test.ts`), for both packs through the real API with real sign-ins from the real first-password flows: this year, a class, a terminal, two subjects with theory and practical components and credit hours, a hired teacher, two walk-ins; the teacher saves a draft, is refused while a mark is missing, submits both; the Co-ordinator sends one back and verifies both; nothing before publish, the Admin cannot publish, the Co-ordinator does; each student's card follows the school's pack (Royal Softech +2: NEB GPA 3.82; the sample school: 86.50%, Distinction); the Top 20 exists only where the school uses it (the sample school switched it off); a recheck corrects a mark into card version 2, the Admin sees the change, the other student sees nothing of it; the CSV; the audit chain.
- **Not done in Phase 7, and why.** NEB scale verification (see D-079; blocking for real use). Marks card templates with logo, signature and seal, and a PDF (Browser Rendering). A native Excel file (library needs the PM). The results-day load test (Phase 10 load testing; not run here). SMS or email for results published (Phase 9). The client's grading and ranking rules, Bachelor's pass rules, components and maximum marks, and the Top 20 pool (defaults, each `OPEN:`). Staging deployment of Phases 5 to 7.

**D-084 End-to-end test of a school year; six bugs found and fixed.** 2026-09-29, at the PM's request ("test the entire website, one complete year, all edge cases, and fix any bugs"). **Touches fees and results** (and a closed-year lock on fee requests).
- **What was run.**
  - `year-e2e.test.ts`: one school year for **both** packs through the real HTTP API, with real sign-ins from the real first-password flows. It covers setup (staff, year, three terminals, classes, subjects with theory and practical, an elective group, teachers); admissions (a public application sent twice, email verification, ask for changes, walk-ins, an Accountant's registration, a rejection that stays final, two Co-ordinators approving at once); daily life (register, teacher day, activity log, notes, homework with review and resubmission); fees (a structure approved by two Admins at once, charges run twice, cash retried, voucher, demo online payment with repeated callbacks, gapless receipts, discount, overpayment refunded, reversal, a late joiner, dues and CSV, reminders); and results over three terminals (elective picks, an absence, a sheet sent back, the Maths teacher replaced mid-year, a student joining after the sheets were verified, a recheck, Top 20, the class sheet). It then covers a teacher deactivated mid-year, and finally the year closed: every write in every module refused, reads still working, both hash chains unbroken. 17 steps per school.
  - `edge-probes.test.ts`: zero, negative, fractional and absurd amounts; future dates; empty classes; unknown ids; changes after publishing.
  - In a browser, against the local app with data loaded through the real API: every page (60) as each of six roles (anonymous, Co-ordinator, teacher, student, Accountant, Admin) at 320 px, checked for script errors, server errors, raw message keys and sideways scroll. Then a click-through of the real forms: sign in, a teacher's grid (a mark above the maximum refused, AB, send for review), the Co-ordinator verifying from the board, a student's recheck, the Co-ordinator correcting a mark, the Accountant taking cash and proposing a discount, the Admin approving it with a two-step sign-in.
- **Bugs found and fixed.**
  1. *A closed year still took discount, reversal and refund requests.* Refused now in the service, and by a new trigger (`0023_adjustments_year_lock.sql`).
  2. *A refund went stale on the wrong changes.* A payment or discount (which only add credit) made a pending refund stale, so approving a discount and then a refund failed. A new charge (which takes credit away) did not. It now goes stale on charges, carried dues, reversals and refunds. Recording a refund already re-checked the credit, so no money could be over-refunded.
  3. *After a year closed, a student's own Fees page said "no fee account"*: it only looked at the active year. It now shows the most recent year when none is active, so dues and receipts stay visible.
  4. *A bank deposit could be dated in the future.* Refused now.
  5. *A mark component's maximum could be lowered below marks already given*, making a grade pass 100%. Refused now, in the service and inside the update.
  6. *The Admin's approvals inbox showed fee requests by their code* (`fee_structure`), with an intro that mentioned only website content. A fee structure's line did not say what it cost, and amounts were not in Nepali grouping. Every kind now has words; a fee structure's line lists its items and the yearly total; amounts use `formatNpr`. Requests made before this change keep the summary they were made with.
- **Found, not bugs:** a check in my own browser script ran before the list refreshed. The `/design` component gallery (internal, not a school page) is wider than 320 px.
- **Not tested, and why:**
  - The year's close, promotion and carried dues (Phase 8, not built).
  - A real payment gateway and SMS (Phase 9).
  - File uploads (R2 off).
  - Staging (no Cloudflare credentials in this session).
  - A result against NEB's real scale (unverified, D-079).
