- **Tests added:** `year-e2e.test.ts` (34 steps over the two schools), `edge-probes.test.ts` (9), a refund-staleness case in `fees-adjustments.test.ts`, and web `approvals-kinds.test.ts`.

**D-085 Phases 5 to 7 reach `main` and staging; the NEB check; three fixes from the review.** 2026-09-30, at the PM's request ("complete open items"). **Touches fees and the approvals engine.** Tests were written before the fixes and failed first.
- **Merged and deployed.** The Phase 5 to 7 branch (built in a cloud session) was merged as PR #16. The PM applied migrations 0015 to 0023 and the pack to staging with `npm run provision --remote` and deployed with `wrangler deploy` (version `71acd9b2`); `/api/health` answers `{"status":"ok","database":"ok"}` and the new Fees, Results and Attendance pages load. The new screens were not clicked through on staging with a real sign-in.
- **NEB scale: checked as far as reachable; still `OPEN:`.** `neb.gov.np` answers (without `www`; its `www` certificate is wrong), but lists only two directives, neither the grading one. The Letter Grading Directive is published by the Curriculum Development Centre (`moecdc.gov.np`) and the Ministry (`moest.gov.np`), and neither could be reached from this machine (connection refused, in the shell and the browser). From secondary sources:
  - The bands and grade points in `grading.ts` (A+ 90 = 4.0, A 80 = 3.6, B+ 70 = 3.2, B 60 = 2.8, C+ 50 = 2.4, C 40 = 2.0, D 35 = 1.6, NG below 35) agree with every source but one, which gives D as 2.0 and looks like a copying error.
  - The fourth amendment (2081-03-16), as quoted: no original certificate without Grade C in the internal part and Grade D in the theory part. That is 40% and 35%, the two minimums in `grading.ts`.
  - **New: a Letter Grading Directive 2083 from the Ministry (reported May 2026) replaces the 2078 one.** Its reported bands are the same. Whether it changes the component minimums, or how a subject's final grade is made from its theory and internal parts (`grading.ts` combines the marks weighted by their maximums; NEB grade sheets print a theory grade, an internal grade and a final grade), is not known.
  - **Still needed before any real +2 result:** the official PDF of the 2083 directive (or the 2078 one with all four amendments, if 2083 does not yet apply to this year's exams). The PM or the client can download it in Nepal and put it in `docs/source/`.
- **Review of the merged fees and results code (medium depth).** Three findings, all fixed:
  1. *Discounts could add up to more than was charged.* Each discount was checked alone against the charges, and a pending discount did not go stale when another was approved, so two approved discounts could create credit that could then be refunded in cash. Now a discount is refused if, with the discounts already given, it would pass the charges, and approving a discount makes any other pending discount for that student stale (ask again).
  2. *Two approved refunds recorded at the same moment could both be paid.* The credit was checked before the batch only. The credit check now also sits inside the batch's own conditional update (after the refund, the sum of the student's entries may not go above zero), so exactly one wins.
  3. *Approving a discount or reversal left pending when its year closed gave a server error*, and a ledger too busy to append was reported as "someone else decided it". Now a closed year answers 422 with a plain message and applies nothing, and a busy ledger is an error, not a false "already resolved".
- **Tests added** (in `fees-adjustments.test.ts`): discounts together never pass the charges; two refunds recorded at once pay back only the credit; a discount waiting when the year closes is refused plainly and nothing is applied.
- **Not done.** The NEB scale (above). Clicking through the new screens on staging. Review findings below medium confidence were not looked for. A deeper review (`/code-review ultra`) is the PM's call.

**D-086 The staging link made ready for UAT: a test mailbox and a starter set.** 2026-09-30, at the PM's request ("the current link should be UAT ready"; "make initial ids but admins should have access to create IDs"). Phases 8 to 10 and UI polish stay open. **Touches permissions** (a new row `dev.mailbox.view`: Admin, Super Admin) **and fees** (the starter set charges fees, through the real API).
- **PM answers (2026-09-30):** load a starter set; a test mailbox page for emails; who creates accounts stays as built (Admin: Co-ordinators and Accountants; Co-ordinator: teachers and students; Super Admin: Admins); the older staging accounts are left as they are.
- **Test mailbox.** On a deployment whose email goes to the `dev` adapter (refused in production by `core/environment.ts`), nothing is sent, so testers could never follow a verification or reset link. `GET /api/dev/mailbox` (module `notifications`) returns the last 100 kept emails, newest first, to an Admin or Super Admin; with any other adapter it answers 404, even for Support. The page `/portal/mailbox` lists them with their links made followable (text shown as typed, only `http(s)` addresses become links). It is not in the menu, because the menu would show it in production too. Note: it shows password-reset links for any account on staging, which is acceptable only because staging holds fake data.
- **Starter set.** `npm run uat:seed` (`scripts/uat-seed.ts`, logic in `scripts/uat/seed.ts`) loads, through the deployed site's own API as the screens would: the active year (or else this BS year: a draft of it activated, as staging had, or made and activated), three terminals, class A of the +2 programme's two levels, five subjects with 75 theory + 25 practical and credit hours, three teachers hired and assigned (a Class Teacher per class), six walk-in students per class, and a fee structure per level (drafted by the Accountant, approved by the Admin, charged). It acts as the existing whole-school Admin, Co-ordinator and Accountant, signing short-lived access tokens with the session secret only the operator holds. It refuses a site whose test mailbox does not answer 200 (so never production), refuses a year that already has classes, and writes the new temporary passwords only to a file outside the repository (`~/.school-platform/uat-accounts-<tag>.txt`). `OPEN:` the subjects, marks split, credit hours and fee amounts are stand-ins until the college gives its own.
- **UAT guide** for testers: `docs/uat-guide.md` (sign-in, who creates whom, the mailbox, the starter set, what to try by role, known limits, how to report).
- **Tested** (written first): `dev-mailbox.test.ts` (Admin and Support read it newest first; every other role 403, signed out 401; with another adapter 404), web `mailbox-screen.test.tsx` (links followable, text never run, empty state), `uat-seed.test.ts` for both schools through the real API (two classes, three teachers, twelve students with distinct SIDs; a new student signs in through the first-password flow and sees charged fees; both hash chains unbroken; a second run is refused).
- **Design review.** A small staging-only page built from the existing list pattern (`setup.module.css` list and items, the shared loading shape and messages); one heading, no buttons, links in the theme's link colour, long addresses wrap (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`) so nothing scrolls sideways at 320 px. No full `ui-ux-pro-max` / `apple-design` pass: UI polish is left open by the PM for now.
- **Not done.** Running the starter set on staging and deploying this code (the PM runs both: the deploy is blocked for me). A real email provider (Phase 9). Clicking through every flow on staging with the testers' sign-ins.

**D-087 Programmes are the Admin's; a school starts empty.** 2026-10-01, at the PM's request ("Program only admin. And no initial program in any school. Things should be empty out of box."). **Touches permissions** (a new row `setup.programmes.manage`: Admin, Super Admin; `setup.structure.manage` no longer covers programmes and levels) **and the audit log** (programme and level writes are now made by the Admin). Supersedes D-057's "the Admin can look at everything and change nothing" for programmes and levels, and D-056/D-079's seeding of programmes, levels and grading policies from packs.
- **Built.** Creating, renaming and switching off a programme, setting its grading policy, and adding, renaming and switching off its levels are the Admin's and the Super Admin's alone. They are checked by the route permission and again inside each write (`adminForProgrammes`), so no Co-ordinator of any scope can make or change one. The Co-ordinator keeps years, classes, terminals, subjects, curriculum and teaching. The Programmes screen gives the Admin the add and change controls for every section, and tells everyone else that only the Admin changes them. Packs no longer seed programmes: the `academics` block is gone from the pack schema (a pack carrying one is refused) and from both packs, so a fresh school has sections but no programmes or levels. The UAT starter-set loader now asks for the Admin to make programmes first.
- **Stays fixed:** sections still come from the pack. They key receipt numbering and Top 20 ranking, and scope section-limited staff. Making sections creatable on a screen is a separate decision.
- **Tested** (written first, failing first): hand-written permission rules (the Admin manages programmes; no Co-ordinator, whole-school or section, does); `academics-programmes.test.ts` rewritten (the Admin and Support create, change and add levels; every Co-ordinator is refused with nothing written; the grading policy too); route tests (every Co-ordinator gets 403 on all four programme writes); a pack test (neither pack has programmes, applying one twice makes none, a pack that tries is refused); web `setup-model.test.ts`. Tests that need programmes now make them as an Admin through the services (`test/programme-fixtures.ts`), with the same programmes, levels and grading policies the packs used to seed. API 88 files / 1495 tests, web 45 / 547, typecheck and lint clean.
- **Not done.** Staging: the fresh UAT database still holds the two programmes the pack made on 2026-10-01; they can be switched off by the Admin on the Programmes screen (nothing is deleted). The setup checklist still tells the Co-ordinator what is missing; it does not yet say a missing programme is the Admin's to make.

**D-088 The Principal's dashboard, part 1: its data.** 2026-10-01, at the PM's request: a dashboard for the Principal (Admin) built to the PM's reference design (`School website design/adminhomepage.png` on the PM's desktop), with every figure from the database. This entry covers the backend; the screens follow in later PRs. **Touches permissions** (a new row `dashboard.overview.view`: Admin, Super Admin), **fees** (read only: collections and dues, computed from the ledger as the dues list does) **and the audit log** (read only: the recent-activity feed).
- **PM answers (2026-10-01):** money is shown in Nepali Rupees only, as stored (paisa, NPR, Nepali grouping); the sidebar's Setup becomes "Programs" (the Admin sees and adds programmes only) and "Reports" holds everything else the Admin can only look at; "Settings" has change password, profile edit (name, phone) and sign out; no bell; the greeting and website photos come later from the PM.
- **Built.** `GET /api/dashboard/overview`, one database round trip. Each module offers a dashboard part over its own tables only (`core/dashboard.ts`: statements plus a reader), and `modules/dashboard` composes them in one `batch()`. Parts: students (`admissions`), staff (`accounts`), attendance today, the last 7 register days, by programme and by class (`attendance`), collections in the last 30 days against the 30 before, net of reversals and refunds, and the active year's charged, paid, due and overdue with the number of students with anything overdue (`fees`), programmes with levels, classes, students and teachers (`academics`), published results by programme with pass rate and averages (`results`), pending approvals by kind (`approvals`), website drafts, items waiting and the last publish (`content`), and recent activity: the newest audit events of 13 kinds the Principal cares about, with the build team shown as "Support", never by name (`core/audit/recent.ts`). Migration `0024_dashboard_indexes.sql` adds indexes only.
- **Rules chosen while building, each `OPEN:`** "vs last month" is the last 30 days against the 30 before (students and staff as of then; collections in each window). An attendance anomaly, on a school day only, is a class below the 75% alert line (D-070's placeholder), or from noon Nepal time a class with no register yet (`UNMARKED_FROM_HOUR`). A fee follow-up is a student with anything overdue. The overall status counts each kind of thing waiting (approvals, follow-ups, anomalies, drafts) once: none is "on track", one or two "a few things need your attention", three or more "several".
- **Tested** (`dashboard.test.ts`, 9; written just after the code, so two mutations were run: removing the noon rule is caught, and counting every student who owes rather than those overdue was NOT caught until a not-yet-due amount was added to the test): Admin and Support see it, every other role 403, signed out 401, no-store; an empty school is on track with zeros and no made-up percentages; students and staff with the change since a month ago; attendance today, the day before, the trend oldest first and by programme; anomalies low and unmarked, never before noon or on a Saturday; collections and the change, due, overdue and follow-ups; approvals by kind, drafts, waiting, last publish; activity filtered to the Principal's kinds with Support unnamed; programmes with classes and students; the overall status. A hand-written permission rule. API 89 files / 1506 tests, web 45 / 547.
- **Not done.** The screens (sidebar, header, greeting, cards, tabs, attention panel, quick actions, website card, activity), global search, Reports, Settings, the role briefs for other roles, and the add buttons at the top of lists.

**D-089 The Principal's dashboard, part 2: the screen, the shell and the theme.** 2026-10-01, built to the PM's reference design (`adminhomepage.png`) on the data of D-088. **Touches no permission, fee, result or audit rule** (the screen only reads `GET /api/dashboard/overview`; the Approvals badge reads the existing inbox).
- **PM answers (2026-10-01):** Lucide icons and a self-hosted Source Serif 4 for headings may be added; the portal is **always light** (Royal's theme has no dark set any more, nor has the platform default); Royal Softech calls the Admin role **"Principal"** (its pack's wording: `role.admin`).
- **Built.** The Admin's and Support's home page is the dashboard: a greeting by Nepal time with today in BS and the worked-out status; four cards (students, faculty and staff, attendance today, fees collected in Nepali Rupees, compacted to lakhs or crores), each a link; "Institution at a glance" with four keyboard-accessible tabs (attendance chart over the last register days with BS dates, and today's bars by programme; programmes with classes, students and teachers; the year's fees; published results by programme); "Attention needed" with only the kinds that wait, or "You're all caught up."; quick actions to the real flows (`/portal/people?add=coordinator|accountant`, `/portal/content/edit?kind=post`, `/portal/setup/programmes?add=1`); the website card; recent activity with relative times, the build team shown as "Support". Placeholder shapes while loading, one retry when it fails, refetched when the page comes back into view. The portal shell for every role: a full-height sidebar with the school's name and icons, the Admin's Setup becoming "Programs" (programmes only, D-087), a red badge with the real number of approvals waiting, and an account menu (initials, role, school; Sign out). The phone keeps its bottom tab bar. The theme gains `accent`, `accentSoft`, `warn` and `warnSoft` (purple and amber, with 3:1 icon checks) and an optional `headingFont` (`source-serif`); both packs carry them.
- **Not yet in the reference's places, on purpose (no dead links):** the header search, "Reports" and "Settings" come in the next PRs; the bell was left out by the PM; the greeting and website photos wait for the PM's own photos. The "Last 7 days" label is a label, not a menu: the API serves the last 7 register days only.
- **Reference vs built, decided by the rules:** money is NPR (Rs.), not the reference's ₹; dates are BS; the logo is the school's name in text until the college gives its own (no AI-made identity).
- **Tested:** web `admin-dashboard-model.test.ts` (7: greeting by Nepal time, lakh and crore amounts, change labels, BS dates, relative times, every activity kind has words and a link, chart geometry), `admin-dashboard.test.tsx` (7: greeting, status, figures, attention only for what waits, the caught-up state, quick-action links, activity naming Support, no colour of its own), shell tests updated (Overview, icons, Programs for the Admin, Support named in the account menu), theme tests (new tokens, heading font, dark sets now brought by the tests themselves). In a browser on the local copy: light even with the device in dark mode, "Good afternoon, Principal", no sideways scroll at 320 px, no console errors. Web 47 files / 564 tests, API 89 / 1510, build and page-weight budget clean.
- **Not done.** No `ui-ux-pro-max` / `apple-design` pass: the PM's reference design is the source of truth for this screen. Reports, Settings (password, profile, sign out), global search, the role briefs for the other roles' home pages, and add buttons at the top of lists.

**D-090 "Add" at the top of each list, in a pop-up; quick actions open their forms.** 2026-10-01, at the PM's request ("for every add section it is at the bottom of the page. Give a button in top and get that section popped up"). No permission, fee, result or audit rule changes: the forms and their checks are the same, only where they open.
- **Built.** A shared `AddDialog` (`ui/AddDialog.tsx`): an "Add …" button beside the page title that opens the existing form in the browser's own modal dialog (focus stays inside, Escape closes it, the page behind is inert; it rises in with opacity and transform only, not at all with reduced motion). Used on People, Academic years, Programmes, Classes, Terms and Subjects. Notes and homework already had their forms at the top and are unchanged. Inside the pop-up a form drops its own heading and card (the pop-up carries both). The dashboard's quick actions now open the real form ready to fill: `?add=coordinator|accountant` opens People's pop-up on that role (only a role the person may add), `?add=1` opens the Programmes pop-up, and `/portal/content/edit?kind=post` starts a new website item as a Post (any unknown kind is ignored).
- **Tested:** `add-dialog.test.tsx` (button beside the title, a named dialog closed until asked for, a labelled close button), content model (`?kind=post` and an unknown kind ignored). In a browser on the local copy: Add Coordinator opens People's pop-up with Co-ordinator chosen; Add Program opens the Programmes pop-up; Escape closes it. Web 48 files / 567 tests, lint and both typechecks clean.
- **Not done.** The Admin's Programs page still shows the other setup tabs (read-only): they move to Reports in the next PR.

**D-091 Settings and Reports; the Principal's Setup is Programs only.** 2026-10-01, at the PM's request (Settings: change password, profile edit for name and phone, sign out; Reports: everything else the Admin can only see; Setup becomes Programs). **Touches permissions** (a new row `account.profile.edit`: Teacher, Co-ordinator, Accountant, Admin, Super Admin, each their own; two own-account routes on the reviewed "any signed-in user" list) **and the audit log** (`accounts.profile.updated`, `accounts.password.changed`).
- **Built (API).** `GET /api/account/profile` (anyone signed in: their own name, email, phone, and whether they may correct them). `PATCH /api/account/profile` (staff only, re-checked inside the write; a student is refused because a student's personal details are corrected only by the Co-ordinator, CLAUDE.md section 6; the email is the sign-in and cannot be changed here; audited with before and after). `POST /api/auth/password/change` (anyone signed in: the current password is required; a wrong one is recorded as a failed sign-in, so five in fifteen minutes lock the email for sign-in and for this, with nothing written; the new password meets the same rules as everywhere; every other session ends, this one carries on; audited).
- **Built (web).** Settings for everyone (sidebar, and the account menu): your profile (a form for staff; for a student, their details and "ask your Co-ordinator"), change password (current and new, with show and hide), and sign out. Saving a new name renews the sign-in so the new name shows at once. Reports for the Principal and Support: grouped links to every page they read but do not change (years, classes, terms, subjects, curriculum, teaching; find a student, student and teacher attendance; dues; class result sheets, Top 20). The Principal's Programs page no longer shows the other setup tabs (they are under Reports).
- **Tested** (API tests written first and failing first): `account-self.test.ts` (9: own profile and whether it may be edited; staff correct name and phone, audited; an empty name, an unknown field or an email change refused; a student refused with nothing written; the password change ends other sessions and keeps this one; a wrong current password changes nothing and counts toward the lockout, after which even the right one is refused; weak, school-name and same passwords refused; signed out refused). Web `settings-reports.test.tsx` (7: refusal messages, profile checks, staff form vs student view, password autocomplete hints, every Reports link is a page that exists, headings). Nav tests (Settings for every role, Reports for the Admin and Support). In a browser on the local copy: a name saved from Settings shows in the header after the automatic renewal; Reports loads with no console errors. API 90 files / 1520 tests, web 49 / 574.
- **Not done.** Global search (left out by the PM, 2026-10-01); the other roles' briefs (D-092); the UAT test doc update.

**D-092 "What you can do" on each role's home page.** 2026-10-01, at the PM's request ("every user should see what they can do… a brief on the home page that the user sees and understands their role"). No permission or data change: the home page only shows links.
- **Built.** A card per role the person holds, titled in the school's own word for the role ("What you can do as Vice Principal" at the sample school): one line saying what the role is for, then each thing it does as a link to the page where it is done. Co-ordinator: setup, teachers, admissions, teacher attendance, results, website drafts. Accountant: fee structures, payments and receipts with discounts, reversals and refunds, bank vouchers, dues and reminders, registering an application. Teacher: class attendance, the activity log, notes, homework, marks. Student: attendance, homework, fees and bank deposits, results and rechecks. A line for a module the school has switched off is left out. A person with two roles sees both cards. The Principal's home stays the dashboard (D-088, D-089); the Accountant's home, which said "nothing here yet", now has its brief.
- **Tested:** `role-brief.test.tsx` (4: every line leads to a page that exists; the school's own role word; switched-off modules left out; each role's home shows its brief and two roles show two). Web 50 files / 578 tests, typechecks, lint, build and page-weight budget clean.

**D-093 Amounts read "NPR" everywhere, the dashboard included.** 2026-10-01, at the PM's request ("It shows Rs. It should be Nepali currency"). The dashboard's money cards said "Rs. 28.4 L", while every fees screen already said "NPR". The dashboard now reads "NPR 28.4 L" (lakh and crore shortening and Nepali grouping unchanged). This is wording only: amounts are still whole paisa from the ledger. The two placeholder programmes left in the UAT database by provisioning before D-087 ("+2 Programmes", "Bachelor's Degrees") had no classes, applications, subjects or fee structures. At the PM's request they were removed from `school_staging_uat` with a one-off command the PM ran. This is a cleanup of test-database leftovers. Removing real records is still not allowed: real records are deactivated, never deleted.

**D-094 A holiday post names the holiday's own days.** 2026-10-01, at the PM's request after UAT ("implement trap 1"). In UAT the Principal set "Show from" to the holiday's date (2 October), so the post stayed hidden until the holiday itself. Website content only, no permission or money change.
- **Built.**
  - A holiday has a **Holiday date** and an optional **Last day** (for a holiday longer than a day, such as Dashain). The form asks for these before "Show from", whose hint now says "Today shows it now. It comes off the website after the holiday."
  - A holiday has no "Hide after" field. The server sets it to the holiday's last day, so the post shows from its start day through the holiday and is gone the day after.
  - Refused, with a message on the field: no holiday date; a last day before the first day; a start day after the holiday (nobody would see it in time); holiday dates on any other kind.
  - The dates are stored as AD days (migration 0025: `holiday_from`, `holiday_to`). Triggers keep the "holidays only, last day not before first" rule even if the service check were removed.
  - The public Notices page (the Worker's copy and the app's board) shows "Holiday on 16 Ashwin 2083", or "Holiday from … to …", in bold under the title, and no "until" line.
  - The portal list and the preview show the same line.
  - Holidays saved before this change have no dates. They show as before, and editing one asks for its date.
- **Tested.**
  - API `content.test.ts`: 5 new tests. The last day sets hide-after whatever was sent; the post is public before the holiday and gone after; the four refusals; an edit moves hide-after and is audited; the triggers hold. `site-render.test.ts` checks the Notices page line. The route tests list the new fields.
  - Web: model (5), client (1) and screen (4) tests.
  - API 1,525 tests; web 588 tests, typecheck, lint, build, page-weight budget, BOM and boundary checks, and a wrangler dry run, all clean.
- **Design review** (`apple-design`): `hig/entering-data.md › Best practices` (be clear about the data you need; prefill a sensible default: "Show from" is today) and `hig/typography.md` (the holiday line is set apart by weight, not colour). Nothing left open.
- **Not done (explained to the PM, not built).** The home page does not list posts: it links to Notices and updates, as designed in Phase 2 (D-039, D-046). A "Latest notices" block on the home page is a separate request.

**D-095 Sections are the Principal's to create; a school starts with none.** 2026-10-01, at the PM's request ("this should be out of box. For college admin can add Bachelor, Master, Architecture and then under this we can have subjects. If same is used for school it can be primary, high and junior college"). This extends D-087 (programmes) to sections, and replaces the pack-defined sections ("+2", "Bachelor's") of CLAUDE.md section 5. **This touches permissions and money**: section scopes, receipt numbering and the Top 20 are all keyed by section. None of those rules changed; only who creates sections, and when.
- **Built.**
  - Royal Softech's and the sample school's packs list no sections. A pack may still list some (demo and test packs), and applying one still only adds.
  - The Admin or Super Admin adds a section on the Programs screen, and can rename it. This uses the existing `setup.programmes.manage` permission, now worded "Manage sections, programmes and their levels".
  - Each write re-checks the person inside its own batch and is audited (`academics.section.created`, `academics.section.renamed`).
  - The key is generated (`s` plus 10 hex characters) and never changes, so everything keyed by it is safe across a rename.
  - Two sections may not share a name, whatever the capitals. A section is never deleted.
  - The programme list now also returns the sections the person may see, in the same round trip, so a new section shows at once.
  - On the Programs screen, a "Sections" card sits above the programmes. With no sections, "Add a Section" is the page's one prominent button and no programme can be added yet. Once there are sections, "Add a Programme" becomes the prominent button and "Add a Section" becomes secondary. Each section has a quiet "Rename".
  - AddDialog gained `variant`, `plus` and `ariaLabel` props for this.
- **Tests.**
  - API `sections.test.ts` (11 tests):
    - a new school has none;
    - Admin and Super Admin add sections, which are audited, ordered and verified on the chain;
    - Co-ordinator, Accountant and Teacher are refused, and nothing is written;
    - an Admin switched off since signing in is refused inside the write;
    - duplicate, blank and over-long names are refused;
    - a programme can be added under a new section;
    - rename keeps the key and the programmes, and is audited with before and after;
    - a rename to the same name changes nothing; another section's name is a conflict; an unknown key is not found;
    - only the Admin or Super Admin may rename;
    - a section-scoped person sees only their own section.
  - Pack tests rewritten for the new rule.
  - Every other test school gets its old sections from test data: `testPack` in `test/programme-fixtures.ts` and `test/sections.ts` on the web side.
  - Web: 3 Sections card tests.
  - Totals: API 1,536 tests, web 591, plus typecheck, lint, build, page weight, BOM, boundaries and a wrangler dry run.
- **Design review** (`apple-design`): D-030's one prominent button per view, applied by giving the prominent button to whichever action comes next; `hig/entering-data.md › Best practices` (be clear about the data you need: the hint gives examples, "Bachelor's, Master's or Primary").
- **Staging.** `school_staging_uat` still held the two sections from its first provisioning. Nothing referred to them (no programmes, role assignments, staff home sections, receipt counters or receipts), so at the PM's request they were removed with a one-off command the PM ran, like the programmes in D-093.
- **Not done.**
  - Switching a section off, which nothing needs yet.
  - The public website's Programmes page still groups by the section key each public programme names in the pack's site words. With no matching section, those programmes are listed without a heading.
  - `OPEN:` whether the public site's programme list should come from the Admin's programmes rather than the pack's words is a PM decision.

**D-096 The Programs screen becomes "Academic Structure".** 2026-10-01, from the PM's reference image and written brief. The page shows sections, then programmes, then levels, after the PM's design. No permission or money rule changed. The page reuses the existing models and routes; there is no new table and no second API.
- **Built.**
  - **The data.** `GET /api/academics/programmes` now carries, in the same single round trip:
    - each level's `students`: active enrollments in the active year, counted by one grouped query, never by fetching records;
    - each programme's `students`;
    - `totals` for sections, programmes switched on, levels switched on of programmes switched on, and students.

    A section-scoped person's figures cover only their section.
  - **The layout.**
    - The page title is "Academic Structure", with the subtitle in the school's own words, lower-case mid-sentence.
    - "Add a Section" is the page's one prominent button.
    - Four figure cards.
    - Each section is a raised card showing its programmes, levels and students, with Rename and a show/hide chevron.
    - Inside a section: "Programmes" with Add a Programme, and each programme as a bordered card. A programme card shows its affiliation badge (the existing affiliation; nothing like "NAAC" is invented), its counts and grading, Edit (name, affiliation, grading, switch off/on) and a chevron.
    - Inside a programme: "Levels" with Add a Level, and each level as a row with its students and a ⋮ that opens rename and switch off/on.
    - The first section and its first programme start open.
    - Singular forms read correctly ("1 Level · 1 Student").
    - Empty, loading (skeleton of the page's shape) and failed (inline, with Try again) states. Change failures are announced politely.
  - **Kept as it was.**
    - Nothing is deleted: a programme or level is switched off and keeps its history.
    - Every change goes through the existing service, which re-checks the person inside its batch and is audited.
  - **Smaller changes.** AddDialog gained `icon` and `hideLabel` for the icon-only ⋮. The old ProgrammesView and SectionsCard were replaced, and their now-unused words removed.
- **Not built, and why.**
  - **Reordering levels.** A level's ordinal is its place in the programme, which the year lifecycle (Phase 8: promotion) will rely on. Changing it is a Phase 8 decision.
  - **Deleting anything.** Not allowed (CLAUDE.md section 6).
  - **The ⋮ on sections and programmes.** It would have held one entry or none, and D-030 says a menu of one entry is not shown.
  - **Header search and bell.** Left out by the PM.
  - **Programme-specific icons.** The model has no programme type to choose them by, so each programme gets the same book icon in a rotating tint.
- **Tests.**
  - API `academic-structure.test.ts` (3 tests): counts per level and programme, students who have left not counted; totals ignore switched-off items; a section-scoped person's figures cover only their section.
  - `academics-routes.test.ts` level shape updated.
  - Web `setup-screens.test.tsx` (10 tests): figures, section counts, what starts open, heading levels 2, 3 and 4, badges and singular forms, the Admin's named controls with no second prominent button, a read-only view with no controls, empty states, and no colour of its own.
  - Totals: API 1,539 tests, web 596, plus typecheck, lint, build, page weight, BOM, boundaries and a wrangler dry run.
  - Checked in the browser preview as a Co-ordinator at 1440 px and 320 px, with no sideways scroll.
- **Design** (`ui-ux-pro-max`): the "nested accordion" and "progressive disclosure" searches returned no matching guidance, so the built-in defaults were used: real buttons with `aria-expanded`, 44 px targets, transform and opacity motion only, reduced motion respected.
- **Review** (`apple-design`):
  - `hig/layout.md › Best practices` (group related items) and `› Visual hierarchy` (alignment and indentation show the hierarchy), applied as raised card, then bordered card, then row.
  - `hig/disclosure-controls.md › Disclosure triangles` (a descriptive label: "Show Mechanical Engineering").
  - The same page's "no more than one disclosure *button* in a view" concerns a different control; the chevrons here are disclosure triangles, one per row, as the PM's design asks.
  - `hig/entering-data.md › Best practices` (each form says what it needs, with examples).
- **Seen in passing.** Next.js link prefetches log 404s for page-data files on every portal page. This was already the case before this change; it is flagged as a separate task.

**D-097 Delete only what nothing is attached to; otherwise switch it off.** 2026-10-01, at the PM's request ("if the higher set has dependent sub sets it can't be deleted. It can only be deleted if no further connections is present. This should be applicable until the very last"). **This changes CLAUDE.md section 6's "no hard deletes" for the academic structure only.**
- **The rule.** A section, programme, level or class may be deleted only while nothing points at it. Students, enrollments, the ledger, receipts, marks and the audit log are never deleted: a student always has an enrollment and history, so there is nothing to delete there. Anything in use can always be switched off instead, which keeps its history.
- **What counts as "attached".** All checked inside the delete statement itself, and backed by the foreign keys, so a link added a moment earlier makes the delete fail rather than leave anything pointing nowhere.

  | Item | Cannot be deleted while it has… |
  |---|---|
  | Section | a programme, a role assignment scoped to it, a staff home section, a receipt counter or a receipt |
  | Programme | a level, a class or an application |
  | Level | a class, a subject offering, an elective group, an application or a fee structure |
  | Class | an enrollment (its students, and through them attendance and fees), a teacher assignment, an activity-log entry, a note, homework, a mark sheet or a published result |

  A class in a closed year is never deleted (the year lock).
- **Who.** Sections, programmes and levels: the Admin or Super Admin, as for adding them. Classes: a Co-ordinator of the class's section, or the Super Admin (`setup.structure.manage`), as for changing them. Each delete re-checks the person inside the write and is audited (`academics.<kind>.deleted`, with what was deleted).
- **Sections can now be switched off** (migration 0026, `sections.is_active`). A switched-off section keeps everything in it, takes no new programmes (refused with a message), and is no longer offered where a section is chosen (the public configuration lists only sections switched on).
- **Screens.**
  - On the Academic Structure page, a section's Rename becomes **Edit**: rename, switch off/on, and Delete. A programme's Edit and a level's ⋮ also offer Delete.
  - Delete asks once more ("Delete X? This can't be undone." with "Yes, delete X" or "Keep it").
  - Where something is attached, the Edit says what is in the way and to switch it off instead.
  - On the Classes screen, Delete appears only on a class nothing is attached to.
  - The list answers carry `canDelete` (and a section's `active`), worked out by the database, so the screens never guess.
- **Routes.** `DELETE /api/academics/sections/{key}`, `/programmes/{id}`, `/levels/{id}` and `/classes/{id}`. Anything attached gives 409 `in_use`. `PATCH /api/academics/sections/{key}` now takes `active` too.
- **Tests.**
  - API `structure-removal.test.ts` (8 tests):
    - an empty section is deleted and audited;
    - bottom-up deletion: a level, then its programme, then its section, each only once the one below is gone;
    - a level with a class, and everything above it, is in use;
    - a staff home section is in use;
    - only the Admin or Super Admin may delete, and an unknown id is not found;
    - a switched-off section takes no programmes, is left out of the configuration, is audited, and can be switched back on;
    - a class with students is in use, while an empty one is deleted by the Co-ordinator and audited;
    - another section's Co-ordinator and a Teacher are refused.
  - Earlier tests updated for the new fields.
  - Web: 3 new tests.
  - Totals: API 1,547 tests, web 599, plus typecheck, lint, build, page weight, BOM, boundaries and a wrangler dry run.

**D-098 Website Content: the Principal's publishing page, redesigned to the PM's reference.** 2026-10-01, at the PM's request (a reference screenshot and a written brief: "REFERENCE IMAGE = VISUAL SOURCE OF TRUTH. EXISTING CODEBASE = TECHNICAL SOURCE OF TRUTH."). As in D-089, the PM's reference is the design for this screen. **Touches the audit log** (a new action, `content.archived`, written in the same batch as the change, by the existing `recordAudit`). **No permission row changed:** archive uses the existing `content.publish` (Admin, Super Admin), and listing still needs `content.draft`. No fee or result rule is touched.
- **PM answers (2026-10-01):** Post becomes **News** (existing posts become News); **Event** and **Information** are added; **Routine stays** as a seventh type, because routines are in the original requirements (§6.7). An Event is a plain post, with no date of its own (unlike a Holiday, D-094). **No images**, anywhere in the flow: posts are text first.
- **Data (migration 0027).** `content_items` is rebuilt, since SQLite cannot change a CHECK in place. Every row, id and rule is kept, and the index and the holiday triggers are made again. Changes:
  - Kinds `event` and `information` are added. `post` keeps its key and reads "News", so nothing stored or linked changes.
  - `publish_time` holds Nepal time "HH:MM". Existing items get 00:00, so nothing that showed stops showing.
  - Status `archived`, with `archived_at` and `archived_by`. A CHECK keeps the stamp and the status together.
  - The migration was applied to a local database holding real rows; they came through unchanged.
- **Scheduling stays derived, not a job.** An item shows from its publish day and time (Nepal) to the end of its hide-after day. This is worked out in the query on every request: the public list, the Worker's copy of `/notices`, and the Admin list's state all compare against `nepalMinute(now)`. A scheduled item appears, and a finished one goes, without a job having to run (D-039's design, now to the minute). Publishing answers with `state`, so the screen says "Published" or "Scheduled". The public answer is still cached for up to a minute (D-039).
- **Archive.** It takes a draft or a live item off the website at once and keeps it. A waiting item is the approval's to settle first. "Move to drafts" (the existing unpublish route, now also from archived) brings it back. Publishing straight from the archive is refused (409 `archived`). A Co-ordinator may no longer edit an archived item (they could only edit drafts and waiting items before; an archived item was once live). The **Archived** filter shows archived and expired items, since both are no longer shown. Nothing is deleted.
- **The list (`GET /api/content`).** Filters by kind, by group (Published = on the website now; Draft = draft or waiting for approval; Scheduled; Archived = archived or past hide-after) and by urgency. A search covers the title, the text and the author's name; `%` and `_` are taken literally, and the build team is neither shown nor found by name, appearing as "Support". Results come in pages of 10, at most 50. The same answer carries:
  - the total;
  - the four figures, counted over everything whatever the filters (Published, Drafts, Scheduled, and Urgent meaning urgent and on the website or scheduled);
  - the public website's address (`SITE_ORIGIN`), whether this is the real deployment (`ENVIRONMENT=production` reads "Live"; anything else reads "Test site", so a test copy never claims to be the school's website), and when anything was last published;
  - today in BS and the time now, for a new item;
  - each item's author name and a 200-character excerpt.

  All of it is one database round trip (one `batch()`). The old `limit` still works, as the page size.
- **Text formatting, safely.** The brief asked for a rich-text editor with server-side HTML sanitising. Built instead: text stays plain, with a few marks that are read into a small tree and drawn only as React elements (app) or escaped HTML (the Worker's crawler copy). There is no HTML to sanitise, so nothing typed can ever run. The marks are a blank line for a paragraph, `## ` for a heading, `- ` and `1. ` for lists, and `**bold**`, `*italic*`, `__underline__` and `[words](https://…)`. A link goes only to http(s), mailto: or tel:. The toolbar buttons (Paragraph or Heading, B, I, U, two lists, Link; Ctrl or Cmd with B, I or U) write these marks. The app and the Worker cannot share a file, so both readers are held to one fixture (`apps/api/test/fixtures/text-format.json`), and a web test compares the two outputs.
- **Screens (after the reference).**
  - The header, with "+ New content" as the one prominent button.
  - Four figure cards and the Public website card (status, address, last updated, View website).
  - Type and status filters as segmented buttons, and a server-side search that waits until typing stops.
  - The table: title and excerpt with the kind's icon, a type chip, Urgent, a status with "On website", "Will be published" or "Not visible", the BS date and time, the author with "x ago", View (items on the website) or Edit, and a ⋮ menu (Edit, Publish, Take down, Archive, Move to drafts, Send for approval for a Co-ordinator). Publish and Take down keep their Undo (D-048). A menu of one entry is drawn as a button instead (D-030).
  - Paging ("Showing 1–10 of 12 items").
  - Placeholder shapes while loading, the two empty states, and the load error with Try again.
  - New content and Edit open in the browser's own modal dialog. Inside:
    - seven type cards (radios; locked after saving);
    - Title, and Content with the toolbar;
    - the contact (vacancy) and the holiday days (holiday);
    - Mark as urgent;
    - the publish date (BS day, month, year) and time;
    - Hide after;
    - Cancel, Save draft and Publish, with the live preview beside them.

    The preview is drawn by the public board's own component, with the same date line. If publishing fails, everything typed stays in the form. On a narrower screen each row becomes a card.
  - The dashboard's Publish Post now opens the pop-up (`/portal/content?new=post`). The old edit page still works.
- **Reference vs built, decided by the rules.**
  - Dates are BS with a time. The reference's second AD line is left out (CLAUDE.md section 6). The public website card's "Last updated" is AD with time, as the dashboard's card already is (D-089).
  - The date is entered as day, month and year boxes, not the reference's calendar pop-up: the web app does no BS conversion (D-014).
  - News and Information keep the brand blue on their icon only, not on their label (D-030).
  - No header search and no bell: the PM left these out (D-089, D-091).
  - No "Manage" link on the website card: there is no website-settings screen to link to.
- **Build setting (affects every page, PM to note).** The new screen's stylesheet pushed `/sign-in` 1.2 KB over its 210 KB budget. The default CSS chunking merged every route's CSS into one shared file, so portal styles rode along on public pages (`main`: 208.2 KB). `next.config.ts` now sets `experimental.cssChunking: "graph"` for the export build. This is Turbopack's documented strategy for that problem. Every public page is now within budget; sign-in is at 208.1 KB, slightly under `main`. Pages were checked by eye afterwards: home, Notices, sign-in, the dashboard, People and Website Content. The budget is unchanged.
- **Tested.**
  - API, `content-redesign.test.ts` (28): Event and Information, and the database refusing an unknown kind; the publish time to the minute, Nepal midnight, the state matching the public list, a bad time refused by the service and by the database, edits audited; archive (live and draft, a repeat changes nothing, waiting refused, publisher only, Co-ordinator cannot edit it, not publishable until moved to drafts, the stamp tied to the status, the audit chain still whole); search (title, text, author, literal `%` and `_`, Support not found by name); the four groups; type, group and urgency combined; paging and its order; the four figures; the author shown as Support; the routes (shape, bad filters 400, who may list and archive, publish answers with its state).
  - API, `text-format.test.ts` (17) and updated content tests. Web: `text-format.test.tsx` (19: the shared fixture, agreement with the Worker, the same markup, no script or element from typed text), `text-edit.test.ts` (10: every toolbar button), and updated model, client, outcome and screen tests (seven type cards, the toolbar, labels, time, the preview's marks, no image anywhere, the list's filters and figures). A guard keeps button labels wrapping on this page.
  - In a browser on the local copy, as the Principal, with sample content loaded through the API: the list, the menu, the pop-up (bold and urgent shown live in the preview), search, a phone at 375 px, and 320 px with text at 200% (no sideways scroll on the page or in the pop-up). No console errors.
  - Totals: API 95 files / 1,592 tests, web 52 / 637, plus lint, typechecks, build, page weight, BOM and boundaries, all clean.
- **Not done.**
  - No `apple-design` review beyond the house rules and the 320 px / 200% check, as for D-089: the PM's reference is the design.
  - The dashboard's quick action still says "Publish Post".
  - A "View" link goes to the public Notices page, since a post has no page of its own (D-039).
  - Two people editing one item at once: the last save wins (as before, D-041).

**D-099 People & Access: the Principal's access-control centre, redesigned to the PM's reference.** 2026-10-02, at the PM's request (a reference image and a written brief: "REFERENCE IMAGE = visual source of truth. EXISTING CODEBASE = technical source of truth. DATABASE = data source of truth."). As in D-089 and D-098, the PM's reference is the design for this screen. **Touches permissions:** a new matrix row, `accounts.staff.access` ("Change which sections a Co-ordinator's or Accountant's access reaches"), for Admin and Super Admin only, with a hand-written rule in `permission-matrix.test.ts`; `docs/permission-matrix.md` is regenerated. **Touches the audit log:** a new action, `accounts.access.changed`, with the scope before and after, written in the same batch as the change. No fee or result rule is touched.
- **The model, unchanged, and used as it is.**
  - The hierarchy is the one already enforced server-side (D-059): the Principal (Admin) gives access to Co-ordinators and Accountants; a Co-ordinator adds and manages teachers; a teacher reaches only their assigned classes.
  - No new user, role or permission tables.
  - Several sections at once uses the existing model: one role row per section. The permission layer already combines them (`authorize` gathers every section claim), and so do the SQL guards.
  - "Permissions" are not per-person switches, because the platform has none: each role's actions are fixed in the matrix. The screens show what a role can do in the plain words of its home-page brief (D-092), never permission ids.
- **Built (API).**
  - `GET /api/people` (`accounts.staff.view`). One page of the administrative staff (`group=admin`: Co-ordinators and Accountants, never teachers or Admins) or the teaching staff (`group=teaching`).
    - Search covers name and email, taken literally.
    - Filters: role, account status, section (a whole-school person counts for every section), and for teachers their home section and a programme they teach in.
    - Pages of 10, at most 50, in one round trip.
    - The answer also carries the switched-on counts (teachers; Co-ordinators and Accountants for the Principal only) and every section.
    - Each person carries their sections (empty is the whole school), home section, account status and last sign-in as separate facts, whether the viewer may manage them (the same SQL guard the writes use), and for a teacher their subjects, programmes and who added them. "Added by" is read from the audit log's creation entry; the build team shows as Support, never by name.
    - Visibility is the same as `/api/staff`: a Co-ordinator sees only teachers, and only their own sections' if limited; an Accountant, a teacher or a student sees nobody.
  - `academics` exports three SQL fragments (`teacherSubjectsJson`, `teacherProgrammesJson`, `teachesInProgramme`), so `accounts` never names its tables. This is the same pattern as `teacherManageableBy` (D-060). Only active assignments in years that are not closed count.
  - `POST /api/staff` takes `sectionKeys` (several sections; `sectionKey` still works). Every section must exist and be switched on (D-097).
  - `PATCH /api/staff/{id}/access` (`accounts.staff.access`) sets the whole school (`[]`) or switched-on sections. It applies to a Co-ordinator or an Accountant only, never to oneself, and the actor is re-checked inside the batch. Missing rows are added switched off, then one statement switches on exactly the new scope. Nothing is deleted, a repeat records nothing, and the change is audited. It takes effect at the person's next sign-in renewal, within 30 minutes (D-021); fee, approval and publish actions re-check at once.
- **Built (web).** The Principal and Support get People & Access at `/portal/people`; a Co-ordinator keeps the existing Staff and Teaching screens unchanged.
  - The page: title and subtitle; three counts; "How access works" (two plain sentences, a "Learn more" that opens in place, and a small Principal → Co-ordinator / Accountant → Teacher picture, using the school's own role words); and two tabs (WAI-ARIA tabs, arrow keys).
  - **Staff & Access.** "Administrative staff" with search, role, status and access filters, and "+ Add a person" as the one prominent button. One card per person: initials, name and email, a role chip, an access chip, then Active or Switched off with the last sign-in under it. "Manage access" (or "Switch on" for a switched-off person) and a ⋮ menu (Switch off/on, New temporary password).
  - **Add a person** is a pop-up in four steps:
    1. Role: two cards, Co-ordinator or Accountant. There is no Teacher, and a line says teachers are added by a Co-ordinator.
    2. Name, email and phone.
    3. Access: the whole school, or selected sections.
    4. Review, with what the role can do.

    The one-time temporary password is shown once (the existing D-059 notice). A failure keeps everything typed; an email already in use goes back to step 2 with the message on the field.
  - **Manage access** shows the person, role, account, sign-in ("has not chosen their own password yet"), access now, the scope choice with Save access (enabled only after a change), what the role can do, Switch off/on and New temporary password.
  - **Teaching**, for oversight. Search, and section, programme and status filters. A table (cards on a phone) of name and email, subjects, home section and programmes, "Added by" (name and role), status and last sign-in, with a "View teaching" link to the assignments page. There is no create-teacher button, and the Principal cannot switch teachers off; their Co-ordinator does.
  - Loading shapes, both empty states, "No people found" with Clear filters, "People couldn't be loaded." with Try again.
  - The row menu moved from `content/` to `ui/RowMenu`, so both pages share it.
- **Reference vs built, decided by the rules.**
  - No header search and no bell (the PM, D-089 and D-091).
  - Role and status colours keep the brand blue off labels (D-030): Co-ordinator is the theme's accent, Accountant its green.
  - "Last signed in" is shown as "x ago", in the dashboard's words.
  - The Teaching column is "Added by", not "Assigned by": the record is who created the account; who gave each subject is in the assignments.
  - The reference's teacher ⋮ is a "View teaching" link, since a menu of one entry is not shown (D-030).
- **Tested.**
  - API `people.test.ts` (23):
    - several sections on create, the old one-section way, and refusals (unknown, switched off, twice, both ways);
    - the permission layer seeing every section;
    - changing access there and back, audited with before and after, with nothing deleted, and a repeat recording nothing;
    - who may change access: Admin and Support yes; Co-ordinators, Accountants and teachers no; never a teacher, never oneself; an Admin switched off a moment ago changes nothing and leaves no entry;
    - the route's answers (200, 401, 403, 400);
    - the two lists: who is in each, separate status and sign-in, never a password, the filters, a teacher's subjects, programme and "Added by" (Support unnamed), the Principal seeing but not managing teachers, a limited Co-ordinator's view, an Accountant and a teacher seeing nobody, paging and order, counts, and the route.

    Plus a hand-written permission rule. Web `people-access.test.tsx` (15): who gets the screen, the plain words, step checks, the page's markup (no hard-coded names or numbers, no create-teacher), Add a person (no Teacher), and Manage access (no switched-off section, no permission ids, Save waits for a change). The People page test is updated.
  - In a browser on the local copy, with sample people and teaching added through the API:
    - both tabs and both pop-ups;
    - changing Hari's access to two sections, and adding an Accountant through all four steps to the one-time password, both through the screens;
    - 320 px with text at 200% on both tabs: no sideways scroll, and labels break between words;
    - no console errors.
  - Totals: API 96 files / 1,617 tests, web 53 / 652, plus lint, typechecks, build, page weight, BOM and boundaries, all clean.
- **Not done.**
  - Sign-in times show "Never" for the sample people, who have not signed in.
  - A Co-ordinator's own screens were not redesigned.
  - Changing a person's role (Co-ordinator ↔ Accountant) is not offered: the brief did not ask for it, and today it is a new account.

**D-100 The Admin (Principal) functional user test, and the Add a Programme fix it found.** 2026-10-02, at the PM's request ("do all the operations an admin can do … a detailed screenshot in a folder … a completed admin account FUT"). No rule is changed and no fee, result, permission or audit behaviour is touched.
- **Where FUTs live.** `docs/fut/<role>/`: a `README.md` (the test, its data, a step table and a screenshot per step, the server checks, the findings, and which permission each step covers), `screens/` (the screenshots, JPEG) and `scripts/` (the Playwright scripts that ran it, so it can be run again on a fresh local school). The first is `docs/fut/admin/`: 180 steps in 12 areas and 15 server checks.
- **How it runs.** Every Principal step goes through the real screens in a browser, signed in with a password and an authenticator code. Work only another role can do (hiring teachers, marking attendance, entering marks, recording payments) is done first through the same API their screens use, signed in as those people. It never writes to the database directly (CLAUDE.md section 2).
- **Fixed (F-02, blocking).** Add a Programme always failed with "That is not allowed. Check what you entered.": the form passed its hidden grading choice (`gradingPolicy: null`) to `createProgramme`, and the API's strict body refused the unknown field. `createProgramme` now sends only `name`, `sectionKey` and `affiliation`; a regression test in `setup-client.test.ts` fails without the fix. The unit tests had not caught it because each side was tested alone: the client test called `createProgramme` with exactly the three fields, and the API test sent a correct body.
- **Not fixed: 18 findings for the PM to schedule**, listed with severity in `docs/fut/admin/README.md`. Most are a wrong or missing message on one screen. Three are larger: there is no audit-trail or sign-ins screen (F-11, against CLAUDE.md section 6); a discount request does not show its reason to the approver (F-06); and receipt numbers start with the section's generated key, such as `s7e402b08e9-2083-00007` (F-18), which touches how receipt numbers are formed (a fees rule).

**D-101 The Co-ordinator functional user test.** 2026-10-03, at the PM's request ("create an end to end FUT document with detailed screenshots in similar fashion for the coordinator role"). No code is changed, no rule is changed, and no fee, result, permission or audit behaviour is touched.
- **What it is.** `docs/fut/coordinator/`, in the same layout as the Principal's (D-100): 149 steps in 17 areas, each step with a screenshot and the messages seen on screen, and 19 server checks. Sita Sharma, a whole-school Co-ordinator, does every operation the matrix gives her through the real screens: first sign-in, the year, terminals, subjects, curriculum and elective groups, classes, teachers and teaching assignments, walk-ins, the admissions queue (approve, ask for changes, reject, a possible duplicate), search, attendance (registers read, teachers marked today and on a past day with a reason), the activity log, electives, results (send back, verify, publish, class sheet, marks card, Top 20), rechecks, website drafts sent to the Principal, her own account, phone width and 200% text. Applicants, teachers, students, the Accountant and the Principal act only as preconditions, through the API their own screens use.
- **Server checks.** Twelve actions she must not do (the dues list, creating an Accountant, changing another Co-ordinator, adding a section, publishing content or deciding an approval, marking a register, entering marks, the Principal's dashboard, the test mailbox, the Accountant's registration) all answer 403. A second Co-ordinator whose access reaches Bachelor's only gets 404 for every +2 register, sheet, electives list and publish, and search returns only Bachelor's students.
- **Not fixed: 12 findings for the PM to schedule**, listed with severity in `docs/fut/coordinator/README.md`; none blocked the test. Four are medium: a walk-in entered twice is admitted twice with no warning (F-03); the admissions queue prints a section's generated key instead of its name (F-05, as the Principal's F-18 does for receipts); no screen or route opens or corrects a student's record although the matrix grants `students.personal.correct` (F-06); and Publish on the Co-ordinator's content form leads nowhere and does not mention Send for approval (F-08). One concerns scope: the Bachelor's-only Co-ordinator's walk-in form still lists +2 levels (F-11), although every +2 request tested at the server was refused (whether the server accepts a +2 walk-in from him was not tried).

**D-102 Every admin FUT finding fixed, and the Approvals inbox redesigned.** 2026-10-03, at the PM's request ("fix all the issue found in admin fut", and the Approvals reference design). **This touches fees, permissions and the audit log**; the tests that cover each are named below. The admin FUT was run again end to end on a fresh school against the fixes (`docs/fut/admin/`).
- **Receipt numbers (F-18, a fees rule; the PM chose "a short code").** A section has a **receipt code**, 2 to 6 letters or digits, stored in capitals and unique (migration 0028). The Principal gives it when adding a section; a suggestion is made from the name (initials, a run of digits kept whole, a bracketed part left out, "+" read as P: "+2 (Grade 11–12)" gives P2, "Bachelor's" BACH). Receipt numbers are `CODE-YEAR-SEQUENCE`, such as `P2-2083-00007`. The code may change until the section has issued a receipt under it, then it is fixed (checked again inside the update, so a receipt issued meanwhile wins). A section from before codes keeps numbering with its key until it is given one; receipts already issued are never edited, and the sequence per section and year is unchanged. Receipt and ledger dates show in BS. Tests: `fees-payments.test.ts` (numbering with a code, sequence unbroken, old receipts keep their number, locked after a receipt), `sections.test.ts`, `setup-model.test.ts`, `setup-screens.test.tsx`.
- **Audit trail and Sign-ins (F-11; CLAUDE.md section 6).** `GET /api/audit/events` and `GET /api/audit/sign-ins` under the existing `audit.view` (Admin, Support), 25 to a page, filtered by area or failures and searchable; read only, with no route that changes an entry. Support shows as "Support", never by name or email. Two screens under Reports, Oversight. Tests: `audit-trail.test.ts`, `audit-screens.test.tsx`.
- **Approvals (F-03, F-06, F-07, F-13), redesigned after the PM's reference.** Cards (kind, subject, summary, requester, BS date, status in words) with filters by kind and a sort; one Review per card, and a side panel (full screen on a phone) holding the one prominent button, Approve request, behind a confirmation. The panel reads `GET /api/approvals/{id}` (new): what each of the five kinds would change, read from the subject now (fee items and yearly total; a discount's percentage, amount, reason and note; a reversal's original payment, day and receipt; a refund's credit), and `stale` as soon as the subject has changed, so Approve is never offered for it. A decision now answers `stale`, `already_decided` (someone else decided it first, or it was taken back) or `own_request`; the screen says each in plain words. **Permission change:** `approvals.view.own` (see your own requests and take one back) now includes the Admin and Support, so a Principal who sent their own request can withdraw it; nobody can still decide their own. The menu's waiting count excludes the reader's own requests and refreshes after every decision. Refund approval still records no payment method: the Accountant records it afterwards. Tests: `approvals-review.test.ts`, `approvals-service.test.ts`, `approvals-screens.test.tsx`, `approvals-kinds.test.ts`, the permission tests.
- **Smaller fixes.** F-01: a failed change on Programs is said inside its pop-up. F-04: an admission reads "Name admitted (SID)" in Recent activity and the audit trail (read from the student, since the entry is written before the ID exists); the stored line no longer carries an internal id. F-05: the chart's "100%" is no longer cut. F-08: the Principal is offered no registration form; Admissions shows them Student search, and a one-entry tab menu is not shown. F-09: a search result opens the student's record, read only. F-10: a student with nothing charged shows "Nothing charged yet", not "Paid up". F-12: a list or heading button on an empty line starts one. F-14: pages of other roles opened by address all say "You do not have access to this page." F-15: the phone tab bar is one row of equal tabs with the count on the icon; with enlarged text it shows icons only (each still named), never a word broken in the middle. F-16: the sign-in page no longer scrolls sideways at 320 px and 200% (checked again). F-17: the content status label wraps. F-19: a half-filled date asks for the day, month and year.
- **Page weight.** The public pages' shared scripts and styles budget goes from 210 KB to 215 KB: the message catalog every page carries grew with these screens' words, and the sign-in page reached 212.1 KB. The three new portal pages are listed as not public. Found by replaying CI in a fresh clone.
- **Not changed.** No rule on who decides, the approve-and-apply batches, the ledger or the audit chain changed. The Co-ordinator FUT's findings (D-101) are separate and not part of this.

**D-103 "Reading the school" (topic 7): shared read-only page patterns, and Attendance redesigned.** 2026-10-03, at the PM's request (the topic 7 reference image of nine screens and its phased order: "Phase 1 foundation, Phase 2 attendance, stop and verify"). Phases 1 and 2 only; Classwork, Fees, Results, Reports, read-only setup, Students and Oversight (phases 3 to 9) wait for the PM's review of these. No rule on who may do what changed, no permission row was added, and nothing here writes.
- **Shared patterns (`apps/web/src/read/`).** One set of pieces every "read the school" page is built from: a header with breadcrumb, title, one-line subtitle and the day in BS ("Today · 17 Ashwin 2083"); up to four figures (icon, value, label; status colours, never the brand colour, D-030); a card holding a table that becomes stacked rows on a phone, each value with its column's name; status in words; a quiet "Open" link per row; one read-only note; a two-way switch; a "Change date" pop-up with the BS day picker (a half-filled day asks for the rest, F-19; a day outside the verified calendar is refused by the server); a table-shaped loading skeleton; "Couldn't load this." with Retry; and the one answer for another role's page (F-14). The portal shell is unchanged.
- **Attendance overview.** Figures: present today (of the classes already marked), absent today, classes not marked yet, and teachers on leave (only for those who may see teacher attendance and only when that module is on). Then every class of the active year with its Class Teacher, today's state in words and "N absent"; the classes not marked yet come first; each row opens its register. A Class Teacher still gets their own register to mark first, unchanged.
- **Class register.** For anyone but its Class Teacher: today's register in words (Present, Absent, Not marked yet), "This year so far" (days present of days marked, the percentage, "Below 75%" or "On track"), Change date, and the read-only note. The Class Teacher's marking screen is unchanged.
- **Teacher attendance.** The Principal reads the day's list (status in words, reason) with Change date; the Co-ordinator's marking list is unchanged.
- **API.** The attendance class list now also names each class's Class Teacher (`classTeacher`, nullable), read in the same query (one round trip). `openapi.json` and the typed client are regenerated. Test: `apps/api/test/attendance.test.ts` "each class names its Class Teacher".
- **Not built from the reference:** a "By teacher" grouping of the overview (no data asked for it beyond what is shown), and any export from these pages. The `apple-design` and `ui-ux-pro-max` skills are not installed in this environment, so the review was against CLAUDE.md's house rules (D-030) and the reference, not the skill pages.
- **Checked in a browser** on the admin FUT school at 1440 px, 360 px and 320 px with text at 200%: no sideways scroll, the keyboard reaches every Open link, every Open link reaches its class, the Principal sees no control outside the closed Change date pop-up, and the loading, empty and failed states each show. The admin FUT's topic 7 steps were updated for Change date and re-run (192 steps).

**D-104 "Reading the school" (topic 7), phases 3 to 9: Classwork, Fees, Results, Reports, read-only setup, Students and Oversight.** 2026-10-03, at the PM's request ("go ahead with Phase 3 and all other phases"), on the shared patterns of D-103. **This touches how fees, results and the audit trail are shown, not how they are kept:** no ledger, receipt, grading, publish, recheck or audit rule changed, no permission row was added, and no write route was added. Tests: `apps/web/test/{classwork,fees,results,teaching,admissions,setup,subjects}-screens.test.tsx`, `apps/api/test/fees-dues.test.ts`, `apps/api/test/results-views.test.ts`.
- **Classwork.** Figures (subjects written of those expected, classes with every subject written, subjects with nothing yet), then every class with how far its log is in words, the incomplete first, and who has not written; each row opens the class. A class's day lists each subject in the teacher's words or "Not written yet", with Change date. Teachers' and students' views are unchanged.
- **Fees.** Money for reading drops ".00" (`formatNprShort`); typed amounts and the ledger are untouched. The finder shows this year's balance in words (from the dues list; without it the search still works). The account has four figures (charged, discount, paid, balance, the balance said as "Nothing charged yet", "Paid up" or "Credit NPR …"), a line for due, overdue and next due, the history as a timeline (newest first; a reversed payment says "Reversed"; a payment opens its receipt) and the receipts. Refunded totals are no longer a separate figure; refunds stay in the history. The receipt is a paper document with the amount largest and Print secondary. Structures and their items read as on Approvals. Dues: figures, a class filter (in the page, over one load), rows that open the account, status in words beside the amount columns, and the CSV for the class shown. The Accountant's forms are unchanged. **API:** each dues row now carries `studentId` so it can open the account (same query).
- **Results.** Changes after publishing list why each recheck was asked, the marks now, who decided, when, and why. **API:** rechecks carry `requestedOnBs` and `decidedOnBs` (worked out on the server), and a Super Admin who decides one is named "Support", never by name (CLAUDE.md section 5; this was a gap). The previous mark is not shown: the API keeps the current marks, not the old ones, and adding the history is a larger change (see Not built). Class sheets scroll inside their card with the student column kept in view; Top 20 per section as tables.
- **Reports** is a two-column directory and now also reaches Classwork, Fee accounts, Fee structures and Changes after publishing.
- **Setup, read only.** For anyone who cannot manage it: years, classes, terminals, subjects and the curriculum as tables with status in words, a path back to Reports, and "Read only. The {Co-ordinator} manages this." (the school's own word). The curriculum opens on the first level instead of an empty "Choose…", and shows each subject's credit hours, marks and elective group ("Choose 1 of: …"). Teaching reads by teacher (subjects and classes, Class Teacher of), from each class's teaching loaded side by side. The Co-ordinator's editing screens are unchanged.
- **Students.** Search lists students in a table with Active, Left or Graduated in words; the record has its status in the header, the personal details, and a quiet "Fee account" link for the roles that read fees (Admin, Accountant, Support), which the server checks again.
- **Oversight.** Audit trail and Sign-ins take the shared header, card and status words.
- **Not built:** the old mark beside the new one on a recheck (needs the mark history in the API); a "By teacher" grouping of attendance (D-103); any export beyond the existing CSVs. The `apple-design` and `ui-ux-pro-max` skills are not installed here; the review was against the reference and D-030.
- **Checked in a browser** on the admin FUT school at 1440 px, 360 px and 320 px with text at 200%: no sideways scroll on any page, one heading per page, and no control for the Principal beyond pickers, search, Change date, Print, Download CSV and the pager. The admin FUT's topic 7, oversight and phone steps were re-run with no failure (192 steps).

**D-105 Enlarged text on a phone: what looked like a shell bug, and the real layout faults it hid.** 2026-10-03, at the PM's request ("fix the shell bug"). No rule changed; layout only.
- **The "blank stretch" was not in the app.** Full-page screenshots taken with the browser's text size at 200% drew the text at normal size while the page kept its 200% height, so pages seemed to end in a long empty area with the tab bar halfway down (a fixed bar is drawn once in a full-page capture). Viewport captures, and enlarging the text with the page's own CSS, both show the page ending right after its content. The shell (sidebar, tab bar, header) is unchanged.
- **The real faults, found by measuring every portal page at 320 px with the browser's text at 200%** (overflow against the true 320 px, and every word checked for a break inside it):
  - The shared Button never wrapped its label, so "Change date", "Next" and "Change password" pushed pages sideways. Buttons now wrap between words below 24em of width and never exceed their container.
  - The phone table's hidden header row still had a width; it is now a block, so its cells cannot widen the page. Status words may wrap.
  - Overview's cards, Reports' links and People's staff rows gave their words a sliver beside rem-sized icons, arrows and padding ("S t u d e n t s" one letter per line). Below 24em the decorative icon tiles, the repeated arrows and the initials circle step aside and the padding tightens; the whole card or row is still the link. Figure tiles keep whole words. Long heading words hyphenate.
  - The audit pager's buttons wrap.
- **Allowed breaks:** an email address, a receipt number or a word with a hyphen may break across lines; nothing else does.
- **Checked:** 30 portal pages at 320 px with the browser's text at 200%: no sideways scroll and no word broken inside it, apart from the allowed cases above.

**D-106 The Co-ordinator FUT findings fixed, and the Co-ordinator's screens redesigned in the admin language.** 2026-10-03, at the PM's request ("fix all errors in co-ordinator; redesign the entire UI as done in admin level; reuse the admin components").
- **Findings (Co-ordinator FUT, `docs/fut/coordinator/`):**
  - F-01: a dialog, a field or a form no longer keeps an old error. Each opening of an Add pop-up is a fresh form, and a field's message goes as soon as that field changes. After "Ask for changes" or "Reject" goes through, the review closes into what happened.
  - F-02: a year whose last day is not after its first is refused on the form, in words.
  - F-03: a walk-in who matches an existing student or application (same phone, or the same name and date of birth) is not admitted. The server answers 409 with the matches, and nothing is written. The screen names them; "Admit anyway" sends `confirmDuplicate` for real twins or siblings sharing a phone.
  - F-04: an admitted walk-in shows the new student ID beside the temporary password.
  - F-05: the queue and review show the section's name, never its key.
  - F-06: a Co-ordinator can correct a student's personal details, with a reason, through `PATCH /api/students/{id}` (action `students.personal.correct`, already in the matrix: Co-ordinator `inst`, Support). It is limited to the person's sections (another section answers 404) and audited as `students.personal.corrected` with before, after and reason. The student ID is never editable.
  - F-07: the review board opens on the latest terminal with any marks, and "missing" counts students, not mark rows.
  - F-08: a Co-ordinator's website form saves and sends for approval in one step, "Send for approval", because she cannot publish.
  - F-10: Reports is in her menu, with only the groups she may read.
  - F-11: the walk-in level list follows her sections.
  - F-09 and F-12 were checked in the browser sweep.
- **Redesign, built from the Principal's read patterns (D-103, D-104) and one new shared piece:**
  - **`read/SidePanel`:** a modal drawer at the side of a wide screen and over the whole of a phone. It is used for deciding an application, managing a staff member, a curriculum subject, and a recheck.
  - **Home:** a greeting shown at once, then four figures (applications waiting, registers marked, subjects to verify, setup steps done) and today's work as rows with status in words. The setup checklist puts what is left first and becomes one line when everything is done. "What you can do" follows. It replaces the three stacked cards.
  - **Admissions:**
    - the queue has figures and cards, and each card opens a review panel where Approve is the one prominent action;
    - the walk-in form sits in one card.
  - **Staff:** figures, a search, an Everyone / Active / Switched off filter, a card per person, and Manage opening a panel with Switch off or on and New temporary password.
  - **Teaching:** year and class side by side, with the first class open straight away. Figures, the Class Teacher in its own card, and each subject with its picker.
  - **Setup:** years, terms, subjects and classes are the Principal's read tables with one more column, "Change", for the Co-ordinator's controls.
  - **Curriculum:** the first level opens. Subjects are in a table, and Edit opens the subject's panel (elective group, mark components, Switch off). Elective groups are in their own card, and "Add a subject to this Level" sits in the header.
  - **Teacher attendance:** live figures, and Present / Absent / On leave as one tap each, instead of a column of dropdowns.
  - **Results:**
