    - the review board has figures and each class in its own card, with a table, status in words and Publish, plus one "Verify selected" bar (it stays in view only where the menu is a sidebar, because the phone's tab bar owns the bottom edge);
    - a review sheet has the shared header with a breadcrumb and a decision card;
    - rechecks have figures, and each is decided in a panel;
    - electives have figures and the first class open.
- **F-09, found still open on the re-run and fixed:** a content row's actions wrap, the second under the first, inside the table.
- **Phone with text at 200%, checked on every Co-ordinator page at 320 px.**
  - The sweep found search fields and pickers with a rem-sized flex size wider than the screen, and "Open the class sheet" in a link that never wrapped. They now shrink, and a link with its own words wraps.
  - It also checked two pages the Principal shares: Programmes and Website Content. Their icon tiles and nested padding left words a sliver of the width. Below 24em the tiles step aside, the padding tightens and the programme names step down one size.
  - Result: 27 pages and three side panels (staff, curriculum subject, application review) with no sideways scroll and no word broken. The recheck panel uses the same shared panel and was not checked at that size.
- **Kept:** one prominent button per view; status in words in the theme's status colours; the brand colour only for actions; theme tokens and the message catalog only. Every new component has a markup test.
- **Page weight.** The public pages' shared scripts and styles budget goes from 215 KB to 220 KB (as in D-102): the message catalog every page carries grew with these screens' words, and the sign-in page reached 216.4 KB. The lasting fix is in the open items below.
- **Not changed:** no ledger, grading, publish or approval rule. One new write route: the F-06 correction, permission-checked and audited, with data-level tests (another section, every other role).

**D-107 The Teacher's, Student's and Accountant's screens redesigned on the same read patterns.** 2026-10-04, at the PM's request ("do the same UI changes in all other logins, i.e. teacher, student and accountant; make all the UI in the same fashion").
- **Homes (`dashboard/RoleDashboards.tsx`), after the Co-ordinator's (D-106):** a greeting shown at once, up to four figures, today's work as rows with the state in words and the page to act on it, then "What you can do". Every figure and row comes from an API the role already uses on its own screen.
  - **Teacher:** their class's register (Class Teachers only), the activity log written today, homework waiting for review, and mark sheets still in draft.
  - **Student:** attendance this year, homework to hand in (with the next due day), fees due now, and the latest published result; their record as facts.
  - **Accountant:** deposits to check, students overdue, what is due now, and fee structures waiting for approval.
  - A row that only informs (the latest activity log, a published result, fees due but not yet overdue) has no state word. Someone with two roles gets the first home and, under it, what the other role can do. A Co-ordinator who also teaches sees "Your teaching today" under her school day. The old Roles card and the stacked Today cards are gone.
- **Teacher:**
  - **Register:** figures that follow the choices, and Present / Absent side by side for each student, like the Co-ordinator's teacher list, instead of a column of "Absent today" ticks. The save is unchanged: the same request, and saving again the same day replaces the day.
  - **Activity log:** each subject is a row with its state in words (not written first). Write or Change opens a side panel with the text box.
  - **Notes:** "Share" in the header opens a pop-up; shared notes are rows with Withdraw.
  - **Homework:** "Set homework" in the header opens a pop-up; figures; the work set is listed with what needs the teacher first. An assignment has figures, its instructions, and every student's answer as a row; Review opens a side panel with the answer and the review (or the resubmission decision).
  - **Marks:** one terminal at a time (Segments), figures, and a table with status in words and "Enter marks" or "Open"; the mark sheet has the shared header, breadcrumb and status word.
  - **Own attendance:** figures and every day of the BS month with its status in words; Previous and Next month in the header.
- **Student:**
  - **Attendance:** `/portal/attendance/mine` now serves students. Their "See your attendance" line led to "You do not have access to this page" (found in this pass). The page shows the year's figures, a word when below the threshold, and the days absent.
  - **Activity log, notes, homework:** the shared header and panels. Notes keep the watermark and the soft protection (source 6.1), said once as a quiet note. Homework is a list with what is still to hand in first, and each piece opens in a side panel to hand in, read the feedback or ask to resubmit.
  - **Fees:** the account as before, with "Tell us about a bank deposit" in the header opening a pop-up.
  - **Results:** one terminal at a time, figures (GPA or percentage, the result in words, subjects passed), the subjects as a table with Passed or Not graded in words, a link to the marks card, and rechecks in their own card with "Ask for a recheck" opening a pop-up.
- **Accountant:**
  - **Find a student:** this year's totals as figures above the search.
  - **A student's account:** "Record payment" is the one prominent button and opens a pop-up; "Propose a discount" and, when there is credit, "Refund credit" are quieter pop-ups in the header; Reverse on a payment opens a side panel with the reason. Discounts, reversals and refunds asked for are rows with their state in words, and an approved refund is recorded from its row.
  - **Vouchers:** figures, each deposit a row, and Check opens a side panel with the facts, Verify (makes the receipt) and Reject (with a reason).
  - **Fee structures:** figures (live, waiting, drafts, levels with none); levels without a structure are rows with Draft. A structure has its status, Add item and Send for approval in the header, and its charges per class as rows with Make charges.
  - **Dues:** Send reminders sits in the header beside Download, and shows only when someone is overdue.
- **Settings (every role):** the shared header and panels; Change password is secondary so Save profile stays the one prominent button.
- **Shared pop-up at 320 px with text at 200%:** a long word in a pop-up's title ("Set homework") pushed the pop-up wider than the screen. Below 24em the close button now has its own line and the title takes the full width, one size smaller. This applies to every Add pop-up.
- **Checked:** every Teacher, Student and Accountant page (29) and eleven pop-ups and side panels at 320 px with text at 200%: no sideways scroll, no word broken. The Co-ordinator FUT was run again on a fresh local school as a regression check of the shared pieces.
- **Not changed:** no API, permission, ledger, grading, publish or approval rule. Every action calls the same client function with the same body as before; recording cash still holds one idempotency key per payment being entered, and a new key only after it lands.

**D-108 Code review of the whole platform: query cost, memory, and the edges left open.** 2026-10-04, at the PM's request ("do a complete code review of the entire website; make sure we have standard time and space complexity, and no open edges at the code level").
- **How:** every SQL statement the Worker prepared (307 distinct) was captured while the Co-ordinator FUT, the Teacher, Student and Accountant click-through, an end-to-end school year and every list endpoint ran on a fresh school. Each went through SQLite's query planner, and the full scans of tables that grow with the school were checked at 2,000 students. Each API module and the web app's loading were also read for correctness, races and repeated work.
- **Indexes (migration 0029):** a class's enrollments, the year's enrollments, a teacher's assignments, role holders, case-insensitive name and date of birth for the duplicate check (students and applications), the previous refresh token, a student's rechecks, failed sign-ins, and a terminal's publications. Without the class index, the classes list alone read 40 × 2,000 enrollment rows per load. The outbox sweep reads `next_attempt_at` directly, so its partial index serves it, and the migration fills the column for any old row.
- **Memory stays flat:** the audit and ledger chain checks read 500 entries at a time up to the head they read first, instead of the whole table at once. A ledger entry past the head counts as a break. The HMAC key is imported once per Worker instance, not once per entry.
- **Counts without reading everything:** the audit trail's and the sign-ins' totals use the insert-only tables' contiguous ids when unfiltered, an index range for an area, and the partial index for failures. Only a free-text search still counts by scanning.
- **Fewer round trips:** the teachers' attendance list reads role holders through the new index. Publishing finds its mark sheets by class and terminal. The elective check matches a class's own sheets. The Top 20 builds its pools without copying arrays, and looks rows up in a map. The Principal's Teaching page makes one new request, `GET /api/academics/teaching` (permission `setup.assignments.view`, section-scoped), instead of one per class: three requests instead of N + 2.
- **Edges closed (money and approvals):**
  - Two discounts for one student approved at the same moment could together exceed the charges, because each was checked only before its batch. The approve statement now re-checks, inside the batch, that the subject's version is still the one sent for approval. The second gets 409 and goes stale.
  - A discount, reversal or refund is two writes (the draft, then the engine's request). If the second failed, the draft stayed behind and, for a reversal, held the payment's one open reversal for good. The draft is now closed (audited `fees.<kind>.closed`), and a leftover reversal draft is sent when the reversal is asked again.
  - When an approval's batch changes nothing, the engine re-reads the request: a subject changed in that moment makes it stale, a person no longer an Admin gets "not allowed", and otherwise it is "already decided".
  - Two Accountants verifying one voucher at the same moment: the loser gets 409, not "not found".
  - Generating charges for a class reads once and writes in chunks of 100. It re-plans only on a duplicate, stops at once on a charge the ledger refuses, and throws after 1,000 attempts instead of silently stopping after 50 rounds with charges missing.
- **Edges closed (admissions):** a walk-in checks that its class is open and of the right level, and that the email is free, before anything is written. A refused walk-in used to leave an application behind. An approval whose email already signs someone in now says so, and points to Ask for changes.
- **Web:** a load that throws now shows the failed state instead of loading forever.
- **Accepted, not changed:**
  - Dues and the fees dashboard read the active year's ledger. That is O(entries) and comes with never storing a balance.
  - The free-text audit search scans everything.
  - Deep pages cost their offset.
  - `sessions` and `application_submission_events` grow without pruning. How long to keep them is a retention decision for the PM.
- **Tests:** `test/review-fixes.test.ts` covers the concurrent discounts, the leftover reversal draft, the concurrent voucher, more than one chunk of charges, the true totals, and the year's teaching with its scope. `test/admissions.test.ts` covers the refused walk-in leaving nothing, and the taken email for a walk-in and an approval.
- **Merged, not deployed.** Merged to `main` as PR #32 (`142153a`) after a CI replay in a fresh clone (GitHub Actions has no runners). The PM deploys it to staging from their laptop: apply migration `0029_review_indexes.sql` with `wrangler d1 migrations apply <db> --remote --config wrangler.local.jsonc`, build `apps/web`, `wrangler deploy --config wrangler.local.jsonc`, then check `/api/health`. A cloud session cannot deploy: it has no Cloudflare login, and the account and database ids live only in the PM's `wrangler.local.jsonc`.

**D-109 Academic terms of any length, set by the Principal; Phase 8 rewritten around them.** 2026-10-04, PM decision in conversation ("we are building school SAP: one school can have semesters, another years"; "what if there are 4 semesters in 1 year, as in an MBA"; "go with your recommendations"). **Replaces D-006's "yearly with terminals" default and the "semester vs year" open point. Decided, not built:** the code still has "academic years" until Phase 8 is built.
- **The academic term is the unit of time.** It is any period the Principal sets: a name, from and to dates, and the levels that run in it. Nothing assumes its length: 3 months (an MBA quarter), 6 months (a semester), 12 months (+2, Grade 9). It no longer has to match a BS year.
- **Programme and levels: the ladder, set once.** The Admin defines a programme's ordered levels: Year 1 to 4, Semester 1 to 8, Quarter 1 to 8, Grade 9. Each level also has a **usual length** in months, entered once, used only to fill in the next term's dates.
- **Who does what:**
  - **Principal (Admin):** creates, opens and closes terms. This moves from the Co-ordinator (today's `setup.structure.manage`) to the Admin. That is a permission-matrix change, made when this is built.
  - **Co-ordinator:** inside an open term, creates classes, adds subjects to levels, assigns teachers and places students, as today.
- **Several terms can be open at once.** A term names the levels it covers; the Principal picks them by section and programme, with shortcuts such as all levels or odd only. **A level can be in only one open term at a time.** For example, "CSE Odd, Shrawan–Poush" holds CSE Semesters 1, 3, 5 and 7, while "+2 2083" holds Grades 11 and 12. A mid-year intake is another term holding Semester 1, if no other open term has it. Which semesters are active is never configured: it follows from which batches exist.
- **The next term is filled in for the Principal.** It proposes the next level of each batch (1, 3, 5, 7 become 2, 4, 6, 8), dates starting the day after the last term ended, and the usual length. The Principal confirms or edits; nothing is created automatically, because real start dates slip.
- **What hangs off the term (what hangs off the year today):** classes, enrollments (one per student per term), exams, fee structures, and the lock. A closed term refuses every write, and corrections go into the current term pointing back. Unchanged: the student record and SID (the admission year is still the BS year of approval), subjects defined once and offered per level, teachers per class, the ledger and its chain, the audit log.
- **Closing a term (Principal):** refused until every class in the term has its results published for every exam in the term. Then the term is locked.
- **Promotion (Co-ordinator):**
  - The system proposes Promote for every student, into a class of the next level in the open term that holds it. The Co-ordinator changes any student to Repeat or Leaving.
  - At the last level, Graduated is offered instead, and only with zero dues (CLAUDE.md section 6).
  - Unpaid dues are carried into the new term as `carried_dues`.
- **Receipt numbering:** a gapless sequence per section per term (was per section per year).
- **Names on screen:** "Academic term" for the period, "Exam" for what is now called a terminal. Code names may follow when Phase 8 is built.
- **Tests when built:** both patterns run every flow in CI. A semester programme is added to the test fixtures beside the yearly ones, and the second-school pack stays yearly.
- **Not decided here:** CGPA across terms (a new grading output; not in scope until asked), and how a term's fees are billed beyond today's frequencies, which will use the term's dates instead of the year's.

**D-110 Academic terms built: the Principal's terms, closing, the next term, and moving students on (Phase 8, first part).** 2026-10-04, at the PM's request ("make sure you build this; make all necessary changes at all logins; follow the UI design of the Principal pages"). Builds D-109. **Touches permissions, fees, results and the audit log.**
- **Names.** The table stays `academic_years` and every column `academic_year_id`; a row is now an academic term. The API keeps its `/api/academics/years` routes. The screens say "Academic term". The school's word for an exam now defaults to "Exam" (was "Terminal"); a pack can still rename it.
- **Migration 0030.**
  - `academic_years` is rebuilt, because D1 cannot drop a UNIQUE constraint. The rows are copied aside, the table dropped and recreated under the same name, and the rows put back with foreign keys deferred. Nothing is renamed, so other tables' triggers keep working. Re-inserting the parent rows settles the deferred foreign-key check (a rename does not; tested in SQLite 3.45 and on a seeded local D1).
  - Several terms can be open at once: the one-active index is gone.
  - `bs_year` (the BS year the term starts in) is no longer unique.
  - A unique receipt `code` is added. Existing years get their BS year, so their receipt numbers do not change.
  - New `term_levels`. Triggers enforce that a level is in only one open term, that a closed term's levels never change, and that a level with classes in the term cannot be taken out.
  - A class may only be made for a level its term runs.
  - `levels.usual_months` is added.
  - `enrollments.previous_enrollment_id` is added, unique.
  - Teacher attendance is locked for a day only when every term covering that day is closed.
- **Permissions (matrix rows).**
  - New `setup.terms.manage` (Admin, Super Admin): make, change, open and close terms.
  - New `students.promote` (Co-ordinator institution, Super Admin).
  - `setup.structure.manage` now covers classes and exams only.
  - The independent permission test lists both new rows.
- **The Principal.**
  - Terms are made with a name, an optional receipt code, days in the verified calendar, and their levels.
  - Details change only while a term is a draft; levels while it is open.
  - Closing is refused until every switched-on class with active students has its results published for every exam of the term. A term with such classes and no exams is not ready either. The close statement re-checks this inside its own batch.
  - The next term is proposed, never created: the next level of each batch with students, starting the day after, for the longest usual length (or this term's length).
  - Screen: **Academic terms**, in the Principal page design (D-103), at `/portal/terms` in the Principal's menu. Figures, a table, "New term" with a level picker (a level in another open term is shown, not offered; "All" and "Odd only" shortcuts), and a side panel to open, edit or change levels, close (with the check said class by class and exam by exam) and fill in the next term. Each level's usual length is set from its options on the Programs screen.
- **The Co-ordinator.**
  - Setup's first tab reads the terms, with a note that the Principal changes them.
  - The class form offers only the chosen term's levels.
  - New tab **Move students** (`/portal/setup/promotion`): the most recently closed term with students waiting, its classes in the person's sections, each student with what they owe and a proposed move. Promote into the first class of the next level, or Graduated at the last level. Repeat and Leaving are a choice away; one button moves a class.
- **Moving students (API).**
  - A move needs the old term closed; nothing is written to it.
  - Promote and repeat make a new enrollment in an open term's class of the next or the same level, naming the old one. A second move of the same student finds it already made.
  - Leaving and Graduated set the student's status, only with nothing owed; Graduated only at the last level.
  - What the old enrollment owes is carried into the new one as one `carried_dues` ledger entry. It is idempotent: a ledger source can be used once. A repeat move finishes a carry that failed after the move.
  - `OPEN:` a credit (the school owes the student) is not carried. A refund cannot be written to a closed term, so how a credit moves on needs a decision.
- **Everywhere else.**
  - Fees: a fee structure belongs to the open term that runs its level, and can be prepared while the term is a draft. Monthly items are billed for each BS month the term spans, from the month the student joined; the first due day is the term's first day if it falls mid-month. "Yearly" now reads "Once a term", and totals are per term.
  - Receipts are numbered per section per term, with the term's code (`P2-2083-00007` for a term whose code is its BS year).
  - Admissions: approval and walk-ins enrol into the chosen class's term; the SID's year is that term's BS year.
  - Student lists use each student's latest enrollment.
  - The exam pickers name each exam with its term when more than one term is open.
  - Wording across every login says "term" where it meant the academic year, and "exam" where it said "terminal".
- **BS 2084 limits the next term.** A term's days must be inside the verified calendar (BS 2000 to 2083). Today (Ashwin 2083) a six-month term that starts now ends in Falgun 2083, and the term after it would run into BS 2084, which is refused until BS 2084 is verified. The open item to verify BS 2084 is now urgent for any semester school.
- **Tests.**
  - `academics-years.test.ts`, rewritten for terms (24): who may manage terms, codes, any length, one open term per level, several open terms, close and its check, the next-term proposal.
  - `terms-lifecycle.test.ts` (10): a semester school beside a yearly one through the real routes (billing over a term, a receipt with the term's code, close, next term, promote with carried dues once, leave, graduate refused below the last level, section scope, both chains whole).
  - Schema tests for the new triggers.
  - Every earlier test was moved to the term rules. The Co-ordinator no longer makes terms; each fixture puts its level in its term.
  - Web: terms client, model and screens; the promotion screen's choices and figures; the Setup pages.
  - A browser check of every new screen and pop-up at 1440 px and at 320 px with text at 200% found a select pushing the Move students page sideways and "Manage" breaking mid-word; both were fixed.
- **Design review.** The `apple-design` and `ui-ux-pro-max` skills are not installed in this environment, so the screens follow the house rules (D-030) and the Principal patterns (D-103) without a skill-led review:
  - one prominent button per view;
  - status said in words, in status colours;
  - 44 px controls;
  - no sideways scroll at 320 px with text at 200%;
  - the shape of the page shown while it loads.
- **Not done (Phase 8, rest).** A waive-dues flow, the +2 to Bachelor's handover, and reactivation. Also not done: CGPA across terms; billing choices beyond monthly and once a term; a credit at promotion (`OPEN:` above). The Co-ordinator FUT harness (`docs/fut/coordinator/scripts`) still makes years as the Co-ordinator and needs updating before it is run again.

**D-111 BS 2084 checked: not verified, and our converter's 2084 data is likely wrong.** 2026-10-04, at the PM's request ("do what you can to verify this"). Details in `docs/spikes/bs-2084.md`.
- **Not verified.** The network policy of this cloud environment blocks every Nepali calendar site and npns.gov.np, and the official 2084 almanac is likely not out yet. The verified range stays BS 2000 to 2083, so no term, due date or other date may fall after Chaitra 30, 2083 (13 April 2027).
- **Evidence found.**
  - Seven date packages split into two families for 2084.
  - An astronomy check (`docs/spikes/bs-year-check/check.py`) is calibrated on the 1,008 verified month starts and predicts all 168 months of BS 2070 to 2083 out of sample. It puts 8 of our pinned converter's 13 boundaries for 2084 (counting Baisakh 1, 2085) a day early.
  - Three of the converter's months would start a full day before anything seen in 84 years.
  - The other family (31 32 31 32 31 30 30 30 29 29 30 31) agrees with the astronomy.
- **Consequence.**
  - When the official calendar is published, verifying 2084 will probably need converter data other than `@inicrea/bikram-sambat-core` 0.1.3: an upgrade or a replacement.
  - Changing the converter changes D-022, so it needs PM approval when it comes.
  - We still never write conversion tables ourselves.
  - The check script is evidence for the two-person check, not a source.
- **Unblock.**
  - Either the PM allows hamropatro.com, nepalipatro.com.np and npns.gov.np in this environment's network settings,
  - or the PM sends the twelve 2084 month lengths from the published calendar.
  - Then compare them with the check, choose the converter, extend the golden test and the verified range.

**D-112 BS 2084 opened provisionally, on the calendar printed by nepcalendar.com.** 2026-10-04, the PM: "Use the calendar given for now. If anything changes we will re-implement it." Supersedes D-111's "not verified" for day-to-day use.
- **The data.**
  - 2084 runs 31 31 32 31 31 30 30 30 29 30 30 30 days, from Wednesday 14 April 2027 to Wednesday 12 April 2028. It was read from the PM's screenshot of nepcalendar.com, and the weekdays carry over correctly from month to month.
  - It matches the pinned converter (`@inicrea/bikram-sambat-core` 0.1.3) day for day, so no conversion data changes. D-022 holds and we write no calendar tables.
- **The change.**
  - `VERIFIED_BS_YEARS` now runs from 2000 to 2084, and the golden calendar has a 2084 entry (31,046 days, each converted both ways).
  - A new test pins every 2084 month's printed length and first weekday, so a converter change that disagrees with the printed calendar fails CI.
  - BS 2085 is now the first refused year.
- **Provisional.**
  - The page does not say it is the official almanac, and the astronomy check in D-111 predicts 8 of these 13 month starts a day later.
  - When the official 2084 almanac is published (by about Falgun 2083), compare it with this calendar.
  - If it differs, the dates already stored in 2084 (term ends, fee due days, holidays) must be reviewed. Then follow D-111: change the converter with PM approval, update the golden entry and the pinned test.
- **What it unblocks.** Terms, fees and every other date may now run to Chaitra 30, 2084 (12 April 2028).

**D-113 Ready for the staging deploy and main user testing (2026-10-05).** 2026-10-04, at the PM's request ("make this all ready so in the morning I can pull it and deploy"). `main` at the merge of PR #36.
- **Rehearsal of the upgrade.**
  - A local copy matching staging was built: the code before D-108, migrations to 0028, the Royal Softech pack, sections, a +2 programme, the UAT starter set and a cash payment (receipt P2-2083-00001).
  - It was then upgraded the way the PM will: `main`, `provision` (migrations 0029 and 0030 applied, about 7 s locally), new Worker.
  - Afterwards:
    - the year became an open term, code 2083, with both levels, 2 classes and 12 students;
    - the next receipt was P2-2083-00002;
    - a repeated payment with the same key was not charged twice;
    - the fee account balanced (30,500 charged, 7,500 paid);
    - the close check listed the missing results class by class;
    - the next-term proposal gave Grade 12 for BS 2084;
    - Move students was empty, as it should be with no closed term;
    - fee structures read;
    - the audit chain (105 entries) and the ledger chain (110) both verified.
- **Deploy steps.** `README.md` has a new "Updating an existing deployment" section:
  - pull and build;
  - note a D1 Time Travel bookmark;
  - list the new migrations, run `provision`;
  - `wrangler deploy`;
  - how to roll back.
- **Testers' guide.** `docs/uat-guide.md` now covers:
  - the term, Exam wording, Academic terms for the Admin and Move students for the Co-ordinator;
  - that closing a term is final;
  - what is not built, and the provisional 2084 calendar.
- **Not done.** The Co-ordinator FUT harness (`docs/fut/coordinator/scripts`) still makes years as the Co-ordinator (D-110). It is not needed for UAT.

**D-114 The PM's FUT of 2026-10-05: wings, courses, level lengths, terms, classes, subjects and admissions.** 2026-10-05, from the PM's functional user test of staging (15 points), the design they approved in conversation (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-fut-15-points-design.md`) and its plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-05-fut-15-points.md`). Built points 1, 2, 4 to 9, 11, 13, 14 and 15. **Touches permissions (data scope of subjects), but no row of the matrix changes. Fees, results and the audit chain are untouched.**
- **Words (points 1, 8).** The built-in words are now **Wing** (`term.section`, was "Section"), **Course** (`term.programme`, was "Programme") and a new **Section** (`term.classSection`) for a class's division (A, B, Morning), which replaces "Label". A school may still rename each. Internal names (tables, columns, API fields) do not change. Messages now fill in the school's words for `{section}`, `{programme}`, `{level}`, `{classSection}` and `{terminal}` when a screen does not pass them (`setSchoolWords`, set by the config provider), so a raw placeholder never shows. The nav entry for the Principal reads "Academic Structure".
- **Level length (point 2).** `levels.usual_months` (1 to 60, from D-110) is now required when a level is added and can be changed but not cleared. Existing levels show "Length not set" until the Admin sets one.
- **A term takes only levels of its own length (point 5).** The rule chosen with the PM: the term's length, in whole BS months rounded (`termLengthMonths`: months between the start and end, plus the days left over as a fraction of 30), must equal the level's length. Checked in the service (create; update for the levels being added, and every level when a draft's days change) and again in the SQL that adds a level (`AND lv.usual_months = ?`), so a length changed between the check and the write cannot slip a misfit in. That second check only matters in a race and is not tested by a race. A level already in an open term whose length no longer matches is **flagged** on the Academic terms page ("Check the length of ..."), never blocked or dropped.
- **Create a term (points 4, 6).** The form asks for the days first, then the wing, then shows each course's levels that are free (no other open term runs them, D-110) and of the term's length. It no longer lists taken levels as disabled: it hides them and says how many it hid and why.
- **Shared Wing › Course › Level picker (points 4, 9, 14).** `setup/structure-picker.ts` and `StructurePicker`; a step with one choice is stated in its place ("Wing: +2"), not offered as a menu of one (D-030).
- **Classes (points 7, 7b).** Edit, Switch off and Delete sit in one row (they wrap only on a narrow screen or with enlarged text). Edit changes only the class's section; its term and level stay (the PM's choice). Shared pop-ups now start their own text, so one opened from a table's right-aligned column is not right-aligned.
- **Curriculum (point 9).** It stays one per level (the PM agreed), and shows by default only the levels an open term runs, with a "Show all Levels" switch for preparing ahead.
- **Subjects belong to a wing (point 11).** Migration `0031` rebuilds `subjects` with `section_id`; a name, and a code when there is one, are unique within a wing. Existing subjects were sorted from their curriculum: used in one wing, that wing; used in several, kept in the lowest wing id with a copy made for each other wing and that wing's offerings moved to the copy (marks, teaching and results follow their offering); unused, left without a wing ("No Wing yet") until a whole-school Co-ordinator chooses one, which then never changes. A level takes only its own wing's subjects. A wing's Co-ordinator lists and adds only their wing's subjects; renaming and archiving still need a whole-school Co-ordinator (D-058). The sorting block of the migration acts only on subjects with no wing, so it is safe to repeat; its test runs it again on prepared rows.
- **Teaching is a Setup step (point 13),** after Curriculum (`/portal/setup/teaching`). Links that pointed to `/portal/people/teaching` now point there; the old address still works.
- **Admissions (points 14, 15).** "Applying for" asks for the wing, then the course, then the level (the open-level list now carries `programmeId`). The class picker looked only in the first open term, so with several open a class in another term was missed ("No open class of this level yet"); it now looks in the open term that runs the level, which is unique. The Principal's Teaching page read only the first open term too; it now reads every open term.
- **Tested.** API: 1,697 tests, including the length rule (counting, rounding, misfit refused with nothing written, missing length, flagged-not-blocked, a draft's days), required level length, subjects in wings (create, conflicts per wing, scope, giving an old subject its wing, listing, offering across wings), the migration's sorting (one wing, several, unused, repeated), and the class edit. Web: 765 tests (picker steps, term level list, class row and Edit, subject wing column and pop-up, curriculum filter, admissions steps). Broken on purpose: the term length check (3 tests failed), and five subject rules (each caught by a named test). Checked in the browser on a local copy with real data after the migration: every changed screen at desktop, and at 320 px with text at 200% (no sideways scroll, nothing clipped). Apple-design review: `layout.md › Visual hierarchy` (alignment, grouping), `entering-data.md › Best practices` (choices over typing; dynamic validation), `writing.md › Best practices` (errors next to the problem; consistent language), `lists-and-tables.md › Content`, `modality.md › Best practices`, `accessibility.md › Mobility`, `typography.md › Supporting Dynamic Type`, `color.md › Inclusive color`. Fixed from it: the no-wing status said the same instruction as its button; now it states the state. Left: the school's words keep their capital mid-sentence ("13 Levels are not offered"), as everywhere else in the product; a lower-case form per word is a later catalog change.
- **Not done.** Points 3 (grading), 10 (credit hours and mark components may move to an exam pattern) and 12 (electives, theory and practical): waiting for a design session with real mark sheets. Point 16 (admit a student without a class, place them later): noted, not built. The UAT seed script now makes subjects in the programme's wing; on a site whose levels have no length it stops at the term until the Admin sets them.
- **After deploying (PM).** The Admin sets every level's length on the Academic Structure page; until then those levels cannot join a new term. A whole-school Co-ordinator gives each "No Wing yet" subject its wing.

**D-115 FUT points 17 and 18: editing a subject, and a student's own class.** 2026-10-05, at the PM's request ("build points 17 and 18 now"), as agreed in conversation.
- **Point 17, Subjects › Edit.** A whole-school Co-ordinator edits a subject's name, code and wing in one pop-up (it replaces "Choose Wing"). The wing changes only while no level's curriculum uses the subject (the PM: "allow to change the wing as well if no further operation are done"): marks, teaching and results all hang off a curriculum entry, so an unused subject has nothing attached. The service refuses a move for a used subject, and the update itself carries `NOT EXISTS (offerings)`, so a curriculum added at the same moment still wins. The list says `inCurriculum` per subject, and the pop-up locks the wing with the reason.
- **Point 18, My class.** `GET /api/students/me/class` (permission `students.personal.view`, a student's own) gives the student's class in their newest open term: wing, course, level, section, the term, the Class Teacher, each subject with its teacher (an elective only if they take it), and elective groups they have not chosen from yet. There is no id in the address, so no other class can be asked for. It is a panel on the student's home, not a new menu entry: a student already has the five tabs a phone shows. Teachers appear by name only.
- **Tested.** API 1,702 (new: moving a used subject refused with nothing recorded, an unused one moves, the list flag; the student's own class, an elective taken and one not chosen, a student with no class, staff, a teacher refused, signed out). Web 767. Broken on purpose: the wing guard (service and SQL together) and the own-student filter; each failed a named test. The Edit pop-up was checked in the browser on the local copy.

**D-116 FUT point 19: a class as one page.** 2026-10-05, the PM: "let's treat class as 1 major container"; "every class they teach they can see that in their id"; "subject teacher cannot see the attendance"; "a class teacher can see all subject marks" (published only, the PM's choice); "keep classwork limited to own subjects". **Touches permissions: two new read rows; nothing that writes changes.**
- **Who opens which class.** A new "Classes" menu entry (Teacher, Co-ordinator, Admin, Support). The Principal and a whole-school Co-ordinator open every class of an open term, a wing's Co-ordinator their wing's, and a teacher the classes they teach a subject in and the class they lead. Another class is 404, the same as a missing one. New action `classes.view` { TEA: assigned, COO: inst, ADM: read, SUP: all }; reads `GET /api/classes` and `GET /api/classes/{id}`.
- **Inside a class.** Tabs: Students (roll, name; the SID and the record link only for those who see the whole class), Attendance (the Class Teacher, the Co-ordinator and the Principal; never a subject teacher), Classwork (staff, and a teacher who teaches a subject there; a teacher's classwork stays their own subjects, a Class Teacher's too), Results. The Attendance and Classwork tabs are the existing screens in an embedded form (a second-level heading, no breadcrumbs of their own).
- **Results.** Those who see the whole class read each published exam's sheet, every subject; a Class Teacher only their own class (new action `results.class_sheet.view` { TEA: class, COO: inst, ADM: read, SUP: all }, now on the class-sheet route instead of `results.view`; a student asking is refused, 403, where it was 404). The CSV export stays with the reports permission, so a Class Teacher reads but does not download. A subject teacher opens the marks of their own subjects, exam by exam.
- **On a phone** the Classes entry takes a tab; Classwork moves into More for the Co-ordinator and the Principal (it is also inside each class).
- **Tested.** API 1,714 (new: who lists and opens which class, data-level for a stranger teacher and the other wing's Co-ordinator, names only for a subject teacher, the Class Teacher's own sheet and not another class's, a student refused; independent permission rules for both actions). Web 776+. Broken on purpose: the Class Teacher filter on the sheet and a teacher's reach in the class list; each failed a named test. Browser: a local Co-ordinator and a local subject teacher, every tab, and 320 px with text at 200%.
- **Not done.** Students and parents do not get this page (they have My class, D-115).
**D-117 Exam pattern: one exam per term, out of 100, replaces the Phase 7 grading policies.** 2026-10-06 (numbered D-117 because the PM's FUT session of 2026-10-05 had taken D-114 to D-116 on another branch; its migration took `0031`, so this one is `0032`), decided with the PM in conversation ("I want to rebuild the entire exam system"), answers given one at a time. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-06-exam-pattern-design.md`. **Supersedes D-079's per-programme grading policy, NEB credit GPA and division**, and the free per-subject components of D-079 and D-080. **Touches results, permissions (wording only) and the audit log.** Approved and built the same day (PM: "Yes completely remove the old system. And start with the build"); see "Built" below.
- **One exam pattern per term, 1 to 1, out of 100.** Every class in the term follows it; a class that needs a different exam goes in a different term, and the term page says so (PM).
- **The Co-ordinator creates it** for a term the Principal has created (PM).
- **Questions when it is created:** Grade system Yes / No; the terminals and their weights, adding up to 100 (for example 30, 30, 40); the minimum % for theory and for practical; the grade ranges when Grade = Yes (PM).
- **No credit system for now** (PM: "we should not focus on that"). **No grade points or GPA for now** (PM). Both `OPEN:`, to be added later if needed. Without credits a GPA could not match NEB's credit-weighted one anyway.
- **Subject:** full marks of the paper, and a "has practical" tick with its own split (75/25, 70/30, 50/50) (PM chose the split on the subject, not the terminal).
- **Terminal:** Practical Yes / No. No: every paper is theory only. Yes: ticked subjects get a practical column. The practical is not fixed to the last terminal (PM).
- **Marks:** the teacher enters marks out of the paper's full marks; the system scales them to the terminal's weight (PM). Kept to the hundredth, rounded only at the end.
- **Pass or fail is decided on the final result only** (PM). Theory and practical are added for the score but each must reach its minimum, the practical counting only the terminals where it was held (PM). **A student must pass every subject** (PM).
- **Grade = No:** percentage and Pass / Fail, no division (PM). **Grade = Yes:** letter grades from the ranges; a failed subject is **NG** and so is the overall grade (PM, on the recommendation).
- **Publishing:** each terminal per class, as today; the **final result is published automatically** when the last terminal is (PM).
- **Top 20 ranks the final result only** (PM). Ties share a rank, as before.
- **Build now, during UAT** (PM). Results test data on staging made under the old design is cleared.
- **Stated defaults (`OPEN:`):** an absence counts 0 and shows AB; the pattern locks at the first mark; no "copy from last term" yet.
- **Effect on the NEB open point:** grade ranges become school-entered data rather than a coded scale, so D-079's unverified scale is no longer in the code. The school must still enter the official figures before a real +2 result is published.

- **Built (2026-10-06).**
  - **Migration `0032_exam_pattern.sql`, not backward compatible on purpose** (the PM: remove the old system completely; results so far are UAT test data). It drops `mark_components`, `marks`, `mark_sheets`, `rechecks`, `result_publications`, `marks_cards` and `programmes.grading_policy`, and makes them again: `exam_patterns` (one per term: graded, theory and practical minimum %, grade ranges as JSON); `terminals.weight` and `has_practical`; `subject_offerings.full_marks_hundredths` (default 100) and `practical_hundredths` (null: no practical); `mark_sheets` copy the paper's theory and practical maxima when made; `marks` are one per student, sheet and part (`theory` or `practical`); `result_publications.terminal_id` is null for the final (one per class, its own unique index) and keeps the pattern as published; `marks_cards.passed` is null on a terminal's card. Triggers: no sheet without a pattern; the pattern, a terminal's weight, practical and place, and deleting a terminal are refused once any sheet exists in the term; a practical below the full marks; no practical mark where the sheet has none; the earlier no-delete, closed-term and snapshot rules kept.
  - **Calculation** (`modules/results/grading.ts`, pure, tests first): exact fractions in `bigint`, rounded half up only at the end; `terminalResult` (scaled, for information) and `finalResult` (theory % and practical % each over the terminals that held it, every subject must pass, NG when graded and failed); `validatePattern` (weights add to 100, 1 to 12 terminals, names once, grades highest first and once, the lowest grade at or below both minimums so every pass gets a letter).
  - **The pattern** (`academics/exam-pattern.ts`): `GET` and `PUT /api/academics/years/{id}/exam-pattern`, the whole-school Co-ordinator or the Super Admin (`setup.structure.manage`), one batch with its audit entry, each statement conditioned on the actor, the term not closed and no marks yet; 409 `locked`. The old create and rename terminal routes and the component routes are gone; a subject's paper is set on the offering (`POST` and `PATCH /api/academics/offerings`). The setup checklist's "terminals" item now means the open term has its pattern.
  - **Results:** the grid shows the paper's parts out of the paper's own marks; publishing a terminal is one batch as before, and when it is the class's last terminal the final publication and its cards go in the same batch; a recheck corrects marks and adds the next version of the terminal's card and, when the final is out, of the final's; class sheets for each terminal (for information, no rank) and for the final (rank, pass or fail) with CSV; the Top 20 on the final only, per open term, section and level; the dashboard counts final results.
  - **Web:** Setup, Exam pattern (was Terminals): the questions, the exams in order with weight and practical, a running total, grade ranges when graded, Save; read-only once locked or for others. Curriculum: full marks, "This subject has a practical" and its share, on adding a subject and in its panel. The Programmes screen lost its grading pop-up. The Academic terms page and its New term form say every class in a term follows one exam pattern. Results screens show each exam's card (for information) and the final card; theory and practical are named from the catalogue.
  - **Starter set and packs:** the UAT seed makes a graded pattern (30, 30, 40; practical in the last two) with the widely republished NEB-style ranges as a stand-in (`OPEN:`), and Physics and Chemistry with a 75/25 practical.
- **Tested.** API 1,703 tests in 100 files, all passing: `results-grading.test.ts` (30, three property-based), the pattern (8) and the subject's paper (4 + 2 schema) through the service and the database, the grid, review, publish and the automatic final through the real API (`results-marks.test.ts`, 30), views, rechecks and the Top 20 (`results-views.test.ts`, 22), the Phase 7 exit test and the school-year test for both packs (graded and percentage) with the figures worked out by hand, the term close, permissions and the checklist. Web 741 tests, typecheck, lint, the static build and the page-weight budget pass. After merging the FUT 15 points branch (D-114 to D-116): API 1,738 tests in 103 files and web 786, all passing. The shared message catalog then put the sign-in and apply pages 0.5 KB over their 220 KB scripts-and-styles budget, so the budget is now 225 KB, as in D-102 and D-106 (splitting the catalog stays open for the PM).
- **Browser check** (house rule D-030): the app run locally from the Royal Softech pack with a Co-ordinator; the pattern filled in and saved through the real screen; Exam pattern, Curriculum (and a subject's panel) and Academic terms at 320 px, and at 320 px with text at 200%: no sideways scroll, no script errors. **Found and fixed:** in a row of fields, `.inline input { width: 100% }` stretched the "Practical in this exam" checkbox and squeezed its label to a letter a line; checkboxes are now left out of that rule and get a line of their own.
- **Design review: not done with the `apple-design` skill.** Neither it nor `ui-ux-pro-max` is installed in this cloud session (`.claude/skills/` is absent); `CLAUDE.md` asks the PM before UI work without them. The PM asked for the build, and the web app would not compile against the new API without these screens, so they were built from the existing components and tokens only, with D-030's house rules applied (one prominent button, Save; plain words for every state; a total for the weights as you type). The review is owed.
- **Not done, and `OPEN:`.** Credit hours and GPA (the PM: later). The official NEB grade ranges must be typed in by the school before any real +2 result is published (D-085 still stands, now as data rather than code). A student who joins after a terminal was published has that terminal counted as absent in the final (stated default). A recheck decided in the moment between the last terminal's read and its publish could leave the first final card without it (the next recheck or card version corrects it); not guarded. "Copy the pattern from the last term" is not built.

**D-118 The Students page: every student, by wing, course, level and class.** 2026-10-06, from the PM's bug report ("when we click on student it just show a field where we can enter the data") and the design they approved in conversation ("yes, go ahead"). **Touches permissions (data scope only): no row of the matrix changes. Fees, results and the audit chain are untouched; nothing writes.**
- **What the PM asked:** wing, then course ("branch"), then level, then class, with the students below; only classes that have students, and a wing or course with no students left out; filters on top; the People & Access layout.
- **The page** (Admissions, tab "Students", was "Search"; same address `/portal/admissions/search`, and the Principal's Admissions page): the counts on top (active, in open terms, left or graduated), a search (name, SID, the student's or guardian's phone), Term (open terms by default) and Status (Active by default; Left, Graduated, All), then Wing, Course, Level and Class, each shown once the one before it is chosen and each option with its count. The list shows at once, 10 a page: name and SID, class, term, guardian's phone, status, and the record. A student with no class yet is listed, in no class, only under the open terms.
- **The server:** `GET /api/students/browse` (`students.search`, as the old search), one batch: the page, the total, the wing-to-class lists (built from the same rows before the wing-to-class and search filters, so they offer only what has students), the counts and the terms. Scope follows the old search: the section of the class a student is shown in; a section-scoped Co-ordinator gets nothing from the other section. A teacher (whose reach is classes, not sections) is refused with 403; the class page already lists a teacher's students. `GET /api/students?q=` stays for the Fees search.
- **An open term is one that is not closed** (draft or active), as on the class page (D-116). A student's place with no term chosen is their most recent enrollment, shown only if its term is open.
- **Shared parts:** the search box, filter, loading rows and pager moved from People & Access into `apps/web/src/people/ListParts.tsx`; both screens use them. The old Student search screen is gone.
- **Fixed on the way (People & Access too):** below 64rem the table's name cell kept its wide-screen 28% width, so at 320 px with text at 200% a name broke a letter a line. It now takes the whole row.
- **Tested.** API: `test/students-browse.test.ts` (16): the default list, the no-class student last, paging, the counts, the lists leaving out empty courses and wings, each filter, search (a typed `%` is a plain character), status, a closed term, a draft term as open, malformed filters (400), the whole school for the Principal, Co-ordinator and Accountant, a +2 Co-ordinator getting nothing from Bachelor's, a teacher and a student refused, signed out 401. Broken on purpose: the scope (1 test failed), the open-term rule (7 failed), the teacher refusal (1 failed). Web: `test/students-screen.test.tsx` (5: the filter steps, counts, clearing what is under a changed choice, the row) and the updated admissions tests. Browser, locally with sample students: wing to class narrowing with counts; 320 px at normal and 200% text (no sideways scroll after the fix above).
- **Design review (`apple-design`):** `lists-and-tables.md › Content` (descriptive column headings; succinct rows), `pop-up-buttons.md › Best practices` (a useful default selection: open terms, Active; a label before each), `search-fields.md › Best practices` (search starts as you type; filtering results), `layout.md › Visual hierarchy` (progressive disclosure: each list after its parent), `typography.md › Supporting Dynamic Type` (the name-cell fix), `accessibility.md › Mobility` (44 px controls from the shared filters), `color.md › Inclusive color` (status in words beside its dot), `loading.md › Best practices` (the list's shape while it loads), `writing.md › Best practices` (empty states). Left: the name and "Open record" both open the record (two stops per row), kept because the admin FUT (F-09) asked for the name to open it; the empty state has no button, because the Principal cannot register students; the school's words keep their capital mid-sentence ("by Wing, Course, Level and class"), as elsewhere.
- **Not done:** no export of the list; no sort choice (wing, course, level, class, roll number, then name).

**D-119 Students page, round 2: Department, filters always shown, the class as level and section, and the record's place in parts.** 2026-10-06, the PM after using D-118 on staging: "after wings also add Department filter"; "in the table show department in 1 column and section ... like 4th Semester-Evening"; the record "should have details like wings, degree, current level (4th Semester), Section"; "active student and in open term is the same thing, just remove in open term"; asked how to name it, "do what i said for department same to course" (Department is the Course), and "Always show" for the filters. **No permission, fee, result or audit change; nothing writes.**
- **Department is Royal Softech's word for Course:** `term.programme` = "Department" in `packs/royal-softech/pack.json`, so every screen that says {programme} says Department there (applied with `npm run provision`). The built-in word stays Course (D-114) for other schools.
- **Filters always on screen:** Wing, Department (every department, or the chosen wing's; a name shared by two wings carries its wing), Level and Class. Level and Class stay switched off with "Choose a Department first" / "Choose a Level first" until the one before is chosen, because a level such as "4th Semester" is a different level in each department.
- **Counts:** the "In open terms" card and its figure are gone (the API's `counts` is now `active` and `leftOrGraduated`).
- **Table:** a Department column (the wing under it) and a Class column, "4th Semester – Evening" (the level alone when the class has no section label).
- **Record:** `StudentDetail.place` (wing, course, level, section, term of the most recent enrollment; null in no class). The record lists Wing, Department, Level, Section and Term on their own lines; the line under the name reads "2083-90110 · Computer Science & Engineering, 1st Year – Section B". `className` stays for the screens that still use it (the student's own dashboard, the Fees search).
- **Tested.** API: `students-browse.test.ts` (18: the counts without the open-term figure, the record's place apart, no place in no class). Web: `students-screen.test.tsx` (8: all six filters from the start with two switched off, the school's word Department, every course before a wing and a shared name with its wing, a course without a wing, the Department and Class columns, the record's line) and the record test in `admissions-screens.test.tsx`. Browser, locally with the pack applied: the filters, columns and record as above; 320 px with text at 200%, nothing past the edge.

**D-120 Principal's screens: add a Co-ordinator or Accountant straight from the dashboard, no Recent activity, Academic structure starts closed.** 2026-10-06, the PM: "add coordinator and add accountant goes to people page. it should ideally go to the add a person 2nd screen where the 1st screen is already set"; "remove the recent activity"; "in academic structure everything should be in collapse status by default at the wings level"; and "fix the timing test". **No permission, fee, result or audit-log change; nothing new writes.**
- **Add from the dashboard:** the quick actions already link to `/portal/people?add=coordinator` (or `accountant`, D-089), which only the old Staff screen read. People & Access now reads it too and opens Add a person on its second step, Details, with that role chosen (`AddPersonDialog startRole`); Back still reaches Role. The address loses `?add=` when the pop-up closes, so a reload does not open it again.
- **Close fixed (the PM, same day):** a pop-up opened from the dashboard did not close: Close set the page's own choice to "none", which it already was, so nothing redrew and the address still asked for it. Close now marks the asked-for pop-up dismissed (`addOpen`, tested).
- **Recent activity removed** from the Principal's dashboard and from `GET /api/dashboard/overview` (its `activity` field and the audit query behind it). The audit trail (Reports, Activity) is unchanged; the two pieces it shares stay in `core/audit/recent.ts`.
- **Academic structure** starts with every wing closed; opening a wing shows its courses (Department at Royal Softech, D-119), opening a course its levels. Before, the first wing and its first course started open.
- **No status line:** the greeting no longer says "Everything important is on track", "A few things need your attention" or "Several important items need your attention" (the PM: "remove ... or any other words there"); today's date stays. The API still works out `status`; nothing shows it now.
- **Timing test:** `migration-0031.test.ts` compared D1's whole result, timings included (`meta.duration` 0 against 1 ms); it now compares the rows.
- **Tested.** API: dashboard tests (the overview has no `activity`). Web: the preset role opens on Details with no role choice (`people-access.test.tsx`), the dashboard has no Recent activity (`admin-dashboard.test.tsx`), the structure starts with nothing open and the content tests open it explicitly (`setup-screens.test.tsx`); 795 tests, typecheck, lint.
- **Not checked in the browser:** these are the Principal's screens, and a local Principal signs in with an authenticator code this session cannot give. The markup tests cover them; the PM checks them on staging.
- **Design review (`apple-design`):** `layout.md › Visual hierarchy` ("Take advantage of progressive disclosure": the structure opens a level at a time), `writing.md › Best practices` (a quick action does what it says: "Add Co-ordinator" opens that form, not a list). Nothing left.

**D-121 The Principal's menu: Attendance, Classwork, Fees and Results come off it; Admissions (Students) goes on; fees show on a student's record.** 2026-10-06, the PM: "remove attendance, classwork, fees and results from the side column as we can access these data via classes column" and "add fee under student record". **No permission change (the pages and the API are as before; only the menu and a screen change). Fees are read, never written, from the record.**
- **Whose menu:** the Principal's (`admin`) only. A teacher marks attendance and writes classwork from those entries, the Co-ordinator marks teachers' attendance and publishes results there, the Accountant works in Fees and a student reads their own; their menus are unchanged. Support keeps them. The pages still open by address, and the dashboard's links to them still work.
- **Admissions joins the Principal's menu** and opens on the Students page (D-118). It closes the old `OPEN:` in `nav.ts` (the Principal had no menu way to a student), and with Fees gone it is how the Principal reaches a student's fees. On a phone the Principal's tabs are Overview, Classes, Approvals, Admissions and More.
- **Fees on the record:** for the roles that may read fees (Principal, Accountant, Support; never the Co-ordinator, CLAUDE.md section 6), the record shows the fee account of the student's current term under the personal details: the figures, history and receipts (`AccountView`, read only), and "Open the fee account" for the full fee page, where the Accountant takes payments. A student in no class: "No fee account yet". It is a second request after the record's, so the personal details show first.
- **Tested.** Web: the Principal's menu and phone tabs (`nav.test.ts`), the fees section and its no-account line (`fees-screens.test.tsx`); 796 tests, typecheck, lint. The audit trail's admission test no longer reads the dashboard's removed activity (D-120).
- **Not checked in the browser:** the Principal signs in with an authenticator code this session cannot give; the PM checks it on staging.

**D-122 The Classes page grouped: course, then level, then its sections; the Co-ordinator's actions on it.** 2026-10-06, the PM's redesign brief and reference image ("Never put Wing + Course + Level + Semester + Section into one long text string"; course > semester > section; first course open; filters; "+ Add Section" per semester; a menu per section), approved in conversation ("yes let's fix these 3 things"). **No permission change: every action is an existing API call with its existing check (`setup.structure.manage`, `teaching.assign`); the class list gains ids only.**
- **Grouping:** one card per course (Department at Royal Softech), its wing once under its name, and three figures: sections, students, sections with no Class Teacher. Inside, each level once with its term and counts, and its sections as rows: a letter badge, the section name (a typed "Section B" or "Sec A" reads "Section B", never "Section Section B"), the Class Teacher or "No Class Teacher yet", the students, and a menu. The section name opens the class. Order is the server's (wing, course, level, section).
- **Open and closed:** the first course starts open, the others closed (the PM's reference; Academic structure stays all closed, D-120). While a search or filter is set, every matching course is open.
- **Filters:** a search (course, level, section or Class Teacher), then Wing, Course, Level and Section, each offering what is left under the choices above it, and Clear filters.
- **Actions, the Co-ordinator and Support only:** "Add Class" (to Setup, Classes, where the term and level are chosen), "Add Section" on each level (the same `POST /api/academics/classes`, the level and term filled in), and in each section's menu: Open class, Students, Rename section…, Set Class Teacher… (the class's own teacher list) and Switch off… (a class is switched off, never deleted; it can be switched on in Setup). The Principal and teachers get Open class and Students.
- **API:** the class list (`GET /api/classes`) also carries `termId`, `courseId`, `levelId` and `classTeacherId`.
- **Left out of the reference:** its sidebar (Students, Teachers, Subjects, Exams, Timetables), the "School 2025-26" picker and the bell: the brief says keep the existing navigation, and those screens do not exist. One Level filter, since the level is the semester.
- **Tested.** API: the class list carries the ids (`class-hub.test.ts`). Web: grouping, counts, filters and their narrowing, the typed prefix and the badge (`classes-grouped.test.tsx`, 8); the grouped markup, first course open, figures, Add Section only for the Co-ordinator, the row's link and labelled menu (`classes-screens.test.tsx`); 809 tests, typecheck, lint. Browser, locally as a Co-ordinator: the cards, filters, the menu's five actions, Set Class Teacher with the current teacher chosen, Add Section saved and shown; 320 px with text at 200%, after two fixes (the shared search box grew tall in a column; a section row squeezed its name to a letter a line, so on a narrow screen the name now has its own line).
- **Design:** `ui-ux-pro-max` searches ("accordion collapsible", "expand collapse disclosure") found nothing; its built-in rules applied (progressive disclosure, labelled controls, 44 px). Review (`apple-design`): `disclosure-controls.md › Best practices` (each disclosure says what it shows: "Show Computer Science"), `menus.md › Labels` (fixed: an item that asks for more before acting ends with an ellipsis, "Rename section…"), `layout.md › Visual hierarchy`, `color.md › Inclusive color` (a course's tint only groups; its name and figures carry the meaning), `typography.md › Supporting Dynamic Type` (the 200% fixes). Left: menu items keep the app's sentence case rather than title case, for consistency with every other menu here (`writing.md › Best practices`, "Build language patterns").

**D-123 The Academic Terms page: Currently Active, then Other Terms.** 2026-10-06, the PM's brief and reference image (the page answers "which terms are active right now?" and "what other terms exist?"; never "All Academic Terms"), and on statuses: "these states are all the term which are not active. all closed or not active". **No server, permission or data change: the same terms list and the same side panel actions.**
- **Header and banner:** "Define the academic periods that control which classes are currently active."; the banner says what an active term does, that a level is in one active term at a time, and the one-exam-pattern rule that the old subtitle carried (D-117).
- **Currently Active (n):** a card per active term only: wing(s), BS period with "5 months remaining", "Applies to" as Course · Level chips (three, then "+n"), classes and students, "Manage term" (the side panel) and a menu: Change levels…, Fill in the next term…, Close term….
- **Other Terms:** every term that is not active (drafts and closed), searched (name, code, levels) and filtered (All, Draft, Closed); columns Term, Period ("Starts in", "Not opened yet", "Ended 4 months ago"), Applies to, Classes, Students, Status, and Manage (View for a closed term) with a menu: a draft's Edit… and Open term…, a closed term's Fill in the next term…. No "Upcoming" (the PM: not active is not active). No Delete (no hard deletes, CLAUDE.md section 6), Archive or Activate early (closing is the archive; opening is the one way in).
- **Statuses read Active, Draft and Closed** (were Open, Not started, Closed), here and on the Co-ordinator's read-only term list in Setup. The four figures at the top are gone; the sections carry them.
- **The side panel opens straight on a step** from a menu (Edit, Next, Close fetch what they show); Open term opens the overview, where the Principal confirms.
- **Left out of the reference:** the extra sidebar entries, the school picker and the bell, as in D-122.
- **Tested.** Web: `terms-page.test.tsx` (11: wings, chips, timing words, Other Terms never active and its filters, the two sections' markup, the actions per status, the banner); the Setup list test for the new words; 819 tests, typecheck, lint. **Not checked in the browser:** the page is the Principal's, who signs in with an authenticator code this session cannot give.
- **Design review (`apple-design`, as D-122):** `layout.md › Visual hierarchy` (what is running first, the rest below), `menus.md › Labels` (an ellipsis on items that ask for more), `color.md › Inclusive color` (status in words beside its tone), `writing.md › Best practices` (time in plain words). `ui-ux-pro-max`: the same built-in rules as D-122.

**D-124 The Principal's Teaching page redesigned: teacher cards in four parts, and the subjects without a teacher.** 2026-10-06, the PM's brief and reference image, and on actions: "This should not change any feature of the teacher page. it is just a UI design. if something doesn't make sense in UI just not add it". **No server, permission or data change; read only, as before (D-104).**

Cards or the previous table, by a toggle.

## Open items carried forward

- ~~**Deploy D-108, D-110 and D-112 to staging.**~~ **Done 2026-10-05** (migrations 0027 to 0030, which staging had not had, and the Worker; restore point noted first).
- **Deploy D-114 to staging (PM).** Migration 0031 (rebuilds `subjects`, so note a D1 Time Travel bookmark first) and the Worker, as in `README.md`, "Updating an existing deployment". Then the Admin sets each level's length, and a whole-school Co-ordinator gives each "No Wing yet" subject its wing.
- **From the FUT of 2026-10-05, for a design session (D-114).** Point 3, grading; point 10, whether credit hours and mark components move to an exam pattern; point 12, electives and theory and practical, redesigned. Bring a real +2 Science or BBS mark sheet and the subject list with its elective choices. Point 16, admitting a student without a class and placing them later, is noted and not built.
- **Re-check BS 2084 against the official almanac (maintenance task, by about Falgun 2083; D-111, D-112).** 2084 is open provisionally on nepcalendar.com's calendar. If the official one differs, review dates already stored in 2084 and change the converter (PM approval, D-022). The same check recurs each year: BS 2085 before about Falgun 2084.
- **A credit at promotion (`OPEN:`, D-110).** A credit stays with the closed term's enrollment and cannot be refunded there.

- **Public pages carry the whole portal word list (D-102, D-106).** `apps/web/src/i18n/messages.ts` is about 108 KB and every page, the public ones included, loads all of it; it has twice pushed the sign-in page over its page-weight budget. Splitting it into a public catalog and a portal catalog (loaded only inside the portal) would take roughly half the weight off every public page. That changes the "words live in `messages.ts`" rule in CLAUDE.md, so it needs the PM's agreement first.

- **No CAPTCHA on the public apply form (D-063).** Relies on rate limiting and an off-screen honeypot, which meets section 7's "rate limiting, CAPTCHA or similar" but is not a CAPTCHA. Add one only if real abuse appears.
- **Admission documents stay a single certificate upload (D-020, D-063).** No file upload is built at all yet; R2 is still not enabled. The build-plan's "public multi-document application" is open until the PM says R2 is needed.
- **The privacy notice (D-067) has not been reviewed by the school or a lawyer.** Its facts are accurate to the code, but it is engineering's own draft. Confirm it before relying on it as the school's real privacy notice; Nepal's Privacy Act compliance itself needs a qualified legal review this session cannot give.
- **No data retention period is set, and no self-service way exists for a person to see or correct their own data (D-067).** Records are kept indefinitely; the only current path is asking the school directly (the privacy notice's contact section). Both are real gaps against common privacy-law principles, not yet asked for as a feature anywhere in the documents.
- **A daily database export to a second location is not built (D-067).** D1 Time Travel already gives 30 days of automatic point-in-time recovery at no cost; the additional export `build-plan.md` asks for needs a destination, most naturally R2, and so waits on the same PM decision as D-020.
- **No real error-tracking or uptime-monitoring service is wired up (D-067).** Unhandled errors are now logged (visible in Cloudflare's own dashboard) but nothing pages anyone; `GET /api/health` is ready for an external uptime monitor, but choosing a service, naming a contact, and any account/payment setup is the PM's step.

- ~~**Every public page makes two failed sign-in checks (found 2026-09-21).**~~ **Resolved 2026-09-21, D-045.**

- ~~**PM DECISION NEEDED BEFORE SLICE 3: how the fixed public pages get their words into the HTML.**~~ **Decided 2026-09-21: option A, D-046** (the PM's "do as you recommend"). The original write-up follows for the record. Raised 2026-09-21. D-039 told the PM the fixed pages (Home, Programmes, Admissions, Scholarships, Facilities, Contact) would be "fully static and crawlable". That is true only if the words are in the HTML. Rule 2 (one build serves every school) means a school's words come from its pack at runtime, so a plain static export serves an **empty shell** to any crawler that does not run JavaScript, and most AI crawlers do not. The goal in D-031 (visibility in AI search) would then fail for the whole public site, not only the five content types. Options:
  - **A. The Worker fills the shell in (recommended).** For public page addresses only, the Worker fetches the Next-built static page and, with `HTMLRewriter`, writes the school's title, description, headings, text and structured data (JSON-LD) into it from its configuration, then caches it. One build for every school stays true, there is no second design (it is the same markup), and crawlers get real text. Costs: a small extra path in the Worker and one Worker request per public page view (the free plan allows 100,000 a day; this school is far below that; static files still cost nothing). The five content types could later be filled the same way, closing the gap noted in D-039.
  - **B. Bake the school's pages in at deploy.** The web build reads the deploying school's pack and writes plain static HTML. Simplest to run and fully static, but the build becomes per school (this breaks rule 2 as written, and CI could no longer test "one build"), and every wording change needs a redeploy.
  - **C. Leave the pages client-rendered and do the AI-search work later.** Least work now; the public site would be invisible to most AI crawlers until it is redone. Not recommended.
  Slice 3 should not start until this is chosen, because it decides how the pages and the pack's content are shaped.

- **Packs do not reset removed overrides.** Applying a pack only adds and updates (D-026). If a pack drops a wording override or a module switch, the old value stays in the database; found when the sample school's "Vice Principal" survived re-applying Royal Softech. Sections must never be deleted, but wording and module switches are settings, not records, so making them match the pack exactly would be safe. Needs a decision, since it reverses "never deletes" for those two tables.

- **Secrets and settings on deployments.** Every deployment needs `AUDIT_HMAC_KEY`, `SESSION_SECRET` and `DATA_KEY` set with `wrangler secret put` before sign-in, the audit log, password reset or two-step sign-in is used, plus the settings `SITE_ORIGIN` (the site's address, for links in emails) and `EMAIL_ADAPTER`. Rotating `SESSION_SECRET` signs everyone out. Rotating `AUDIT_HMAC_KEY` breaks verification of the existing audit chain. Rotating `DATA_KEY` makes sealed values (queued emails, two-step secrets) unreadable. Keep the values with the operator, outside the repo.
- **Sign-in abuse protection.** Add a Cloudflare rate-limiting rule on `/api/auth/sign-in` and, if abuse appears, Turnstile.

- **Audit key and export.** Set `AUDIT_HMAC_KEY` as a secret on every deployment before the audit log is first used. Build the daily export of the chain summary to a second location (Phase 10, or earlier if cheap).

- **BS 2083 is verified** against Hamro Patro (13 dates, 2026-09-20), so nothing to ask the client (PM: they run on BS 2083). **Still to do:** verify BS 2084 when its calendar is published (by about Chaitra 2083) before extending the verified range. BS 2062 Baisakh to Jestha stays flagged as disputed.

- **R2 file storage (D-020).** On hold until needed. Blocks uploads in Phases 4 and 5.

- **Official BS calendar source.** Need one the client trusts, to check BS 2083 before go-live and to verify each later year. PM or client to supply.

Recommendation: model a minimal elective group. Needs PM approval because the docs say not to decide open points.
